Summary

Kara Danvers is the next big thing in basketball, destined to move into the WNBA and elevate the sport to another level. She's popular, exceptionally talented and everywhere Lena Luthor goes.
Lena hates her, the feeling's mutual.
...or is it?
That's the thing about strong emotions, they're not always quite what they appear to be and as it turns out, neither is Kara.

Or

A College basketball AU, where Lena can't help but fall for the charismatic Kara Danvers and honestly, no-one knows who fell first because they're in a secret relationship, with an abundance of unspoken feelings, causing a few episodes of confusion along the way.

out of bounds

by elena_mars

    Chapter 1: collision

    Chapter Summary

    Passion can consume.

    Chapter Notes

    Attending National City University had been part of Lena’s plan to escape her family and their name, if only for a few years. Lillian was displeased to put it mildly, but Lex had at least sounded supportive. Lena should have known better.

    Her brother ultimately found a way to taunt her, offering a more than generous donation, which he insists is all about investing in the future, Lena knows him well enough to be certain it’s more to do with a display of his power and influence over every path she chooses to take in life. It’s thanks to Lex’s idea of support, that Lena gets to spend every day walking past an arena with the Luthor name splashed all over it.

    It’s very Lex, it’s infuriating, it’s been tormenting her for over two years.

    The Luthor Dome - Home of the Wildcats.

    Jack teases her relentlessly, Jess is a little less harsh. Sam sends her a daily snapshot of herself on the way past and Andrea?…well that’s another story.

    Apart from her friends, most people don’t tie the two things together, not that she tells Lex. 

    The reality at NCU, is although the Luthor Dome is home to the Wildcats, everyone calls it ‘the den’. 

    No-one really cares who Lena Luthor is and they definitely don’t know that the NC Wildcats are everything Lena hates about college life. 

    A trip to the final four, to end last season, has left the team with a renewed popularity around campus and talk of this year, being their year, is relentless. Even Lena’s so-called friends can’t get enough of basketball, despite Lena doing all she can to ban the topic at every opportunity. 

    It’s all thanks to the team’s superstar player, potential number 1 draft pick and all-around hero on campus, Kara Danvers.

    Lena’s existed in the same space as Kara throughout her time here and whilst it’s not been the smoothest experience of her life, it had been only a minor inconvenience to share the same air, thanks to a conscious effort on Lena’s part, to avoid all the sports related aspects of college.

    Her plan was working well, but after last season’s almost moment, everything changed. Kara’s name is on everyone’s lips and her star appeal is no longer exclusive to campus. She’s a hot commodity and it’s meant that Kara, appears to be everywhere Lena goes lately and Lena means everywhere.

    Kara’s not just on the court, or turning up late to class, she’s also at every party Lena attends, always at Lena’s favourite bar and as annoying as it was, Lena was handling it. On campus, she could ignore the fanfare, drown out the drinking games and chants and keep her head down, whilst Kara high-fived half the students before she’d even sit down for class. It was manageable, until this summer, when Kara’s reach suddenly, and inexplicably, extended into Lena’s world. A place where she isn’t just a random student, but the daughter of Lionel and Lillian Luthor, sister of Lex. 

    —

    Lena’s fate is sealed, the night Lex insists she attends a basketball game between his beloved Metropolis and their arch rival’s Gotham. It’s not the first time she’s been dragged along to a game, her family expects these things of her and Lena’s always just kind of gone with it. It’s usually fun to spend time with Lex afterwards, but first she has a couple of hours to zone out of, because sweaty men darting across the court, isn’t quite Lena’s cup of tea.

    They’ve been seated for a total of two minutes when Lex spots her. “Isn’t that Kara Danvers?” He leans closer to Lena to get a better look.

    Lena’s heart freezes in her chest, partly because Lex knows who Kara is and also because it undoubtedly is Kara sitting directly opposite them at the Met Huskies game.

    “Who?” She goes for an air of nonchalance, which isn’t too hard, when she’s genuinely not interested in anything to do with Kara Danvers. She’s also not in the mood for another conversation, which will inevitably lead to questions about her life at NCU. Her eyes lock onto Kara, none the less. It’s no surprise to see her smiling easily, talking with the group that is suddenly crowding her.

    “You really need to brush up on your knowledge,” Lex chuckles. 

    She doesn’t need to, because she knows plenty about Kara already, thanks to Jack talking about her non-stop at the end of last semester. 

    Even if it wasn’t for Jack’s enthusiasm, everyone at NCU knows who Kara Danvers is. She was the standout freshman in her first year, but the hype died down once her progress was scuppered by some injury or another during their sophomore year. Lena remembers seeing her hobbling around campus with a leg brace, but that’s as much attention as she was prepared to give at the time, because Kara sucked up all the energy in the room, whenever she chose to show her face.

    Towards the end of last year, Kara got back to playing. Carrying an injury hit team through last March, into a surprise showing at the final four. They lost, but Kara’s performances only cemented her position as a hero amongst the entire student population and beyond. Kara’s reach has clearly spread off the back of the tournament, she has over two million followers on her social’s, is adored far beyond the confines of NCU and, most annoyingly, she’s sitting opposite them and everyone wants to know her.

    “Why?” Lena feigns indifference.

    “She’s the next big thing. Partner up with her and watch the money roll in.”

    He’s talking about an investment partnership, of course. Lena knows this, but for a beat, she considers another type of partnership and her eyes drift to Kara’s exposed midriff as she laughs with one of her many fans. It’s not Lena’s fault, Kara’s the one showing off her athletic body and Lena’s not had sex in—she shuts that thought down. None of it has anything to do with Kara, or the sliver of skin on display, because Lena is not going there. She has more important things to focus on. Like getting her degree, for starters.

    When she looks up, Kara’s looking right at her, her head tilted slightly and then there’s a flash of recognition. Lena looks away.

    “Ad revenue is only a small percentage of—”

    She’s cut off by Lex’s scoff, “it’s a new world, Lena, surely they’re teaching you all this at your mediocre college?”

    “A new world where we exploit athletes and influencers for short-term gains?”

    “Exactly,” Lex’s smile is devilish. “Always rely on you to catch on quick,” he pats her on the knee, his attention elsewhere as a woman in a suit crosses the court to speak to him.

    Lena looks back towards Kara, finds her leaning back in her seat, arm draped around the back of the seat beside her. The occupant is a woman, whom Lena vaguely recognises as another Wildcat player.

    The lights go down in preparation for tip-off. Lena doesn’t look over again.

    —

    The next two weeks are much the same. Kara’s appearances aren’t only linked to the sporting showpieces that Lex insists they attend, but also the gala’s, fundraisers and concerts that Lillian would never let Lena get out of.

    Lena goes out, Kara shows up. Lena avoids looking at her. 

    On the rare occasion where their eyes do meet, Lena simply gives Kara a cold hard stare, hoping it conveys just how much she dislikes her. It does the trick every time and makes Kara look away. It’s the most they’ve interacted since freshman week, when they had exchanged a few words in one of their business classes. Soon after, Kara had shown up in her Wildcat’s tracksuit and attracted enough attention from everyone around her, that Lena had known she was definitely someone to avoid. Lena’s dreams of a quiet life at NCU depended on it.

    —

    Lex mentions Kara a few times over the summer, alongside his burgeoning interest in women’s basketball. His sole motivation, being to tap in early and make as much money as possible from the sports growing popularity. Lena can’t fault him on his business acumen. Revenues improve if you get the right person fronting a campaign, Lex has proven this time and again and Lena’s not missed the recent series of LuthorCorp ads featuring up and coming sports stars. She’s very aware, Kara isn’t one of them. 

    Lex corners her, on the morning she’s about to head back to campus. 

    “Make it your business to get to know the Danvers girl,” he says. 

    Lena gives him a tight smile in response, “I’m not going to do anything of the sort.”

    “Why not? It’s business. We get in early and it could be huge. These players can make millions if they make the right choices, we can double anything they earn. I know you can see it too, Kara Danvers is a whole new world of opportunities. Make friends, it’ll give us an edge when the time comes.”

    Lena absolutely will not be doing what her brother wants her to do. She’s not telling him that though. She refuses to waste her energy on someone as infuriating as Kara Danvers, so she smiles in a way that makes him think he has her full support and it works, because Lex gives her a brief hug, before he waves her on her way.

    —

    The return to NCU is chaotic. Lena’s busy trying to get a head start, whilst doing all she can to tune out of Jack’s incessant talk about everything basketball, whilst he unpacks groceries with Sam, in the kitchen.

    “You can’t let us down, Lena,” Jack’s saying and that has Lena looking up from her books with a questioning raise of her eyebrow. Jack rolls his eyes, when he realises Lena’s not been listening to him, at all. “Make sure Danvers is at my party.”

    “No,” Lena flatly refuses, mostly because it’s a reminder of her other problem. The one which landed at her door this morning. Lena has a new tutoring assignment and an envelope, marked Kara Danvers, sits on her desk, taunting her every time she glances over to it.

    Jack squealed when he caught sight of the thing and he followed up with a stream of questions that had Lena’s head spinning more than it already was. His enthusiasm definitely isn’t enough to quell Lena’s inner panic, so she’s done what she does best and spent the rest of her day, doing everything possible to not think about it. 

    Tutoring has been a passion of Lena’s since she began at NCU. She’s helped several students muddle their way through and her success rate is something she’s proud of. She never once imagined she’d find Kara’s name sitting in her inbox. Rejecting the assignment is not an option. Kara would know she declined it and for some reason that feels a lot like losing to Lena, when her only reason, is their mutual hatred of one another. 

    There’s a slim chance Kara might request an alternative, even though Lena thinks it would be stupid on the athlete’s part to do so. She’s the best tutor available and from her brief glance through Kara’s notes, she really does need all the help she can get. It’s why Lena’s convinced herself she can do this. She can work with Kara, help improve her grades and move on, content in the knowledge that she still gets to ignore her, outside of this arrangement. 

    She will not, however, be asking Kara to any party. Even if it’s weeks from now. 

    “Does all this mean you’ll be making an appearance at your dome?” Sam throws something at Lena, she picks it up and sighs at the realisation it’s a Wildcat’s beanie.

    “It’s another, no.”

    “That’s the spirit, Luthor. Immerse yourself in the complete college experience and enjoy every drop of it,” Sam laughs. 

    Lena squirms, enjoying college life had been the goal, when she fought to come here. She’s definitely told Sam all about it, a few times whilst mildly intoxicated. 

    “Lena won’t have time for us now,” Jack swings his arm around Sam’s shoulders, the pair of them ganging up on her. “Not when she’s getting up close and personal with our star player.” 

    “Jack, could you not,” she whines. Wishing this would stop. She is not going to be getting close to anyone. Tutoring is simply a job, not that details matter to Jack. 

    “What’s more intimate than one-on-one study time?” Jack teases and he’s not done. “Friendships grow, bonds will be made…” Lena closes her eyes, a tension headache quickly forming.

    “Why are you tutoring her?” Sam asks, a smirk on her face as she pulls away from Jack and props her backside on the table opposite Lena.

    “I didn’t choose her, they assigned me,” Lena defends

    “Yes, because Lena Luthor needs the money from tutoring the basketball players,” Jack taunts her.

    “I put myself forward, to make a difference and give something back whilst I was here and—” she shakes her head, they both know all this already. “I did not choose this. It’s a job.”

    Lena throws the beanie back at him. He pulls it over his head with a smug grin, “just promise me you aren’t going to do anything to sabotage the Wildcat’s?” There’s a hint of worry in his tone, which is perhaps understandable considering how many times Lena’s complained about the entitled attitude of the team, but despite her dislike of Kara, she’s not got a secret vendetta that could derail anyone’s sporting achievement.

    “I promise, I will absolutely not be doing anything that could impact the game of basketball. I will help Kara catch up on her studies, that’s all,” she says finally and she means it.

    —

    It really does not need to be complicated, Lena knows this. She knows it, because there is one thing that has remained constant, since her first semester, Lena Luthor hates Kara Danvers and Kara hates her back, just as hard.

    Lena’s come to accept it, even though they’ve barely spent more than five minutes in the same room since the first week, but there have been plenty of glares, shoulder barges and general seething looks between them. Not least the day the name “Luthor” got plastered on the gymnasium. 

    Kara had walked into their shared class, stood in front of Lena for a whole minute and just stared at her. Lena can imagine that if Kara had the ability, there would have been laser beams shooting from her eyes. Kara only moved on, when her fan club arrived and gave her something else to do.

     It’s the day Lena knew, Kara knows exactly who she is, which is why, it’s almost funny when Kara pushes the door open and says, “Lena?” like she has no clue who she’s meeting up with.

    Kara is also ten minutes late. Lena’s been sitting in the Wildcat’s study room, thinking about how ridiculous it is that the players have been gifted a private study area, when half the team don’t seem all that bothered about the academic side of their time at university. Kara Danvers is firmly in that half, in Lena’s opinion. Being late only proves it.

    Lena slowly looks up, making sure to show her disinterest to the new arrival in the room. Her expression blank as she meets Kara’s tight smile, “you must be…” she pretends to read the name on the paper in front of her. “Kara?”

    “That’s me,” the blonde nods, stepping purposefully into the room like she owns it, bypassing the desk where Lena’s sitting and heading towards the fridge to grab two bottles. She offers one out to Lena, who looks at the blue liquid and declines. “Water?”

    “I’m fine,” Lena motions to the coffee cup in front of her.

    “Sure, so…” Kara drops down into the seat beside Lena, pulls at the pad in front of her and reads over the notes Lena’s written out. “How’s this all work?”

    Lena spends ten minutes telling Kara what she plans to do. Targeting the weak spots in her learning, which are too many to mention and assures her, that if she puts in the work. She will pass. “Can you do that?”

    “I can try,” Kara twirls the bottle in her palms, not looking all that bothered.

    “Do you want this?” Lena asks, she’s not in the business of wasting her time.

    The question seems to take Kara by surprise and she takes a second to consider her answer. “Yeah, I mean I came here for the program, but I want the degree too. I know it doesn’t look like it, but I have been trying to put the work in.”

    “Helps if you don’t skip class,” Lena says under her breath.

    “I…” Kara shifts in her seat, but doesn’t continue.

    When Lena looks up, Kara’s looking right at her, “you?”

    “You wouldn’t get it,” Kara looks away as she talks. “Priorities change. I worked hard to get back on that court, being able to play again was all I could think about. Guess all the focus, meant my studies maybe slipped, a little.”

    “A lot,” Lena says, closing up her laptop. “I’ll help you, Kara, but only if you’re prepared to put the work in? If not, I’m sure I could be of more use to a student who actually wants to learn,” she sets the challenge, it’s the last chance for them both to walk away and not go through this whole episode and it has to be Kara who decides. Lena takes a breath and waits as Kara mulls it over.

    Kara sits back in her chair, chews on the inside of her mouth as she studies Lena, thoughtfully, “sure, I’m committed to doing this.”

    It’s as simple as that? If Kara can commit, then Lena can too.

    “When you’re with me, we study. No ball, no socials,” she says, noting the way Kara’s constantly checking her phone. “I’ll get you up to speed, you pay attention and you will pass.”

    “Those your rules?” Kara hums at the challenge. 

    “Yes,” Lena says, firmly.

    “Deal,” Kara juts out her hand. Lena figures it easiest to go along with it and they shake on it. “Cool beans, Lena Luthor. We’re stuck with each other now.”

    Lena’s already regretting it.

    “Can I get your number?” Kara asks as Lena makes her way out. The question makes her pause. “For if I’m late or anything?”

    “Don’t be late,” Lena says, walking out the door.

    —

    It’s almost amicable for two weeks. They study, Kara puts the effort in and Lena makes sure to limit any small talk to the bickering that occurs, whenever Kara disagrees with her on something. Lena can tolerate it, even if it’s all too frequent. What she can’t tolerate is Kara being late and then walking in like it’s not even a problem. 

    Things quickly turn sour, when Kara starts goofing around with a mini hoop that’s been set up in the corner.

    “Can you focus?” Lena snaps.

    Kara huffs, throwing the mini ball through the basket and taking a seat, “having a minute of fun isn’t going to kill you, you know?” she says.

    Lena scowls at her, but it’s not having the desired effect because Kara simply laughs. They get thirty minutes of work done, before Kara’s phone rings and to Lena’s surprise, Kara answers it, walking to the other side of the room, so Lena can barely hear her.

    This was not part of the deal and she’s going to make sure Kara knows it, once she stops talking.

    The reason Kara’s grades have slipped, is obvious, she’s too many distractions in her life. It took Lena, all of five minutes, to work out that Kara’s clever and wouldn’t be struggling if she focussed. They’ve argued about it plenty, Lena telling Kara to put more effort in and Kara insisting she doesn’t have time to study.

    It’s a lie. Kara has time. Lena knows this because she’s witnessed her varied attempts to stall in all their sessions. Has seen the TikTok’s of Kara and her teammates dances, thanks to Jack keeping her constantly updated. She also knows Kara was out in the quad at midnight filming new reels and when she’s not doing any of that, she’s either sleeping or shooting hoops.

    “Think you can squeeze some studying into your abundance of free time?” Lena says when Kara ends the call, the blonde looks irritated by it, but Lena can’t care.

    “I study,” Kara glares back at her, the animosity that simmers between them resurfacing in a heartbeat.

    “Not enough.”

    “We can’t all be perfect academics. Some of us have a team to support and community projects to promote.”

    “And NIL sales to pump up your bank balance?”

    Kara’s cheeks flare a deep red, “it’s part of what I do. I didn’t think having money would be so offensive to a woman who’s going to take over a multimillion-dollar company.”

    “Billion,” Lena says under her breath and earns an eye roll from Kara in return. It pushes Lena over the edge. “I knew this was going to be a waste of my time,” she’s frustrated for even thinking Kara could be bothered. “Why don’t you get back to bouncing the big orange ball around and enjoy the few years you’ll get to play pro.”

    “Yeah, maybe I will,” Kara huffs. “I’ll leave all the highbrow stuff to you, yeah?”

    “Or anyone capable of using their brain.”

    “I use my brain,” Kara yells back. Lena’s perhaps hit on a sensitive point, but she’s not thinking enough to back down. “Playing basketball is mentally draining too.”

    “Well congratulations, you’ll be able to put that on your resume. Failed at business, but can successfully choose which direction to pass a ball,” she’s stuffing her laptop back in her bag and desperate to get away from Kara.

    “It’s easy for someone like you. You get to come to college, study, walk into a job that’s already waiting for you at your family company. You wouldn’t get what it’s like having to earn your way or the weight of expectation on me!”

    Lena does get it. Expectation has weighed heavy on her shoulders her whole life, not that she’s going to tell Kara.

    “You know nothing about me,” she snaps back. “Except that I’m forced to give up my time, in hope you’ll leave college with something to fall back on, if, god forbid, you bust your other leg or worse and your basketball dream goes up in smoke.”

    Rage flashes in Kara’s eyes, Lena knows she’s hit a sore spot. It’s why she said it. She knows all these sporty types are fragile when it comes to thinking about losing everything they think matters to them. Lena could scream at Kara about what loss really is, but she doesn’t.

    “If you hate tutoring me so much, call your bro,” Kara slams her book down on the desk.

    Arguing isn’t new. They’ve done it throughout these sessions, but it’s normally bickering and mostly Lena complaining that Kara’s distracted. This is not that. It’s raw and it stings and neither seems to know how to stop it.

    It’s not all Kara’s fault that Lena’s in this mood either, but the insinuation that she could make one phone call to stop all this, hits a nerve, partly because it’s true. She could have anything she wanted, if she asked and that’s why she doesn’t. It’s why she’s at this school. It’s why she’s worked tirelessly to maintain her grades. She wants to make a name for herself and be more than just a Luthor. Not that she’s telling Kara any of that though, all she’s doing is glaring at her.

    Kara’s jaw drops and the next thing Lena knows, she’s storming out of the room, which will definitely not do. If anyone is walking away, it’s Lena.

    She grabs her things and follows. Which is not her chasing after Kara, no. This is simply Lena’s way of making sure Kara will learn one thing today; you don’t turn your back on a Luthor.

Which is the other reason that Lena was not in the mood for Kara messing about, but that doesn’t really matter right now, all that matters is telling Kara what she thinks. 

    By the time Lena’s halfway across the parking lot, Kara’s striding into the gym, which has Lena quickening her pace. There is no way, Kara’s going to be shooting hoops when she should be studying.

    “It fucking smells in here,” she’s grumbles as she walks in behind Kara. She always hated the stench of the gym. Sweat, feet, Kara Danvers’ quite pleasant, unisex perfume.

    Kara spins around, throws the ball in the air and waits for it to drop. She smiles at the swish sound it makes. The smile fades when the ball thuds onto the court and then she looks at Lena. “You followed me, I never made you storm in this place like you’ve got an axe to swing.”

    “Axe to grind,” Lena corrects her, under her breath and earns a fierce glare in return.

    “Whatever, I didn’t ask you to follow.”

    “I’m not following you, I’m making sure you understand that you do not get to walk out on our allotted sessions. My time is important, I won’t have it wasted by you shooting hoops.”

    “Don’t like it when someone’s done with you?” Kara shifts on her feet, looking like she’s itching to get hold of the ball and keep practicing.

    “I don’t like it when my time isn’t valued. We made a deal, I haven’t asked for much, but I promised I’d help you, the least you could do is show up on time,” Kara’s crossing the court now and Lena is quick to follow.

    “I told you, I got stuck with a rep—” Kara stops walking and spins so fast on the floor that her sneakers squeak. It makes Lena look down and when Kara copies, she throws her hands up in the air. “You can’t wear those shoes on the court.”

    “I can, it’s got my name on it,” she snaps back.

    “Oh, you love that don’t you?” Kara’s irate. “You hate me for the privilege of being successful here, yet you walk about and embrace your own privilege like it’s some divine right. No-one better upset Lena Luthor, queen of campus, super brain, know-it-all, because her family might come down here and pull all their funding.”

    Lena’s shocked to hear what Kara really thinks of her and it stings a lot more than it should, so she yells, “fuck you!”

    “Might make you lighten up some,” Kara bites back. 

    It makes Lena’s nostrils flare and ignites a fire in her belly, she charges forwards, her finger pointing hard into Kara’s chest. Kara looks down at it, then back up at Lena. A mixture of shock and something else Lena can’t decipher. Her chest heaves with how angry she is. “You’re out of line,” she says as calmly as she can muster, which isn’t very calm at all.

    Kara seems to think on what she’s said and then swallows thickly, “yeah, sorry, it was—” 

    She makes a humph sound when Lena’s pushes her again. Not anticipating the second impact, Kara stumbles backwards. Lena sees the alarm flash across her face, before her arms come up to try and catch her, but it’s too late to stop them falling. Kara’s arm wraps around Lena, bracing her from the fall, as they crash into the hardwood. 

    She’s breathless, frustrated. Kara’s beneath her, all warm and strangely soft for someone who looks like she’s made of firm, toned muscle. Lena looks at her and is about to spit out something equally venomous, but gets distracted when Kara’s tongue darts out to wet her bottom lip, there’s a familiar ache forming between her thighs, even before she catches sight of Kara’s lustful eyes.

    This is Kara Danvers, they hate each other. It’s a very bad idea, but she can read it on Kara’s face, knows she feels it too. This heat between them, an unstoppable force pulling her closer.

    She swallows, feebly attempts to push herself back, but Kara’s hand quickly wraps around her arm, stopping her. A silent dare written all over her face and it breaks all Lena’s resolve.

    In the next breath, she’s kissing Kara. Firm, sure, like she’s been thinking about how it might feel to do exactly this, not that she’s going to admit it. Once she’s kissing Kara, she can’t stop. Kara’s not fighting her off, either.

    In fact, Kara’s eager, her hands gripping firmly at Lena’s sides, pulling their bodies flush, sliding her lips to one side, her tongue light against Lena’s lip and she readily deepens the kiss.

    Lena so lost in it all. The feel of Kara’s hands on her body, the way she makes this soft sound when their tongues collide, the way her thigh slots between Lena’s legs, where all the blood has rushed to her clit. It’d be so easy to rock into Kara, to let go. She’s almost at the point of no return, when she pulls back, breathless. 

    “Wow,” Kara says, her cocky grin nowhere in sight as Lena comes to her senses and pushes herself off Kara’s body.

    She gets to her feet, picks up her bag and strides out of the gym, leaving Kara, on her back, on the floor, breathless.

    She doesn’t look back, she doesn’t need to. She can see Kara in the reflection of the glass doors. She’s propped up on her elbows, her gaze following Lena as she marches out of the door.

    At least Lena made her point clear. If anyone is walking out, it’s going to be her.

    Chapter 2: denial

    Chapter Summary

    Lena's not doing a very good job of moving past her moment of madness.
    Kara's a little smug.

    Chapter Notes

    With Kara out of state, playing basketball, Lena has plenty of time to think over her mistake and it really is a mistake, nothing more. A huge, wishes she could time travel and undo it, kind of mistake.

    There’s no easy way to move past it. She’s an idiot, because having spent the better part of two years, avoiding anything to do with Kara Danvers, she has completely fucked up by kissing the woman she absolutely detests.

    Lena has many reasons for hating Kara, so many, really good ones. She can’t quite recall any of them at the moment though, because the stupid kiss keeps playing on a loop in her mind.

    The sounds Kara made, her eagerness, the way her lips were even softer than they looked.

    If there was one thing Lena shouldn’t do at college, this has to be it.

    She counts herself lucky that Kara is away, it gives her time to work out how to handle the situation. Which sounded a lot easier in her head, but as the days pass, the only solution that she can think of, is to act like it never happened. She can’t turn back time, but she can live in denial.

    This plan has a few flaws, but she figures, if Kara comes chasing after her? She’s more than adept to shut it down. 

    If she is to continue this tutoring arrangement, then this is how it has to be. One thing she knows for certain, she can never, ever get close enough to Kara for it to happen again, because there will definitely not be another hate-fuelled repeat performance.

    She’s adamant she can do this. But in the meantime she is also going to have to forget how good Kara smelt up close, how strong her hands felt as they gripped her tightly, how fucking dirty it felt to have her tongue in her mouth. All those thoughts can easily be squashed down. Lena’s a Luthor, which means she’s got a whole heap of skills when it comes to repressing feelings and she needs them now, more than ever.

    It might be easier, if she was capable of avoiding anything to do with Kara, until their next meet-up, but luck is not on her side, thanks to Jack’s love of everything Wildcats. He’s currently on the couch, his feet up and a beer in hand, as the sound of sneakers squeaking across the court, fills the room. Lena’s almost used to it as the background track of her life these days. Today, she could do without the distraction.

    “How is that not a foul?” Jack yells at the screen, making Lena jump and causing her coffee to slosh over the rim of her mug. She’s wiping it up when she glances towards the game, where Kara’s being helped up off the floor by her teammates.

    Lena’s mind flashes back to the last time she’d seen Kara lying on her back and she inwardly groans. She really needs to get a grip. It’s not helping that Kara’s wearing her jersey, her toned arms on display and looking almost as angry as she did during their argument. The game moves on at a pace, Kara sprinting to the other end of the court, picking up the rebound and then quickly passing to Lucy Lane, who darts back to the post and lays it up. Jack pumps his fist into the air. Lena looks away.

    She is not going to be watching basketball.

    —

    The Wildcats win in overtime. It’s significant for a reason that evades Lena, but Jack talks nonstop about it, as he cooks eggs for breakfast the following morning. He takes great delight in telling her, how Kara was pulling all the strings, that Leslie had her best performance and a stunning display from Lucy had her hitting every 3. Lena doesn’t care for any of it, but registers that a late finish, means Kara is unlikely to be on time, later that day. 

    She’s therefore surprised, when the basketball star, walks into the study suite, one minute early. 

    Kara dumps her backpack on the table, stretches her arms above her head, nodding towards Lena as she says, “Luthor.” Her tone isn’t overly hostile, not that Lena’s reading into it, nope.

    “Danvers,” Lena offers her usual curt smile, notes how Kara searches her face briefly, lingering at her lips and then takes a seat.

    “Miss me?”

    Lena stifles the urge to scoff, she’s prepared for this. Made sure she’s ready to deal with whatever smugness Kara brings to the table. 

    “How about we focus on the case study, you were meant to work through,” she says and Kara gets her work out, ready to begin. 

    “I got a bit of time, on the flight back,” she says, clearly pleased with herself. It’s unsettling when it turns out they’re going to have a productive study session, with Kara less argumentative and willingly following Lena’s instructions, for the first time ever.

    Lena had expected Kara to come in and be all cocky. Thinking she’d got the intel on Lena and her secret desires. She’d even, albeit briefly, entertained the idea that Kara, maybe, was as blown away by that kiss, as she’d looked at the time. Lena had at the least expected her to push it and tease, not this.

    Silent. Focused. Study.

    It irks Lena more than it should, but she can’t say anything, that would be akin to admitting defeat. If Kara’s happy to play the amnesia game, then Lena can too. They wrap things up, Lena puts her things away, gets up and walks out. She doesn’t look back.

    —

    The next time they meet, it’s a similar pattern, except this time Kara asks for her number again. Lena’s face remains impassive, long enough for Kara to quickly explain herself.

    “It’s not that I want to be late, just sometimes my life gets in the way and I know it might surprise you, but I do know it’s rude to keep someone waiting.”

    “Fine,” Lena gives in, perhaps a little too easily. It’s not something to argue about either. Things would be easier if Lena wasn’t left sitting in an empty room, waiting for the door to open. Kara hands over her unlocked phone and Lena punches in her number.

    Kara takes it back, taps in a few things and then Lena’s phone lights up.

    Ur fav no 5 🏀

    Lena reads it in confusion. 

    “It’s my number, Lena,” Kara laughs. “You really don’t watch?”

    “No,” Lena lies. She’s not admitting she knows anything about basketball or Kara. “Not if I can help it.” She saves Kara’s number as KD-TA

    “That’s what you’re calling me?” Kara glances over.

    “Kara Danvers, tutoring assignment,” Lena shrugs. It’s all Kara is to her, after all.

    Kara’s eyes narrow, as if she’s trying to work Lena out, but can’t. “You want to know what I saved you as?”

    “No,” Lena says, even if she kind of does.

    Kara’s resulting laugh is rich and warm, it makes Lena’s chest bloom, which definitely will not do.

    “How come you were at the Met’s game?” Kara asks, as she slips her phone back in her pocket.

    “Obligation,” Lena answers tightly. She has no desire to talk to Kara about her family or the duties expected of her. “I don’t like sport.”

    “Wow, that’s quite a broad sweeping statement.”

    “My brother’s a fan, he invited me,” Lena confesses and is fully aware how haughty she sounds.

    “Guess that explains why you spent the whole time either talking, or on your phone and definitely looking anywhere, but the court,” Kara grumbles. 

    Lena’s eyes snap to her then. She wonders just how much of the game Kara was looking at her? By the signs of a blush rising up her neck, she’s realised what she’s just said and it was possibly a lot.

    It makes Lena feel a strange tingle at the corner of her mouth. She coughs, to hide the smile.

    “There was nothing of interest to me on the court,” Lena says, which is also true. She’s not going to admit that she was on her phone most of the time because she was doing all she could, to not look over at Kara.

    “Come watch me play some time, you might enjoy it,” Kara says and Lena looks at her like it’s the most unlikely thing that will ever happen. Kara simply laughs, rich, warm and it’s increasingly hard to bite back her own smile. “Then I’ll see you next time, Luthor. Try not to have too much fun doing whatever it is you do.”

    Lena doesn’t answer, the implication that she doesn’t know how to have fun, annoys her enough to make her dismiss any warm feelings, she’d just been having about Kara’s laugh. She packs up her things and goes to find Jack. Drinking with him is always a good way to forget about anything that gets stuck in her mind. Usually it’s family drama, today it’s definitely the smile on Kara’s face.

    She’s mildly intoxicated, when she crawls into bed at 1am. Which is why, she stupidly unlocks her phone, catches sight of the latest Wildcats post and ends up watching Kara’s buzzer beater from the overtime game, three times, before she passes out.

    —

    A few days later and Lena’s on her way to meet Kara, when she gets a text asking her if they can switch from the usual study suite, to the den. She walks towards the giant dome, trying not to scowl at seeing her own surname on every sign on the way. Despite avoiding all things sports (and Luthor) related, she’s been in the arena before. Once for a welcome day and then a few careers fairs, but this is the first time she’s stepped foot inside, when there is the distinct sound of basketball being played. 

    Not much else is happening at 3pm in the afternoon, aside from the players moving rapidly around the court, so she takes a seat beside the nearest exit, not wanting to be noticed as the practice session continues. 

    Kara’s messing about with her teammates, in-between taking turns to shoot hoops, whilst their coach is on the side-lines, arms folded, watching every move they make. She doesn’t look all that happy. Although, Lena’s not sure if Cat Grant ever does. They run a few drills or screens or whatever it is they do and Lena sits back and takes note of how Kara is the clear leader of the group. Calling plays and being shouted at by Cat for a reason that evades Lena.

    Lena’s half-forgotten the reason she’s in the building, when Kara spots her in the crowd and offers a discreet wave, before she jogs over to Cat. The pair exchange a few words and then the short, blonde woman, squints as she searches Lena out in the bleachers. Cat shakes her head, looking mildly displeased, but Kara’s already jogging off the court.

    She appears only a minute later, having pulled on a training hoodie, with a huge Wildcat logo on the chest. There’s a towel loosely hanging around her neck, which she uses to wipe the sweat from her brow, “sorry about changing our plans, coach is really pushing us, she’s kind of mad at me for saying I had to go.”

    Lena’s heard about the renewed drive for success, after the trip to the last four last season, but is against it getting in the way of Kara’s education.

    “Her lust to win, is not more important than any player’s future.”

    “She knows,” Kara dismisses Lena’s concern, like it’s nothing, motioning for her to follow down the exit tunnel. 

    Lena’s not really sure what to make of it, “does she? I know she’s admired for running such a successful program, but it can’t come at all costs.”

    “Honestly, Lena it’s cool. Coach gets on at us all about our studies too. Why do you think you’re even here?”

    “Because the program—”

    “Because I needed some help and Coach put in for me to have the best available,” she gestures at Lena, like it’s obvious. “Coach wants something, she gets it. I need to catch up on things, make sure there’s less outside pressure on my game. You know what I’m saying?”

    “I…” Lena’s surprised, is all. Her being here isn’t as random as she’d thought, not that it changes anything.

    Kara sniffs at the inside of her shirt and gives Lena this pitiful look, “you mind if I get showered? I’ll be like five minutes, max.” 

    “Feel free,” Lena waves her off towards the locker room. Not really all too inclined to spend the next hour with the lingering smell of Kara’s sweat.

    “Help yourself, it’s all complimentary for us,” Kara gestures to the small kitchen at the back of the athletes’ common room. 

    Lena makes herself a drink, whilst Kara disappears behind one of the other doors. She returns, freshly washed, her hair still wet but in a fresh set of clothes.

    “Better?” Lena asks, not looking too closely. 

    “Much, we did sprints earlier. I was a hot mess,” Kara picks up Lena’s bag for her and motions for her to follow. “It’ll be quieter back here,” she says, pushing open a door to a meeting room, to the side of the court. Whilst Lena takes a seat, Kara roots around in the cool box. “You want one or sticking to coffee?” She asks, holding up a bottle of her usual blue liquid.

    “I’m good,” Lena shakes her head and they get to work.

    —

    “I don’t see why we have to go over this again,” Kara complains, less than thirty minutes in.

    “Did you even look over the notes?” Lena sighs. Kara wouldn’t be stuck, if she’d paid attention last time. “If you put some effort into study—”

    “I told you, I do study,” Kara says, something dark twisting in her eyes. “Not like you, but I do.”

    Lena can’t hold her gaze, it only makes her think of the last time she got angry and what it led to. Kara’s still not said anything and at first Lena was glad, but this has dragged on too long and she can’t understand why.

    Kara’s tapping her pen against her pad and it’s only making Lena even more irritated.

    “Are you really not going to say anything about the kiss?” She snaps, frustration at Kara’s silence getting the better of her.

    Kara lazily raises one shoulder in a shrug, “got the feeling you wanted to forget about it.”

    Lena stares at her, but Kara remains focussed on her notes.

    “It was a mistake,” Lena says, Kara’s pen stops moving, but she doesn’t say anything. It has Lena stewing in the silence and it’s making her doubt her own recollection of what happened. She’s struck by the thought that maybe she read it all wrong. “I’m sorry for what I did. It’s no excuse, but I was angry and lost my head. You can be so frustrating, but I shouldn’t have. I’m sorry,” she says again, wishing she could just shut her mouth.

    “I was kind of it into it,” Kara’s lips curl into a smile, it’s all together sweet and cocky and it makes Lena scoff, even if she is also mildly relieved to hear it.

    “Of course, you were.”

    “What’s that supposed to mean?’ Kara looks up then, her eyes intense as she watches Lena’s reaction.

    She’s hot, bothered and not letting Kara work her up into another raging mess, because it’s not going to lead anywhere good. A mistake. A one time, kind of mistake and she has to keep it that way.

    “You’re very annoying.”

    “Says the woman who couldn’t stop herself from kissing me,” there’s a challenge in Kara’s tone and Lena is not going there.

    “I know you think you’re irresistible, but please, get over yourself.“

    “Can’t help that all the pretty girls want to kiss me,” Kara teases, but something about the way she implies, Lena is pretty, has a different kind of heat pooling in Lena’s belly.

    “Insufferable,” she starts to pack up her bag. “I tried to apologise and this—”

    Kara stops her, placing a hand over Lena’s before she can put her book away, the touch is gentle, warm, “you don’t have to apologise and please don’t worry about it. Not a big deal, you’re not the first.”

    This bothers Lena more than she shows. “The first what?”

    “To get hooked up on this,” Kara tilts her head, a light smirk forming at her lips and Lena’s not having it, she snaps her hand away.

    “I am not hooked up on this,” Lena frowns as she takes the time to study Kara. She’s wearing a t-shirt with Scooby-doo on it, her hair is still damp from the shower, she smells nice, but that’s not the point. She’s got old sweatpants on and her socks don’t match, she’s also wearing sliders and it’s hurting Lena’s eyes to look at. “I only wanted to clear the air, in case you got the wrong idea,” she knows it sounds snooty, she feels the heat in Kara’s eyes as she sits back and gives Lena a similar once over.

    This is not the way Lena saw this conversation going. “No, I…”

    “Look, it was nothing.” Kara says, flatly. Like she really wants to move on. “Unless you wanted it to be?” She waggles her eyebrows, it’s a joke. Lena knows it.

    She scoffs, rolls her eyes and sits back down. She can get past this.

    —

    Kara’s reading over the next case study and Lena’s supposed to be preparing a few questions, but her mind betrays her and she slips back to thinking over what Kara said, she taps her pen on the pad, Kara looks up in question. 

    “Messy?” Lena asks, because that is not how she remembers it.

    “Teeth,” Kara points to her mouth. “bit sloppy and wet,” another shrug.

What she does remember is Kara’s face as she walked out the door. She should let it go, this is exactly how she wants things between them to be. 

    If she keeps her mouth shut, she can dismiss the whole thing as a moment of madness. One she could blame on Kara, but not with this kind of a reaction.

    “I’ve never had any complaints before,” Lena says, her anger boiling up all over again. Maybe this is Kara’s game? She has a feeling she’s walking into a trap, but can’t stop herself.

    “Okay, sure. Maybe it was just the positioning,” Kara says, a wry smile on her face.

    Lena’s almost tempted to grab hold of her and show her how it was definitely a good kiss. 

    “If I recall, you were very into it.”

    “Like I said, pretty girls,” Kara shrugs and oh, Lena’s in trouble if she doesn’t stop this now. “If you want to practice, I can give some tips.”

    Lena takes a breath, tugs her bag over her shoulder and walks out, without saying goodbye.

    —

    She’s too busy to think about Kara, or the ridiculous assertion that their one-time kiss was not good, for the rest of the week. 

    It’s all working out well for her. She’s coping sort of. It’s all fine, until Kara shows up late for their next session, only to walk in, like she’s not a care in the world.

    “I’m a good kisser,” Lena says before Kara’s closed the door.

    “Um, kay,” Kara gives her a curious look as she moves around the desk and takes her seat. “We’re going back to this?”

    “It was just the situation. I was angry and the positioning and you were all grabby and trying to stick your tongue down my throat.”

    “So, I’m a bad kisser?” Kara sounds so incredulous that Lena wants to whack her.

    “No,” Lena says too quickly and the delighted look on Kara’s face is infuriating. “Would you listen to what I’m saying for once?”

    “I am,” Kara chuckles. “You liked kissing me, can’t stop thinking about it, but you think maybe it was bad and you want to do it again, to see if it’s better?”

    Lena’s jaw drops, but she also feels a heat pooling low. “How about we focus on the paper you should have finished yesterday.”

    Kara looks down, guiltily. Which gives Lena all the answer she needs about how much work Kara’s put in this week. “Sure, then later we can talk about how to improve your kissing game. I’m not averse to practice. It’s part of an athlete’s life and—”

    “We’re not kissing again,” Lena shoves Kara in the ribs.

    “Whatever you say, boss,” Kara bumps her shoulder against Lena’s.

    They don’t talk about the kiss again. Lena still thinks about it.

    Chapter 3: connections

    Chapter Summary

    are they flirting?
    Lena couldn't say.

    College life goes on. Lena attends class, Kara mostly doesn’t. She’s playing a game or practicing or building her legacy, as Jack likes to say. Lena’s gotten used to finding him chuckling away to himself as he watches reels of Kara doing something that has nothing to do with the game of basketball, with any one of her team mates and it makes Lena wonder, just what Kara’s legacy is going to be at times. 

    Although, maybe all the skipping classes and messing about works, because the team is winning and Kara’s on a hot streak and everyone is talking about it. Local news, her professors, her so-called friends. Lena can’t escape hearing about Kara Danvers, even when she wants a minute to herself at her favourite coffee shop.

    Which is where she has the misfortune of unexpectedly witnessing Kara’s college-based fame on a Tuesday morning. 

    The second Kara walks in, the whole atmosphere changes. It’s the reason Lena’s been avoiding her from the start; Kara attracts attention. Good and bad. 

    Currently, the guys next to her are not so subtly making comments about the things they’d like to do to Kara and at one particularly lewd comment, Lena swivels in her seat, shooting them a look, which makes them both shut up.

    “They causing you trouble?” Kara appears with a steaming coffee in her hand. Lena hadn’t even seen her place her order, but perhaps that’s a perk of being well-known? Her choice of drinks made, without her asking or even paying.

    “Nothing I can’t handle,” Lena’s scowl slowly fades as she’s met with Kara’s bright blue eyes. There’s another question in them, one Lena can’t decipher, before she shifts her focus to the two guys with an angry glare. 

    “Think you both want to find somewhere else to sit,” Kara says, her tone commanding as she squares her shoulders.

    “Just leaving,” one of them mumbles and the screech of chairs scraping along the hardwood floor signifies their hasty departure. 

    Kara watches them until the door closes and then her eyes are back on Lena, “they talking trash?”

    “I told you I could handle it, you didn’t have to rescue me,” Lena huffs, irritated by the idea of it.

    “Nope, you can definitely handle yourself,” Kara gives her an appraising once over, a smirk forming as she does so. 

    “They weren’t even talking about me,” Lena says and Kara’s smirk fades.

    “Oh,” the comment registers and she looks genuinely chastised, her free hand rubbing at the back of her neck. “Right, sorry, guessing I didn’t help by coming over? I’ll, uh…” she’s looking around for a spare seat, but the place is packed and the space vacated by the two idiots has already been taken. Lena’s no idea what she’s thinking when she rolls her eyes, pats the space next to her and waits for Kara to sit.

    “Is it weird?” Lena asks, noting the way the other customers keep glancing over. “Everyone knowing who you are?”

    “At times,” Kara lifts her coffee to her mouth, takes a sip. “When I want a minute to myself and people stare, or when they hate on me, can get a bit tiring.”

    Lena nods, life in Metropolis, with her family, isn’t too dissimilar when she thinks about it. “I know it’s not the same, but whenever I have to perform for my mother, I know I’m always being watched, judged,” she says. Kara’s eyes soften, her head tilting to the side as she studies Lena.

    “Ever want to run away from it all?”

    “Every day,” Lena admits. “It’s why I came to NCU. Anonymity and a fresh start.”

    “Anonymity?”

    “Nobody really knows me here, I like it that way. Not being seen.”

    “You are definitely seen,” Kara’s reply is quick, her cheeks pink, but she tries to hide it by taking another sip of her drink.

    “And you, why NCU?” Lena quickly brushes past it.

    “The program. A dream. I always wanted to play here, be one of the greats. That kind of thing. Not exactly gone to plan, but I’ve got good people around me, a good coach and I’m fit and healthy again. Been a lot of work.”

    “But you really want that National Championship?”

    “I really do,” Kara says, with a gentle chuckle, it’s obvious it means a lot to her and Lena can’t help but smile softly.

    “I hope you get it.”

    Kara looks surprised as she says, “thank you.”

    Lena’s about to make a comment about how she’s not a monster and does actually want people to achieve their dreams, but before she does, her phone vibrates on the table top.

    “I’m meant to be picking up the wine for my friends,” she says, rejecting the call from Sam and getting to her feet. “I’ll see you, Thursday?”

    “Yes boss,” Kara says with a smile.

    Lena narrows her eyes, there’s something in the way Kara is looking at her that has her asking, “is that what you’ve saved me as, in your phone?”

    “Oh, now you want to know?” Kara teases, Lena raises one eyebrow and notes the way Kara knows she’s not messing. “LLBL? Lena Luthor, Boss Lady, yeah I like it,” Kara grins.

    Lena walks out the door with a shake of her head, she’s still smiling as she walks into the liquor store. 

    —

    There are two whole minutes where Lena thinks she’s going to have a fun evening with her friends, but when Jack places his phone in front of her with an accusatory, “you two pals now?” her stomach sinks.

    Lena looks at the photo and groans. 

    “It was coffee, she needed somewhere to sit.”

    She studies the photo, it’s strange to see a snapshot of her and Kara. They’re not friends, never spent time together outside of tutoring, but Lena can see how soft she looks. Kara smiling up at her is one thing, but the way Lena’s smiling back? It’s the reason Jack’s eyebrows are currently in his hairline.

    “Yes, darling, a quiet coffee date, the caption says it all.”

    “Believe it or not, we are civil to each other, it helps,” Lena swipes out of the app and gives him back his phone. 

    “Just be careful,” Jack teases, but Lena stares back at him blankly. “Kara’s fans are kind of intense. You might want to avoid the comments,” he explains.

    Lena closes her eyes, tight, “they can think what they want to think.”

    “Oh, they will,” Jack says and when she opens her eyes, he’s smirking back at her. She grabs for one of the bottles of wine, she’d picked up earlier and pours herself a large glass.

    —

    “Your girl is killing it,” Jack says the following night, dropping a bag of food on the counter.

    Lena inspects the cartons, relieved he remembered everything for a change, especially for a post game meal, where he often gets distracted and ends up at the bar. She knows who he means, but isn’t going to indulge this new narrative, “and who are we talking about?”

    “KD, your coffee buddy, your gal pal.”

    “My tutoring assignment,” Lena pushes him out of the way as he tries to grab at the pot-stickers.

    “Whatever you like to call her, can’t deny it, the girl is on fire. Scored 30 points tonight. We’re so going in as top seeds this year.”

    Lena questions what the rest of the team do in these games, where apparently Kara’s destroying the opposition, “they’re making space for her to do it,” is Jack’s easy reply. 

    Lena sighs, there’s more to it, but she does know Kara’s breaking records. The Wildcats feed is all about how she’s surpassing previous NCU legends, but despite it catching Lena’s attention, she still won’t watch an actual game. She already knows Kara’s special on the court, she doesn’t have to see it. If she did, she might do something stupid, like get invested, which really would not do.

    —

    Despite the freak encounter at the coffee shop, Lena’s determined to stick to her plan. Tutor Kara, forget she kissed her, get on with the rest of college. 

    It’s working, mostly. If she doesn’t count the fact that she still thinks about the kiss every time she thinks of Kara, which is what she’s doing on her way to their latest meet-up at the Wildcats training centre.

    Even though it has a modern exterior and custom built displays, the centre is still a clubhouse to its core. Full of old jerseys, basketballs and trophies, it’s a gigantic shrine to everything Lena tries to avoid about college and not somewhere she’d ever imagine she’d be spending so much time. Still, it’s better than the Luthor Dome, where she’s reminded of Lex and his need to leave a mark wherever a Luthor goes.

    Lena’s gotten used to seeing Lucy Lane with a different groupie every time she comes to the clubhouse and today, she has a finger hooked in the belt loop of a blonde girl’s jeans. Lena tries not to look too closely, mostly because it’s not the same girl, Lucy was with only two days ago. She almost makes it past unnoticed, but then Lucy throws her head back laughing and catches sight of her.

    “Hey, Luthor, your girl’s upstairs.”

    Lena doesn’t acknowledge Lucy or her comment, but heads up the stairs to find Kara crashed out on the old couch.

    “Make sure you tell Lucy Lane, I’m not your girl,” Lena says, kicking Kara’s foot as she walks past.

    “What?” Kara jolts upright, clearly more out of it, than Lena realised. “Lena? Hey, when did you, uh…”

    “Late night?” She purses her lips.

    “Yeah, big win,” Kara stretches. “And I had early morning pilates.”

    “Pilates?” Lena’s surprised.

    “Helps to keep my body flexible, you know, after the injuries,” Kara shrugs, but she’s looking anywhere but at Lena now.

    “Oh,” Lena makes the mistake of thinking just how flexible Kara needs to be to shoot hoops and it has her looking over Kara’s body in question. “Right.”

    Kara springs to her feet, unaware of the fact Lena had just been ogling her, “let me tidy up.” She looks at the mess around her and sighs, grabbing as much in her hands as she can, to make space for Lena and her books, which Lena thinks is pretty futile seeing as the place is a pit, but appreciates the effort, none the less. “I didn’t mean to fall asleep.”

    “How about we get this over with and then you can find an actual bed to sleep in?”

    “I bet you’ve a nice bed,” Kara says, still half asleep and then seems to catch herself, her eyes go wide before she turns her back to pick up her own textbooks. 

    Lena chooses to ignore the comment and Kara’s reaction.

    Lucy walks in then, gives Lena a not so subtle once over, pointing her finger between the two of them, “this thing getting serious?” 

    “Shut up,” Kara throws a balled up towel at her teammate. “And get out. I told you I have to study.”

    “With Luthor?” Lucy laughs wryly. Which has Lena looking at Kara with an eyebrow raised, but Kara’s not looking, she’s too busy trying to swat Lucy out of the room. “Ok, ok, I get it. I’ll leave you two to…study.”

    Lena’s not sure how anyone can make the word study sound the way Lucy just did, but she knows Kara’s picked up on the innuendo, because she’s turned a different shade of pink as she finally closes the door.

    “Sorry about her, she’s always a bit extra. On and off the court.”

    “Aren’t all you hoopers like that?” Lena says and hears the scoff of indignation. “What you don’t have random groupies trying to get in your pants?” They both know Kara’s got plenty of people fawning over her. Lena’s witnessed it enough times around campus and if anything, this last summer only highlighted it even more. Lena may have made an effort not to look, but she couldn’t miss how many girls were making their presence known to Kara or the way Kara appeared to like the attention.

    “I’m not like Lane,” Kara looks mildly pissed at the comparison. “She brings some girls by to watch practice, not my business.”

    There’s a terseness in her words, that Lena chooses not to explore, returning her focus to the reason she’s in this building in the first place, she asks, “have you got the notes?”

    “Yep,” Kara takes a seat and they get to studying. It’s a surprisingly focussed session or perhaps it’s just that Kara’s unusually quiet? Lena doesn’t press and by the end of their time together, she thinks that if they keep up this level of progress, Kara might actually pass.

    —

    Her optimism doesn’t last all that long, not when she’s stuck waiting in their usual room only a few days later. She’s not even had a text to explain where Kara is and as each minute ticks by, she’s getting more and more irritated.

    She takes to the one reliable source of information on everything Kara Danvers and begins scrolling, half expecting to find a clip of Kara doing a TikTok with her teammates, but instead finds a series of blurred videos of her in an altercation outside the basketball arena. There’s another video of Kara looking decidedly pissed, putting her hand up to stop the person filming. In the background, she can hear Lucy shouting, her hands flailing wildly.

    “Sorry, about the hold-up,” Kara walks in and Lena’s phone slips from her hands at the sight of the bruise forming at the edge of the blonde’s cheek.

    “What happened?” She gasps, already on her feet.

    Kara swallows, her eyes look red and the usual swagger has gone.

    “Luce crossed a line, few guys from the away section got in her face.”

    “They did what?”

    “Shoved us about, campus security stepped in, but yeah, bad night.”

    “Let me see,” Lena steps closer, her fingertips brushing against the bump on Kara’s cheekbone. “They hit you?”

    “My face connected with a signpost, not my best evasive manoeuvre, Lane owes me.”

    “I’ll get some ice,” Lena’s got the lay of the building now, easily finds the ice box and wraps a few cubes in a towel. She leads Kara towards the couch, sits her down and then presses it to her face.

    “It’s nothing,” Kara smiles up at her. “But thank you,” she places her hand over Lena’s, where it holds the ice in place. “Can’t have my fans getting upset if the goods are damaged.”

    “Oh my god, you’re incorrigible.” Lena huffs, but the laughter spills from her mouth as she tugs her hand free and leaves Kara to nurse herself.

    —

    She watches the clips of the incident again later that night. Winces when Kara stumbles, hears the sharp whack of bone on metal. Then she gets scrolling and it’s an epic mistake.

    The release of Kara’s latest magazine cover has her heart skipping. She looks good, really good. Lena lingers a little too long at the image of Kara in a suit, lent back in a chair, her chin dropped and her eyes locked on the camera. It’s hot and makes Lena feel something she really shouldn’t be feeling, not towards Kara. 

    Lena swipes the app closed, opens her laptop and gets on with her own work. She really needs to stop thinking about Kara Danvers and she definitely never needs to look at her socials, ever again.

    —

    Kara’s on time the next time they meet, possibly because it’s already evening. Even so, she looks tired as she slumps into the armchair. Lena sighs as she looks up, not missing the way her blonde hair is left to loosely fall around her face, disguising the feint traces of the bruise.

    “What a day,” Kara moans. “Sorry, it’s late.”

    “Hooping or doing another photo shoot?”

    Kara looks embarrassed, momentarily, but then her confidence returns, “you know they all want a piece of this, almost like I’m irresistible,” her lips curl into a smile. “You liked my cover then?” she’s looking at Lena with interest and Lena realises her slip up.

    She can’t pretend she hasn’t seen it and says, “my friend’s a fan, even if he wasn’t, I think half of campus has something to say about it.”

    “I know it’s crazy,” Kara shakes her head in disbelief. “Don’t think I’ll ever get used to these kinds of things. Usually, it’s all basketball and this was out of my comfort zone, but it was a cool shoot?”

    She appears to be waiting for Lena to give some kind of affirmation, which she steadfastly refuses to do, somehow it’s enough for Kara, who simply laughs, in that deep rich way she does.

    “How about we get on with what I came here to do?“ Lena says, focusing on the notes in front of her, but she can still feel Kara watching her, as she fixes them both a drink.

    They get five minutes into the latest topic, before they’re interrupted by the doors being barged open. 

    “K.D!” a man says, when he spies Kara in the room, his arms full with boxes that he raises to show her. “You gotta check out the merch they sent over for you. Meghan is going to need some shots for publicity, when you can?”

    Kara leaps up, taking one of the boxes and following him towards the edge of the room, like she’s not supposed to be studying. 

    Lena watches as the pair joke around, feeling uneasy at the display. Kara’s rummaging through one of the boxes and then turns to Lena, holding up a grey tee, “what ya think?” 

    Lena’s takes a second to register what she’s looking at, a huge print of Kara’s face. “Really?” She scoffs.

    “People buy it,” Kara shrugs. “Must like my face.”

    “It’s a nice face,” the man who is stealing all Kara’s attention, juts his elbow into her side. Kara swats it away, with a strange giggle, which has Lena’s eyes bulging. The intruder throws a shirt at Kara’s face, before she twists up the one in her hand and whips it back at him. It’s childish, Kara’s giggling. It’s weird.

    Lena takes it all in with a tight jaw. She’s not seen Kara like this and something about the familiarity between the pair, leaves a bitter taste in her mouth. She coughs to get Kara’s attention. Anything to stop this display that’s bordering on flirtatious.

    “Sorry, bro, I’m studying,” she motions to where Lena is.

    “Sure, soon as you’re done, stuhh-day-ing?” he says, making it sound like it’s the last thing they’re doing. He then looks at Lena, his head tilts to one side and there’s a flash of recognition there.

    “This is Lena,” Kara says and that appears to be explanation enough, as the smile wipes from his face.

    “Lena?” He says.

    “Yeah,” Kara ducks her head, looking up through her lashes at Lena, who is struggling to hide how irritated she is at this point. “Uh, Lena, this is James. He’s the…” she pauses, looks at him in question. “He manages, stuff?”

    James gives a tight smile as he looks at Lena, “James Olsen, manager of stuff,” he says, rolling his eyes playfully at Kara and Lena can see it. Clear as day. This man has a crush on Kara. She feels a little sick.

    “He takes care of the business side of basketball, while Cat Grant does the coaching,” Kara explains, Lena’s not really listening though, she’s too busy meeting James’s cold hard stare.

    “Have we met?” Lena asks, when he doesn’t look away.

    “No,” he shakes his head. “I’d remember.”

    She can’t explain his hostility, but Kara picks up on it, nudges him in the ribs, “I’ll look over all this and fix up something with Meg tomorrow?”

    “Sure, don’t study too hard,” he says, bumping his fist against Kara’s. “Luthor,” he nods towards her, as he makes his way back out and the use of her last name, even though it was never offered, leaves Lena knowing exactly what his problem is.

    Kara tosses the shirt back in the box and sits back down. “What?” she asks when Lena simply stares at her, waiting for answers.

    “He has a problem with me being here,” she says.

    “No, why would he?” Kara searches her face, but she works out the answer to her own question before Lena has to enlighten her. “Some people weren’t too happy when the arena name got changed.”

    “I remember,” Lena twists the pen in her hands, she heard the rumblings of discontent over the unveiling of the Luthor Dome, wherever she went those first few weeks. “For what it’s worth, it wasn’t my idea.”

    “No, I got that. You hate sport, so I doubt you’d want to come in, change the name and try and rebrand us, just so your family looks good when we win?” Kara isn’t even bothering to hide her disdain.

    “I wanted nothing to do with you,” she says honestly and Kara’s frown deepens.

    “Got that impression, yep,” she drops her gaze, doodling something on her notes. 

    “LuthorCorp likes to support student programs,” Lena feels an urge to defend her brother’s actions.

    “And that’s cool,” Kara scribbles harder on her pad. 

    “What’s the problem?” Lena snatches the pen from her grip.

    “You support a team, you don’t come in and try and rename it. Rebrand. Mess up our whole colorway.”

    “What are you talking about?”

    Kara’s laugh is indignant, but when Lena looks at her blankly, she gets up and rummages around in one of the cupboards at the edge of the room.

    “This,” she holds up a jersey that Lena’s never seen before. It’s a deep maroon, Lex’s favourite colour.

    “Oh,” she knows exactly what her brother was up to. “I had no idea.”

    “Cat fought hard to make sure we didn’t completely sell out. You might have your name on the building, but this team has a legacy and it matters to some people,” Kara’s angry, Lena can understand why.

    “No, I know. Of course, it does,” Lena’s not ignorant. 

    “This isn’t your family color? It’s on all the Luthor products I’ve ever seen.”

    “I mean, it’s not really a thing,” she drops her chin. It kind of is. “Kara, I really didn’t know.”

    Kara stuffs the shirt back into the cupboard and takes a second to take a few breaths, “you mean it?”

    “I swear. Lex does what Lex wants to do, he always has. I came here to escape his world, even if it was only for a few years, just for me, to be Lena. Only Lena. My brother couldn’t let me have this to myself and made sure I was reminded of what he can do, every day. You might hate what he did to the arena, I do too, just for different reasons.”

    “Okay,” Kara says, a little more calmly as she processes what Lena’s said.

    “You believe me?” Lena’s surprised it’s that simple.

    “Why would you lie?” Kara’s answer is as genuine as ever. Lena’s not sure why it leaves her feeling a little warm inside.

    —

    It’s late by the time they finish, neither of them noticing the time until the janitorial staff appear in the doorway. Kara is quick to apologise for being in their way and makes an attempt to tidy up a few things, before they exit the building. It’s dark outside and there’s hardly a soul in sight.

    “Where do you live?” Kara asks when Lena hesitates in the doorway.

    “Just off campus. You?’

    “They take pretty good care of us here, so…” she thumbs back towards the student housing.

    “As in, you get all your meals and laundry taken care of?”

    “You think I’m going to find time to wash all my kit?”

    Lena rolls her eyes and as she looks up, she spots the first signs of rain, “great,” she huffs. 

    “Come on, if we walk quick, we’ll beat it.”

    “We?”

    “I’m not letting you walk home alone, it’s late,” Kara says, like Lena’s not done this a hundred times before

    “But you can then walk back here by yourself?”

    “Well, yeah?” Kara looks confused. 

    “If it’s safe enough for you, it’s safe enough for me,” Lena turns to walk away, but the first drop of rain, quickly turns into a downpour and she darts back under the cover of the canopy.

    “Ok, no walking you home, how about a ride?” Kara’s jogging away before Lena can stop her. 

    She has to really try not to laugh at the sight of Kara Danvers, driving a golf cart straight towards her, only a minute later. “I’m not letting you drive me home in that.”

    “Why not?” Kara laughs. “It’s got a cover. Quick and safe for me to drive home too.”

    “You stole it?”

    “Borrowed it,” Kara says with a guilty smirk. “Better be quick though, as I kind of need to return it before anyone knows it’s been, uh, borrowed…”

    “Oh my god, Kara. I can’t get into trouble.”

    “You won’t. It’s me. No-one’s going to kick up a fuss about the star-player and I think you know they’re not going to get all too mad at the woman whose family built them a shiny new arena either.”

    It’s true, Lena won’t admit it out loud, but when Kara pats the seat, she climbs in and they drive off as the rain begins to hammer down on the soft top of the cart.

    This is how Lena Luthor discovers that golf carts do not offer a high degree of shelter, not that it phases Kara, who talks non-stop on the way, about nothing Lena can quite catch onto. She gives out directions, whilst keeping her knees tucked in as close to the centre console as possible, but can still feel the water seeping into the side of her cardigan.

    “This it?” Kara pulls up, does a little wolf-whistle. “Nice!”

    “Much as I loved being on campus as a freshman, I could not wait to get out of halls.”

    Kara laughs, “it’s an acquired taste, not quite up to the standard of a billionaire’s daughter, I suppose.”

    “And how much is in your account?” Lena bites back sharply. 

    “Ok, so having money is a sore point, noted,” Kara turns the ignition off and faces Lena. 

    “It’s…” Lena huffs, she’s tired of all this. Her own hostility, the way she reacts to Kara with animosity for no discernible reason. “I didn’t choose to be wealthy.”

    “No, but it helps,” Kara’s smile is easy and Lena returns it. “This place is nice, that’s all I’m saying.”

    “I’ve no doubt you could live here too,” the rain is still falling and she’s no reason to still be sitting in this cart with Kara, yet she’s not made an effort to move. 

    “Yeah, but takes away from the experience and part of being a Wildcat is the connection we have off the court too. Building those kinds of bonds, can make all the difference at finals,” Kara says. “But yeah, I could see myself living here. You’d get up early, cook me eggs and everything.”

    “I’d do no such thing.”

    “Not an early riser?”

    “I am, but I’m not cooking for you. You’re perfectly capable of taking care of yourself.”

    “Ok, so I’ll cook the eggs. You want them in bed, or you a table service kind of girl?”

    Lena foolishly gets caught considering the question and then realises exactly what she’s doing, “you’ll never know,” she says, coyly. It seems to please Kara. Are they flirting? Lena’s so used to arguing that she’s caught off guard. She picks up her bag, deciding she really needs to get indoors and away from Kara, before she says or does something she can’t take back.

    “Hm,” Kara hums, a playful glint in her eyes as she starts the cart backup. “We’ll see.”

    “Goodnight, Kara,” Lena says with a soft shake of her head, giving up hope that it’ll hide her smile.

    “Sweet dreams, Miss Luthor,” Kara calls out, as Lena ascends the steps to her front door. She turns back to see Kara watching her all the way inside. As she clicks the door closed, she hears the distinct whoosh of the golf cart taking off at speed and releases a breath. What is she doing? Feels a lot like playing with fire. 

    Chapter 4: conflicting contradictions

    Chapter Summary

    Lena puts up her defences...kind of, but not really.

    Chapter Notes

    A few minutes in a golf cart, do not define Lena or how she feels about Kara, or so she tells herself over the course of the next few days. There’s a break in their schedule whilst Kara’s away, which gives her some breathing space to get her feelings in check. Not that she has feelings. She has problems. Problems that come from being associated with a person like Kara. 

    Because if there is anything Lena has been striving for the last few years, it’s existing without being seen. Something Kara Danvers is apparently incapable of, which is the whole problem in itself.

    Since the coffee shop photo, Lena has fended off various curious classmates, which she can handle, alongside the constant teasing from Sam, Jack and Jess. 

    She is, however, less prepared for her mother asking about it, during their weekly call. Lena’s made a considerable effort to avoid discussing the majority of her life at NCU up to this point, but one photo has led to her having to confess she’s tutoring Kara. The line goes silent and she knows it’s not a good sign.

    “I’ll speak to you next week,” her mother says, ending the call without Lena even saying goodbye.

    The foreboding feeling is enough to put Lena in a foul mood, which does not improve as her day progresses.

    It’s why she’s scowling across the bar at the recently returned, Kara and her teammates, only a few hours later. The group are loud and drawing all the attention thanks to a big win over their fierce rival. Lena’s given up counting the amount of fierce rivals the Wildcats have at this point, but from the way the beer is flowing, this one matters to both the team and their supporters. She also knows it’s the reason Sam and Jack had been so eager to go out in the first place. A low-level betrayal she should have seen coming and would have, if she’d not been avoiding all the Kara based posts or conversations in her life.

    It’s all the more annoying because it’s incredibly hard to not look at Kara, who is wearing wide-legged grey jeans, with an unbuttoned white shirt, over the top of a sports bra. Lena can make out the defined ridges of her abs, even from a distance and she grabs for her drink, desperately thirsty all of a sudden.

    This was not what Lena needed to witness, all she wanted was a night to herself, to forget. She hates Kara for ruining her perfectly good plan and she hates how her laugh is loud and how her face is pretty and how—she needs more to drink.

    —

    Jack’s smile is too wide as he returns to the table, carrying a tray of shots, “your girl’s here,” he announces in a singsong voice.

    The whole table turns to look and she really hates Jack right now, too. 

    Sam’s the first to catch on, her eyes lighting up with mischief, “oh, Lena, really?”

    “No, Sam, not at all,” she growls out, grabbing one of the shots and knocking it back. It burns its way down her throat, but does nothing to ease the feeling in her gut. Her eyes drift towards where Kara is currently chugging back a beer, much to the delight of all her teammates. “Why can’t they drink somewhere else?”

    “I thought you were friends,” Sam nudges Lena’s shoulder. “Everyone’s seen the coffee da—”

    “Not a date,” Lena cuts her off. It’s one thing for anonymous observers to have something to say about a chance meeting in a coffee shop, but not her friends.

    Sam’s unsubtly looking over to where the basketball team are enjoying their celebrations. “She’s kind of cute,” she says and Lena’s head spins around so fast, it has Sam laughing. “You don’t think…” Sam gestures towards where Kara is now cheering on Lucy. “Maybe she’s the answer to your problem?”

    “What problem?” Jack leans in conspiratorially.

    “The, Lena Luthor hasn’t had sex since And—”

    “I do not have a problem,” Lena kicks Sam in the shin, which thankfully shuts her up. 

    “Oh, but you do,” Jack snickers.

    “And, she is cute,” Sam shrugs. “Maybe if I got her number…”

    Lena’s not being fooled into reacting, she knows what Sam’s game is and with an air of nonchalance, she picks up another shot and says, “be my guest.”

    For a second she thinks Sam might actually get up and cross the room and her heart sits in her mouth as she waits, but Sam is looking at her, like she knows exactly what Lena’s thinking and laughs. “I’m just saying, it wouldn’t hurt to get it out of your system. A mind-blowing orgasm might relieve some of that tension.”
