    “What tension?” Lena snaps and gets to witness the silent conversation between Sam and Jack in reply. “Fine, whatever. Think what you like. I’ve a responsibility to tutor Kara, but it’s becoming more obvious why she’s so far behind in her studies,” she glares over to where Kara’s now got her arms wrapped around two of her other teammates shoulders as they all chant the Wildcat song.

    “You can’t judge people on their academic achievements,” Jack says, watching the same thing, but with an amused smile in contrast to Lena’s frown. “She’s a superstar, who cares if she gets a 2.0?”

    “I care, because I’m tutoring her,” Lena steals his shot. 

    “Hey, no fair,” he grumbles, taking it back, which is probably for the best. Lena can handle her drink, but she’s losing track of how many she’s had and being out of her mind with Kara close by, is probably not a good idea.

    “I’ll get another round,” she takes the tray and heads to the bar. She spends the next hour ignoring every comment her friends make about anything happening across the other side of the room. 

    —

    She’s in line for the restrooms, when she hears her. 

    “Luthor,” Kara says in way of greeting, Lena can hear the smile in her voice before she turns around to witness it.

    “Danvers,” she curtly replies.

    Kara baulks at Lena’s renewed hostility, but it’s not enough to make her walk away and not start up a conversation. 

    “Didn’t think you were much of a party girl,” Kara’s not subtle as she runs her eyes over Lena and Lena knows she looks good. She dressed for a night out, in a figure hugging dress, which she knows shows off all her assets, even without witnessing the effect it has on Kara. “Is the library closed or something?” She tries to joke, but her voice is rough as she wets her lips.

    “Funny,” Lena deadpans, struggling to not track the swipe of Kara’s tongue.

    “You know this is a bar and they serve alcohol?” She says in a faux whisper, which is slightly slurred thanks to the beers she was just chugging.

    “I’ve been here plenty of times before,” she’s overly defensive and it now appears to be amusing to Kara.

    “Oh, I know,” Kara says with her usual smug grin. 

    Lena’s confused, has Kara noticed her presence before? She’d question it, but there’s a group of girls nearby watching their interaction closely and she takes a step back. Kara glances over her shoulder and rolls her eyes. “Fans of yours?” Lena asks coolly.

    “Something like that,” Kara’s less cocky now. 

    Another girl rushes past them, then, shoving Kara as she tries to cut the line. The blonde falls into Lena and she instinctively puts up her arms, catching her. “You okay?” She asks as Kara regains her balance.

    “Yeah,” Kara says. Her irritation fading as she looks down at Lena. “Better now.”

    “Please stop,” Lena’s suddenly aware of where her hand rests on Kara’s waist, pressing against the warm skin. She pulls her hand away, but Kara catches her wrist.

    “Or what?” Kara tilts her head to one side. It’s a dare. Lena’s had too many drinks to fight against how her body reacts to Kara’s words. Her hips roll forward, almost touching Kara’s and it’s impossible to resist the craving. The look in Kara’s eyes says she knows exactly the effect she’s having and it’s all too alluring. It’d be easy to do something reckless now, like kiss Kara and prove to herself, it was good the first time around. She edges closer, Kara’s eyes darkening, her breath ghosting across Lena’s lips.

    “Kara?” A man says, interrupting whatever moment they were just having. Kara exhales, slow and shaky and Lena comes to her senses, taking a step backwards. “What’s the hold up? Oh…” he pauses, runs his eyes over Lena, lingering longer than necessary. 

    Lena shifts uncomfortably under his hot stare. Kara looks between them, her eyes narrowing when she realises he’s blatantly checking Lena out. She elbows him in the ribs, “back off, Mike.”

    “Not going to introduce me?” He smiles at Lena, it’s probably meant to be charming, but Lena’s not interested and she knows it shows. 

    “Lena, this is Mike, Mike this is Lena, she’s not interested,” Kara says with an amused confidence.

    “That’s because she doesn’t know me yet,” Mike says, his focus still on Lena.

    “Actually, Kara’s accurate in her assumption.”

    “Oh,” Mike takes a moment to look between them. “Oh!” He says, like he’s figured out what is going on, which is nothing, of course and Lena’s a mind to tell him as much, but he’s quickly lost interest in her, as he turns to Kara. “Then can you get on with ordering? Some of us are dying of thirst.”

    “Don’t let me keep you,” Lena says.

    “No?” Kara looks mildly confused.

    “No, we’re very done here and I’ve people who will be wondering where I’ve got to.”

    “Guess I’ll see you around then, Luthor,” Kara says, there’s a hint of a question in her words, Lena’s not entertaining any of it.

    “I’ll see you, when I’m scheduled to spend time with you.”

    “Like that is it?” Mike chuckles and earns another shove from Kara. 

    “Come on, you can help me,” she says to him. Angling her head so she can give Lena one last questioning look as she walks away.

    Lena returns to her friends soon after, catches Kara looking at her from where she’s waiting at the bar, for another round of drinks. The same guy, Mike, is talking to her not that Kara appears to be listening to him. 

    Lena glares back and Kara’s lips thin. Lena looks away.

    It’s better this way. Distance, hostility. No risk of feelings.

     

    Chapter End Notes

    Wonder how well that plan is going to work out for her...

    Chapter 5: truce

    Chapter Summary

    Stuck volunteering together for a day and Lena sees Kara in a different light.

    Chapter Notes

    Lena wakes up the next morning with a throbbing headache and a strange feeling in her chest. Her thoughts quickly drift to Kara and the look on her face, in the second before Mike had interrupted them and then, to every moment before and after.

    She’s gone from arguing, to kissing, to denial and now she’s entered the next phase of her, somewhat flimsy, plan and is being cold, standoffish. All the tricks she’s always used, in order to shut down any threat, but it feels wrong somehow and she has an urge to see Kara, if only to try and work out what all these thoughts mean.

    Two coffees and a minivan ride later, she gets the opportunity to do just that. It’s not something she’d planned, in fact it’s a complete shock to find herself in the same room as almost all of the basketball squad. 

    It leads to her quickly regretting her offer to help out at the last minute, (and after two shots of vodka), which left her without the good sense to check the details. Now she’s in a Community centre as part of an outreach project, serving breakfast, alongside none other than, Kara Danvers.

    “This isn’t quite what I had planned when I suggested breakfast,” Kara says, a sure smile on her face as she plops a serving of scrambled eggs onto an outstretched tray. It’s like last night didn’t happen and Lena’s wondering how drunk Kara was now.

    “Sorry?” Lena’s too caught up in her thoughts, to know what Kara’s talking about.

    “The night in the golf cart?” Kara chances, waiting for Lena to catch up.

    “Oh, yes,” she shakes her head, strangely flustered at the reminder of an evening, where she’d stopped trying to argue and was almost able to accept, she likes Kara.

    “You okay?” Kara asks, her blue eyes searching Lena’s for answers. “Or did you have a bit too much to drink, last night?”

    “I can handle my drink,” Lena’s short in her reply and instantly regrets it. She’s tired of being frosty, but she’s not sure there’s any other way to exist around Kara right now. 

    “Okay then,” Kara gives a tight smile, in reply. “For the record, I’m sorry if I made you uncomfortable. I know I’ve been teasing you about, uh,” she stirs her spoon around in the dish in front of her, looking slightly flushed.

    “About?” Lena has a good idea exactly what Kara’s talking about, but wants Kara to say it, she’s not filling in the blanks for her and getting teased if she’s wrong.

    “The kiss,” Kara mumbles. “I get that you want to forget about it and I was…well…maybe I thought…I’ll stop. No more teasing,” her eyes meet Lena’s then, sure, a promise.

    “Thank you,” Lena says and carries on serving as the line moves past them. She’s not sure it’s what she wants, but it might help.

    —

    “Can we get a photo?” A short man with dark hair asks, he’s already snapping away before either of them can consent and Lena freezes. “If you stand to the side, I can get the Wildcats logo in it and—”

    “Maybe give us a second to actually serve the food,” Kara suggests, her tone clipped and the man picks up on it immediately.

    “Absolutely, of course, you serve away. I’ll be right back,” he stumbles in his haste to move on and try and capture another moment from whatever this charade is.

    Lena huffs, she’s all for giving back to the community, but is definitely not comfortable with being part of some basketball PR event.

    “Not seen you at one of these before,” Kara comments as the people move along.

    “Possibly because I’ve made it my goal to avoid any of the sports backed projects?”

    “Obviously,” Kara’s tone is dripping with sarcasm and when Lena chances a look her way, she sees the way she’s rolling her eyes. 

    Maybe there’s some good humour in it, but Lena’s hackles are raised and she can’t hold back her next words.

    “I tend to find the teams only ever do these things, to make themselves look good…” she waves to where the little man is still taking photos for whatever press release is on the horizon. “…which is not something I care to be involved in,” she adds and yes, it’s snooty, it’s also not the whole truth. Lena has deliberately avoided anything involving the sports teams, but not because she hates sports. She’s kept her distance because they tend to attract a lot more attention. Attention she doesn’t need. Not when she’s curated a life that goes under the radar at NCU.

    Kara narrows her eyes, clearly irritated by Lena’s comment and their familiar punchy back and forth returns in an instant, “guess you do this to make yourself feel less guilty about the millions of dollars your family dodges in taxes? You know if the rich paid their dues, we wouldn’t need these kinds of drives.”

    Lena’s anger boils to the surface, as it so frequently does whenever she’s around Kara, but it’s more personal now and she can’t bite back the hostility caused by someone taking issue with her family and judging her by their actions, “because it’s all pure and for the good of the people in the world of sports? If colleges and team owners weren’t so greedy, we could distribute more funds into community projects and make sure access to sport is guaranteed, no matter what your background, but then the players wouldn’t get their inflated salaries or worse, they could lose out on a free ride through college.”

    Kara sucks in a breath through her nose and nods, “okay, fair enough. There’s work to be done at every level.”

    Lena refuses to look at Kara as they get on with the task at hand, the second she’s served the last helping, she whips off her apron.

    “Lena?” Kara sighs. “Can we…”

    “Do you actually need my help for something?”

    “No, I just—”

    “Then I’d like to get on with doing, what I came here to do and I’d prefer to not be doing it, with anyone who chooses to judge me by my last name.”

    “I…” Kara stutters, looking visibly chastised. 

    “Are we done?” She raises one eyebrow and Kara nods. “Good,” she gets back to organising everyone else, but she feels anything but good.

    — 

    Having successfully avoided Kara for the rest of the morning, she pauses at the sight of her shooting hoops with some of the kids. She’s mucking about and showing a couple of girls some tricks, which has drawn quite a crowd, it’s quite obvious that they all seem to love her and Kara’s enjoying herself, too. Laughing and joking, but also sinking bucket after bucket, much to the delight of those around her. 

    Kara drops a perfect three and celebrates by running around the group, high-fiving them as she goes, she’s halfway around when her eyes lock onto Lena and it feels like time suddenly stands still. Kara’s smile grows, Lena’s throat feels impossibly dry. She turns on her heel and gets back to the crafting table she’s supposed to be supervising.

    —

    She’s pinning up the banner, her group have just made, when Kara appears again, taking the other end to help her, Lena’s not averse to the assistance, but because it’s Kara, she takes it begrudgingly.

    “I went too far,” Kara says, once she’s got her side in place. She rushes to assist Lena and that’s a problem in itself. She’s too close, that same smell, the warmth she exudes. Lena wants to run, but she’s stuck with two arms in the air and on her tiptoes. Kara’s hands slide over hers, grabbing for the pin and taking over. “There, looks good,” she says, taking a step back and Lena’s relieved for the space. She looks at the sign, then at Kara, who appears to be waiting for Lena to say something.

    “It does, thanks for helping.”

    “And, I meant it, I’m sorry for what I said before.”

    “Apology accepted,” Lena knows Kara means it and she could do with this conversation ending, but Kara’s not moving.

    “So, we’re good?” She asks, somewhat unsure of herself for a change.

    “Why wouldn’t we be? You told me what you think of my family. Nothing I’ve not heard before.”

    “Um,” Kara bites down on her bottom lip and Lena’s stupid enough to track the motion. “Like I said, out of line. I don’t know your family and I was wrong to judge.”

    “Honestly, Kara, it’s fine. Don’t stress about it.”

    “Yeah?”

    “Yes,” Lena wonders why they’re still talking. It’s obvious now that Kara has an issue with who Lena is and Lena, well, she’s an issue with everything about Kara. She was right last night. Space, distance—

    Her thoughts are cut off by Kara’s continuing need to talk, “we do this thing after an outreach where we take some kit over to the local clubs, then get a few drinks to end the day, if you want—”

    “No, I’m good,” Lena shakes her head in alarm. No way is she going for a drink with Kara or any of the basketball team.

    “Oh, come on, Luthor, do you good to enjoy the other side of what we do here.” Lena’s not sure she needs to do any such thing. “Everyone else said yes,” Kara adds with a little wiggle of her eyebrows and why that happens to be tempting, she has no idea. The invite feels like a challenge and for some reason, other than it’s the only way she’s getting a ride back towards campus, Lena feels like she can’t turn it down.

    “Fine, I’ll come and see the magical work of the Wildcats, but only after this place is completely tidy.”

    Kara’s smile is too wide, it’s weird. They’re supposed to be enemies, or so Lena thought, but these last few weeks and that fateful kiss has upended everything. She should probably find a way out of it, but it’s impossible to do, once she sees the effort Kara puts into getting the whole team fixing things up, just so they can leave. 

    —

    This is how Lena finds herself stuck next to Kara in the minivan on the way over to a youth club. Their shoulders are pressed together, thanks to the limited space, but by some small mercy, Kara’s strangely silent.

    It’s almost bearable until one of her teammates, Nia Nal, starts chatting to her over the back of her seat.

    “Do you think he’s going to show?” Nia asks.

    “He’ll be there,” Kara says, patting her friend on the shoulder. “You asked him.” She turns to Lena then and says, “Nia’s yearning after this guy in her class.”

    “I’m not yearning,” Nia swats at Kara’s hand, but it only makes Kara laugh.

    “Do you think he likes me?” Kara makes a poor attempt at impersonating Nia. “I think he does, do you think he’ll come to the event I asked him to? He came to the last thirteen of them, but I don’t—”

    “Okay, that’s enough,” Nia glares at Kara and she does actually shut up, for one whole second.

    “Nia’s in love and she’s definitely yearning,” Kara teases.

    “Ok, Supergirl, just because you’re hopelessly single after Siobhan bad mouthed you to anyone who’d listen.” Kara’s ear’s turn pink, but Nia’s not done. “And now everyone thinks you hooked up with those twins in Star City and caught that—”

    “I did not,” Kara cuts Nia off, before she can finish, but Lena’s heard this story. Not that she paid it much attention. “I never did anything with any twins and I am not carrying some made-up disease,” she says directly to Lena, not that it would matter to her.

    “Fascinating,” Lena turns away to look out of the window. Nia gets up and switches seats at some point and Kara nudges against Lena.

    “You know I didn’t do any of that?”

    “It’s none of my business.”

    “No, but…”

    “Kind of sucks when people judge you for something you didn’t do, huh?” Lena says, pointedly.

    “Yeah, it does,” Kara at least looks contrite as she rubs at the back of her neck. “I got you wrong and…well,” she thrusts her open hand towards Lena’s side of their shared seat. “Start over?” 

    It takes Lena a second to realise Kara expects her to shake it, “why should we?”

    “I don’t know,” Kara shrugs. “Might make both our lives easier?”

    This is something Lena can definitely agree with. 

    “Fine,” Lena gives in with a soft huff, taking Kara’s hand and shaking it. She doesn’t have the energy to keep fighting like this, but the second their skin touches, she knows it’s a mistake. Kara’s hands are big, warm, calloused from where she lifts weights and spends too much time gripping that orange ball and Lena instantly wonders how good it would feel to have them all over her body? 

    If she’s blushing, Kara wouldn’t know, because she stares at the point of contact for far longer than Lena.

    —

    The decision to go anywhere with the basketball team, is something she quickly regrets, because it leads to her witnessing, for the second time in as many hours, just how approachable and fun Kara is. 

    The kids love her, clamour for her attention and Kara is happy to give it out, smiling and taking time to say hello, pose for photos and sign whatever item is thrust in front of her face. Once she’s finally given some space, she wraps her hands around a ball and shows off the reason why everyone is talking about her.

    Lena’s passively observed enough games in her time, to know, Kara’s good, better than good. She catches Lena’s eye as she passes, winks at her, then switches direction, heading into the paint, before executing a perfect fadeaway. When their eyes meet a second time, Lena can’t bite back a smile. 

    —

    They pull up outside a sports bar an hour later, with the team talking animatedly as they make their way inside. Lena looks around and isn’t surprised to see the walls are adorned with different jerseys from the last few decades, including several from the college teams. There are also several screens scattered around the panelled walls, giving everyone the chance to watch whatever is being streamed, which of course happens to be basketball, much to Kara’s delight. 

    Kara insists on buying her drink. “You’re here as my guest,” she says, like that matters for some reason? Lena sticks to her new plan of not arguing with Kara and uses it as an excuse to escape to the restroom, if only to give herself a moment to gather her thoughts. She clocks her reflection in the mirror and can practically hear her mother’s scathing remark if she were to catch sight of her daughter in a public venue, hair tied back and wearing an oversized grey sweatshirt. 

    It’s definitely not Lena’s usual attire for going out for a drink, but considering the basketball team are all wearing various bits of training gear, she truly does not stand out.

    “One drink,” she tells herself. Taking a breath and pushing the door back open.

    She returns to the table and can’t miss the way Lucy vacates the space next to Kara, leaving it for Lena to move in beside her.

    “Here you go,” Kara slides a glass of white wine towards her.

    “Thank you,” Lena’s not sure why it feels so difficult to sit beside Kara and just act normally.

    “You enjoyed today?” Kara asks, after the awkwardness persists. 

    “Not as much as you,” Lena warms a little at the thought of how happy Kara had looked on the court.

    “It’s kind of a buzz and the kids had fun, that’s what matters,” she says.

    “You like these events?”

    “Love them,” Kara smiles. “Makes a lot of the other pressures in the game, feel a little more worthwhile.”

    “I could tell you were in your element,” Lena tries not to get too caught up in Kara’s smile or the memory of how good she looked on the court.

    “Wait until you see me play for real,” Kara winks and Lena’s not sure why she suddenly has an urge to do just that. Not that she’s letting Kara know it.

    “You’ll be waiting a long time for that one, I’m afraid,” she says coyly, sipping her drink.

    “Why? What is so wrong with the beautiful game of basketball, that Lena Luthor won’t come and cheer on her, and I’m definitely biased here, exceptionally talented and bound for the natty champs, team?”

    “Exceptionally talented?”

    “Oh, for sure, we’re on a hot streak, everyone’s afraid of us.”

    “Hmm,” Lena can’t hide her smile and Kara’s doing an equally terrible job. Their usual bickering and teasing, is more light-hearted, easy, natural. Lena could fall into this kind of interaction. Could imagine how nice it’d be to spend time with Kara, just like this. “That’s why everyone’s talking about you?”

    “It’s just a part of what I do. People want to talk about me and I guess it means they’re talking about the game too, think it’s a good thing. You don’t like all eyes on you?”

    “I hate it,” she says truthfully. She’s seen how Lex loves to attract all the attention in the room and it’s never been something she’s coveted. “Everyone looking at you?” She shudders at the thought, but when she looks at Kara she’s surprised to see how soft her smile is.

    “Plenty of people are already looking at you,” she says, her eyes drifting towards Lena’s lips before she seems to catch herself. “Not sure you’re going to have much luck at being as anonymous as you want to be.”

    Lena opens her mouth and closes it again, Kara smiles and oh, Lena’s getting herself into a world of trouble by returning it. 

    “Danvers!” James appears from nowhere, much to both of their surprise. Kara startles, whereas Lena simply glares, not that he seems to notice her at all.

    “Hey buddy,” Kara greets him, but she looks unimpressed by his sudden appearance, or maybe that’s just Lena’s wishful thinking? “What are you doing here?”

    “Don’t worry, I’m not on duty. Lucy messaged.”

    “She did?” Kara pulls a face, which has Lena slightly intrigued.

    “Can I get you a beer?” James asks, but Kara holds up her full cup in answer. He looks between Kara and Lena briefly, forcing a smile to his face, “I’ll catch you later, then,” he says, heading to the bar, alone.

    “Lucy and him have a thing,” Kara explains once he’s out of earshot.

    “Really?” Lena’s surprised after all the girls, she’s seen on Lucy’s arm and the fact that James blatantly has a crush on Kara.

    “It’s messy, definitely to be avoided,” Kara seems oblivious.

    “But you two get on well?” Lena has to ask.

    “Yeah, I mean he knows my cousin,” Kara takes a drink and glances at the screen, making an “oof” sound when one of the players hits the hardwood.

    “Did you date?” Lena decides it’s best to be direct if she’s going to get an answer.

    Kara almost spits out her beer, ”what? No,” she shakes her head furiously. “James isn’t…not, no, you know?”

    “Does he know?” Lena says with a raise of her eyebrow and Kara’s face is a picture as she processes what Lena’s saying.

    “He should,” she glances towards the bar and then her eyes meet Lena’s, wide as she shakes her head from side to side. “Never going to happen.”

    Why this makes Lena feel good? She’s not asking, but for a second they just sit there, looking at each other and it probably says a lot more than words ever could.

    —

    Nia and Lucy fall into their booth and the conversation moves on to talk of their plans for the weekend. Nia goes quiet midway through a sentence and Kara nudges Lena, gesturing towards the man, who has just walked in. She recognises Querl, from her robotics club, but he only has eyes for Nia as he crosses the room. It’s easy to see the couple’s connection, even if they’re both a little shy around one another. She catches Kara looking at them with a soft affection at one point and it makes her feel something unfamiliar.

    She’s rescued from her own thoughts, when Jack bombards her with texts, asking where she is and why she’s not currently sat next to him at their usual table across town.

    “I’d better make a move,” she says, finishing her drink. She chooses to ignore the sad look on Kara’s face and definitely doesn’t spend too long focussing on the way she’s pouting, or how cute she looks.

    “You had fun though?” Kara checks, likes she’s unsure of how to read Lena.

    “I did, thank you for asking me.”

    “Any time,” Kara looks very pleased with herself and Lena’s tempted to knock her down a peg, but decides she kind of likes Kara like this and doesn’t.

    “I’ll see you Wednesday,” she quickly texts Jack to say she’s on her way.

    “Of course,” Kara gets to her feet and for a second Lena thinks she’s about to hug her before she leaves, but she seems to think better of it and offers an awkward wave instead. Then with her trademark smirk she adds, “at our next scheduled appointment?”

    Lena rolls her eyes and Kara laughs. She can still picture the resulting smile on Kara’s face when she gets into bed later that night.

    Chapter End Notes

    Next up: Lena starts watching and it could be her downfall. (some may say she's past that point)

    Chapter 6: on the court

    Chapter Summary

    Watching, brings all kinds of feelings to the surface.

    Chapter Notes

    Lena starts watching. It happens accidentally and it’s all Jack’s fault. They’re supposed to be studying, when he starts complaining about how Lena never wants to take a break.

    “We could be in your dome with everyone else, actually enjoying a Sunday.”

    “We have to work on this paper, you promised, no distractions.”

    “A break is good for the mind. Helps me focus.”

    Lena’s not in the mood. She huffs, turns on the tv, finding the live stream and has every intention of leaving him to it.

    “We missed the opening five,” Jack grumbles, but is quick to make himself comfortable. 

    Lena doesn’t move from the kitchen table, but as the game plays out in the background and Jack repeatedly shouts at whatever is happening, she slowly gets drawn in. Especially when Jack yells, “get your hands off our Danvers.”

    Lena looks up, watches a replay of Kara being caught in the ribs by a mask-wearing opponent.

    “I thought basketball was non-contact,” Lena says, grabbing two bottles out of the fridge and passing one to Jack, she lingers at the back of the couch, waiting for Kara to take the free throws. The first rattles around the rim, finally dropping in. Kara turns to her teammates, taps hands with Lucy and Nia, then takes the ball for the second.

    “They either double team KD or they foul her,” Jack twists the cap off his bottle, Kara nets the bucket, and the game resumes.

    Lena returns to her seat, but her paper is all but forgotten. She tells herself paying attention, is purely for research purposes. Hoping she can find proof that Kara isn’t the centre of everything that happens on the court, even if the entire student body, would swear that she is.

    It takes five minutes for her to realise it’s a futile endeavour. Kara’s influence on the game is obvious, even to Lena. She really is everywhere. Causing problems in attack, covering in defence. Banging in mid-range shots, drawing players out, setting and calling screens. It’s hectic and then Kara takes another hit.

    “Oh, we got blood,” Jacks seems excited by this development, Lena’s still wincing from the impact and Kara is lying face down on the court. It takes her an age to get up and Lena can see the concern on all those around her.

    Kara sits out the next few minutes, a team around her, making it hard to see what is happening, even though the camera continuously pans back to her.

    Lena goes back to her studies, the game suddenly less appealing without Kara on the court, but she looks up when she hears news of Kara’s return. 

    The bleeding’s stopped or at least been stemmed and she has a white nasal tampon shoved up one nostril.

    “Oh, they do not know what they’ve unleashed,” Jack claps his hands together. Lena sees it too, Kara’s angry and as soon as she’s back in the game, she is on fire. Steaming around the court, stealing balls, shooting buckets and drawing fouls. She’s pumped, gritting her teeth and punching downwards. She looks ridiculously hot, flexed, toned, inviting… Lena looks away. 

    They win the game by fifteen points and Kara’s grabbed for a court side interview before she can exit down the tunnel.

    “You looked like you had a score to settle?”

    “Not a fan of being whacked in the nose,” Kara chuckles.

    “No lasting damage,” the interviewer says, she’s smiling up at Kara, looking like she’s just another one of Kara’s fans.

    “Nope, just a knock.”

    “You made them pay, can we expect more of this Kara in the coming weeks?”

    “We’ll see,” Kara shrugs. “Credit to the rest of the team, they make it easy for me to move about like that, taking some big hits today. We got it done, go wildcats.”

    “Thanks, Kara, go enjoy yourself,” the reporter waves her away and starts talking to the studio. 

    Jack mutes the sound, gets up off the couch and turns to her with a huge smile. “Now that was a game, bet there will be one hell of a party tonight, boo to us having to study.”

    “How about I make it easier by letting you talk basketball, during every break?” Lena caves.

    “It’s a poor second, but I’ll take it,” he says, not like he has much choice, not when they really do have work to get done. For the first time, Lena engages in some of his idle basketball chatter, which gives her the opportunity to find out more about Kara in return. There’s no harm in it, not really. She’s not going to get invested, that’s never going to happen.

    —

    Kara’s sitting in their usual study spot, when Lena arrives the next morning, her head down as she watches something on her iPad.

    “How’s the nose?” Lena asks when she gets closer, catching sight of the replay Kara’s watching.

    Kara pauses the film, looking up at Lena in surprise, “you watched.”

    Lena examines the covered up traces of a bruise around her eye. “Jack did, he was screaming about there being blood,” her fingers hover just over where there’s a mild swelling.

    “Just a knock,” Kara’s eyes never leave hers.

    “Made you angry though,” Lena smirks, pulling her hand away. 

    Kara sits back, folding her arms across her chest, taking a moment to appraise Lena and this new development of her actually knowing what happened in a game. She smiles then, wide and genuine.

    “Don’t poke a bee’s nest,” Kara says. “You see the three pointer?” She asks a little more excitedly.

    Lena knows which three pointer she means, it was quite a shot, the basket making a beautiful swoosh sound as it hit and Kara turning towards the assembled fans, roaring as they all screamed back at her. Not that Lena’s going to admit she watched such a (exciting) moment. She shakes her head, not wanting to inflate Kara’s ego further. “I had work to do.”

    “Shame,” Kara shrugs. “You really should come and watch it live, the atmosphere is hella different, I can get you court side, being up close is best.”

    They share a look then and Lena has to wonder if Kara’s thinking about how up close they could get.

    Lena decides it’s best to stop that thought, “and look like I enjoy sports?”

    “Not the worst thing in the world, Lena,” Kara sighs.

    “Tell that to my mother,” Lena can picture the look on Lillian’s face if she caught sight of her daughter at a college basketball game. If it’s not about business or being seen in the right place, like it is for Lex, then it’s not somewhere a Luthor needs to be. Not that she’s explaining the ins and outs of her life to Kara. “It’s one thing to be tutoring you, another to support you.”

    “You care that much about what your mom thinks?” Kara says nonplussed.

    “You wouldn’t understand,” Lena gets her laptop out and starts to set up. Nothing’s simple, not when you’re a Luthor. “Let’s just say in this case, it’s not worth poking a bee’s nest.”

    Kara hums, but doesn’t push the point. Lena can tell she wants to, it’s written all over her face, but talking about how she has spent most of her life trying not to make waves with Lillian, isn’t what she needs to be doing right now.

    —

    “I’m going to be back late Sunday,” Kara says, once they’re finished. “and they’re hosting an alumni thing here. So, we won’t be able to get this room.”

    “The library?”

    “Actually, I was thinking, hoping really, that you’d come over to the house?”

    Lena hesitates, they do need to meet up. Kara’s got a makeup test on Monday and it’s part of their plan, it’s just that up until now, they’ve kept these sessions on semi-neutral ground, if you can call the players’ lounge all that neutral. Going to Kara’s has zero appeal, it’s not the kind of place known for its academic accomplishments, more its propensity for drinking beer and shooting hoops, which is everything Lena has zero interest in.

    “Won’t there be some kind of party after?”

    Kara’s brow scrunches, “you shouldn’t listen to all the talk about us.”

    “About the team or just you?”

    “Both,” Kara says, stuffing her textbook back in her bag. 

    “I’ve seen the reels,” Lena says flatly. Part of her fast education from Jack the other night, has left her with plenty of insight into what happens off court.

    “Okay, so sometimes we have fun,” Kara concedes. “But I’m committed to doing this work right now. The team have my back, too.”

    “Really?” Lena says incredulously and Kara stops fidgeting with the cuffs of her hoody.

    “Yes, very much really,” she says and at Lena’s disbelieving look, she adds, “I’m serious. My focus is on my game. I want to enjoy every second on court and to do that, I need to put in the work off it. When I’m not playing or training, I’m doing this,” she gestures between them. Lena’s taken aback, but Kara reads her wrong, huffing as she folds her arms across her chest. “Fine, think what you like. I’ve got goals and I know what it takes to achieve them. Doubt all you want.”

    “I don’t doubt you,” Lena says quickly and Kara looks surprised. “I know you’re going to achieve everything you set out to, I just,” Lena swallows, thinks how to phrase it. “I got the impression you were also about seeking fun.”

    Kara’s cheeks pink, just the tiniest. “I like to have fun,” she says, somehow fun, sounds like sex to Lena’s ears, the way Kara’s looking at her, says it’s exactly what she’s getting at too. “But I’m not messing around. This is my future. It’s why I want to work as hard with you here, as I do out there.”

    “Okay,” Lena feels bad for making Kara defend herself, but it’s actually kind of nice to hear that she is committed. She’s invested in Kara’s success, academically at least and wants to make sure she is set for her test. “What time will you be home?”

    “8 maybe, but more like 9pm?” Kara looks doubtful. “Sometimes get caught up with the media stuff.”

    “I’ll be there for 9pm.”

    “Great,” Kara sounds relieved as she slings her backpack over her shoulder. “Thanks, Luthor. Think I’ll have this next test aced and then you’re going to be so proud of me.” 

    It’s a throwaway comment, or Lena thinks it is, but then she gets stuck with it playing on repeat in her head. The idea she’d be proud of Kara? Or that Kara seems to want that to happen? It’s too much to process, so she does what she does when she can’t think it through and simply makes a noncommittal grunt. If it makes the smile on Kara’s face dull just a little, well, Lena’s not going to think on how that makes her feel either.

    —

    The basketball house is exactly as she’d imagined. There are cars parked haphazardly outside and a huge Wildcats banner hanging from an upstairs window. A hoop in the yard and orange balls discarded on the path towards the front door, like everyone was in a rush to get inside and clearing up their mess, is the last thing on a hoopers mind.

    It’s also quiet, no signs of any afterparty and as she steps closer, all she hears is animated chatter coming from inside.

    She knocks, twice and the door is quickly yanked open.

    “You’re not my pizza,” Lucy Lane answers, looking displeased, but ushering Lena in like it’s a normal thing for her to be standing in the doorway, late on a Sunday night. “Kara’s upstairs, second on the right.”

    “Thank you,” Lena says, but Lucy’s already back in the lounge, joining the rest of Kara’s teammates, who are all huddled together, playing video games.

    She climbs the stairs, sucks in a breath and taps her knuckles against the wooden frame.

    “S’open,” Kara calls out. Lena turns the handle and the door swings back to reveal, Kara standing in the middle of the room, her arms in the air and a jersey halfway over her head. She wrestles with it briefly and then finally tugs the thing loose, chucking it towards a pile of clothes in the corner. She turns to Lena with a wide smile, “sorry, I literally just got in.” She makes a hurried attempt to make her bed, whilst Lena stands in the doorway and finds herself incapable of looking away.

    “Or you just got beat in a last minute game of Mario Kart and rushed up here to change when you heard me pull up?” Lena guesses and Kara’s cheeks instantly turn pink.

    “They made me play this one round,” she says, “but I really did just get back. I had to do media, whilst they all got to come back here and order pizza.”

    “It’s still not arrived,” Lena says, taking a step inside. “Lucy was less than pleased to find me on the other side of door.”

    “Was she rude to you?” Kara stops tidying, a pillow gripped firmly in her hands. Lena wonders if she’s thinking about going downstairs to whack Lucy with it.

    “She was Lucy,” Lena shrugs and it’s enough explanation for Kara. “You win?”

    “You know we did,” Kara rolls her eyes playfully. 

    “I’ve other things to do with my day.”

    “We won,” Kara huffs, like Lena not watching actually bothers her. She pulls on a clean sweatshirt, then rummages in her backpack, finding her books and throwing them onto the bed, before climbing on top of it and looking at Lena like she’s waiting for her to do the same.

    Lena does no such thing, instead moving towards the wooden chair and taking a seat at the tiny desk. “Ready?”

    “It’s comfier here,” Kara laughs, but leaves Lena to it as she gets to answering the questions Lena throws at her.

    They’re about ten minutes in and Kara hasn’t got a thing wrong, when a knock at the door startles them both.

    “Pizza,” Nia says from the other side and Kara practically flies off her bed, racing out the door. Lena hears her heavy footsteps as she descends the stairs and the shout she makes when she catches Leslie already helping herself to Kara’s order.

    Kara reappears with a box in her hand and slams her door shut, “Willis can’t keep her hands off my things,” she complains, dropping to the floor and opening the box, patting a spot on the ground beside her, as if Lena’s about to join her. “You don’t eat pizza or something?” She says when Lena makes no move and when she still doesn’t answer, Kara looks at her in shock, “really?”

    “No, I do, but,” Lena shakes her head. “I can eat later.”

    “You know I don’t usually share my food.”

    “Am I supposed to feel honoured?”

    “Sit down and eat, Lena,” Kara says with a resigned sigh and Lena does. The pizza’s good. The way Kara looks at her whilst she’s eating it, is probably what makes it taste even better.

    Studying takes a backseat as they talk over their food, backs resting against the end of Kara’s bed. If Lena paid more attention to the moment she was in, she’d realise how much they had to say to one another. From small things, like the perfect pizza topping, to Kara letting slip how she’s always dreamed of being the number one draft pick.

    Kara tells her all about the game, too. It’s different to hear it from her point of view and she easily gets caught up in the excitement of it all, as Kara moves onto to talk of March and their chance at a top seed.

    “We keep winning and they have to see us as real contenders,” Kara makes it sound like there’s some doubt.

    “Jack seems to think it’s a certainty, half the campus do,” Lena says.

    “Well, yeah,” Kara looks down at the last slice, offering it to Lena, who declines. “You’d think so, but the committee make a final call. Nothing is guaranteed and we’re going to have to go on a streak after break.”

    “I guess all this,” she glances to where their books lay waiting for them to get back to it, “is going to have to take a backseat?”

    “I’ll make time,” Kara says. “I meant what I said to you the other day. Besides, college is all about balance or something. That’s what Alex keeps saying to me.”

    “Alex?”

    “My sister, she’s always good for keeping me grounded.”

    Lena’s not sure why hearing that, makes her happy, but it does. “If it helps, I think you’re going to do just fine tomorrow and from here on out, it’ll be a breeze to graduation.”

    “Well, that’s because I’ve got the best tutor on campus,” Kara says with a smugness that makes Lena chuckle. Kara must read it as self-deprecating when she asks, “do you not believe in yourself?”

    “Oh, I do,” Lena returns with an assuredness that matches Kara’s on court persona. “I’m the best and you definitely got lucky, when they assigned me to you.”

    “Yeah,” Kara says softly, which makes Lena look at her curiously. “I really did.”

    Lena gets lost in Kara’s eyes for a beat too long, “how about we get back to it and make sure you succeed at the first step, huh?” she says, dropping her gaze and hoping Kara misses the blush creeping across her cheeks.

    —

    “Good luck, tomorrow,” Lena says on her way out the door. It’s strangely quiet in the lounge as Leslie, Nia and Lucy do a terrible job at pretending not to eavesdrop.

    “Thank you,” Kara says, before glaring back at her housemates. She steps outside with Lena, pulling the door closed behind her. “And thank you for coming over, it really helps. I just hope I pass this.”

    Lena takes a step closer, reaching out to gently rub Kara’s arm, “you’re going to be fine.” Lena’s learnt a lot about Kara over the last few weeks, but one thing is obvious, she’s a lot smarter than her grades suggested and she’s only been struggling because basketball takes up the majority of her life. 

    Kara looks down at where Lena’s hand is, “I was, um—”

    “Kara, the game’s on,” Leslie shouts from inside and Kara closes her eyes, letting out a soft sigh.

    “Go, I’ll see you next week,” Lena takes another step away. 

    “See you soon, Luthor,” Kara hovers in the doorway, until another shout out has her telling them she’s on her way.

    Lena makes her way down the path, she can feel Kara’s eyes on her all the way to her car, when she turns back, she offers a wave, which Kara returns before she finally heads inside.

    If Lena’s left catching her breath for a few seconds before she starts the engine, Kara will never know.

    Chapter End Notes

    catching feelings?

    Chapter 7: caught in a moment

    Chapter Summary

    a gift, a wager, a feeling.

    Kara does ace her test. Lena finds out when she’s bombarded with messages to say as much and then a delivery arrives at her house and Sam is ecstatic.

    “She bought you flowers,” Sam says, swiping the card before Lena even realises what is happening. “Told you I’d make you proud,” she reads aloud. “Oh, she did, hmm?”

    Lena snatches it from Sam’s hand and pockets it. She’ll read that note over and over a few times later, but first she needs to shut off whatever this thing, that Sam’s creating, is. “It’s part of the assignment, a thank you isn’t all that surprising,” Lena says, like it’s nothing.

    “Yes, because last year, you received countless gifts from Dave.”

    “Darius,” Lena corrects her, even though it’s pointless. “And he did send me a thank you note. I just didn’t share it.” It was an email, it contained the words, thank and you. Lena kept it, no big deal.

    “But not flowers,” Sam dances around the table. “Pretty flowers, for a pretty lady.”

    “It does not say that on the card,” Lena’s not read it, but really? Kara wouldn’t do that.

    “There’s really nothing going on here?” Sam asks, with a curiosity that has Lena having a minor internal panic. Sam always has a knack for knowing when Lena’s catching feelings, not that she is, but if she were…well Sam would know and then she’d tell the rest of the house.

    Lena shakes her head, it’s not a lie. Not really. “Just tutoring the superstar player into actually graduating her way out of college.”

    “Admirable work,” Sam appears satisfied with her answer, for now. She takes the flowers out of the wrapping and begins arranging them. “They are really beautiful though, Kara’s got taste and a few dollars because these are not cheap. Not just another jock, is she?”

    “No, she’s not,” Lena says, her eyes focussed on the flowers, it really is a beautiful arrangement. Not that she wants to think too much into it, Kara probably just put in an order and the rest is all down to the florist’s choices.

    —

    The next time she sees Kara is a few days later, at the training centre. There’s no-one else around as Lena makes her way straight to the second floor, where the repeated thud of a ball on hardwood gets louder and is a sure sign, that Kara’s going to be late for their session.

    She steps towards the viewing gallery, looking down across the practice court, to find Kara is indeed otherwise occupied, bouncing a ball around like she isn’t supposed to be meeting up with Lena. She’s also not alone. 

    A film crew are set up around her and she’s currently being directed to move across the court and shoot. She does as she’s told, the basket swishes and then she does it again, and again.

    Lena’s so caught up in watching the ball, that she misses the moment Kara first catches sight of her. She doesn’t, however, miss the part where the blonde trips over her feet, her eyes still on Lena. A move which makes the rest of the room turn to see what has her distracted. Lena isn’t looking at them though, she only has eyes for Kara, who is now jogging towards a dark-haired woman on the side-lines. The two talk for a few seconds and then Kara’s waving up at Lena, gesturing that she’ll be five more minutes. Lena nods and heads towards the table, unpacking her things.

    The sound of thumping and squeaking sneakers finally stops and soon after Kara appears in the doorway, a towel slung around her neck, looking decidedly like she’s just finished an epic workout.

    Lena’s given up lying to herself, she’s attracted to Kara and all her assets. It’s all the worse when all those assets are on display, hot, sweaty, tempting.

    “Sorry, Meg’s a tough manager, has me working until I can’t shoot and I told her I need to shower, before I study, but if you don’t mind me being sweaty?”

    “You can shower,” Lena looks down at her books, hoping the heat climbing up her neck isn’t visible.

    “You’re sure?” Kara pauses in the doorway and Lena looks up then, finds herself inexplicably focused on the sliver of skin at Kara’s midriff, where her training vest has snagged. It’s definitely better if Kara washes and puts on more clothes.

    “Yep, go shower, I’ll get some questions ready.”

    “Great, I’ll be super quick.”

    And she is, she walks back in with damp hair and pink skin and she smells good. So good. Lena can’t help but inhale deeply.

    “What is that?”

    “Huh?” Kara looks around like she’s done something wrong, but something on Lena’s face must show it’s anything but. “New fragrance, you like?”

    “Yes,” Lena sees no harm in lying. It’s very Kara. Deep, musky. It’s also driving Lena’s senses wild.

    “Noted,” Kara says under her breath but Lena hears it, her eyes snap up to look at Kara, but all she sees is the tiniest of smile as she fixes herself a snack and then makes her way over to begin their session.

    —

    They’re wrapping up when she remembers to say, “thank you, for the flowers.”

    Kara stops what she’s doing then, almost shy as she asks, “you liked them?”

    “They’re beautiful.”

    “Yeah? I picked out the ones I thought, um,” she mashes her lips together. “They just seemed, very you.”

    “I love them,” she says, knowing Kara chose them herself is making her feel something, she can’t quite put a name to.

    “And you deserved them,” Kara looks delighted now. “I mean it, thank you for helping me.”

    “Well, I am getting paid,” Lena chuckles.

    “Mmhm, yep, but you’ve gone the extra mile for me. I know it’s not been easy and I’m hardly the model student or whatever, but yeah. I appreciate your help, so uh...”

    It’s kind of cute seeing Kara likes this. She’s unsure, a bit nervous. Lena likes it, she’s not even thinking when she reaches across the table and covers Kara’s hand, giving it a light squeeze. “I’m proud of you,” she says and Kara’s smile flashes wide.

    What is she doing? She’s not sure, but it feels good, so she figures there’s no harm.

    “Danvers?” The woman from before peeks her head around the door.

    “Oh, hey,” Kara scurries to her feet. “Meg, this is Lena.”

    “Nice to meet you,” Meg says, stepping into the room. “I’ve heard all about you,” her smile is friendly and Lena finds herself returning it. “Kara’s told me how important all this is and I promise I won’t get in your way, just needed to ask Kara to approve this last shot before I go.”

    “You want to see it?” Kara asks, once she’s had a quick look, not waiting for Lena to reply before flipping the iPad around for Lena to take a look. “Any good?”

    “It’s…” whatever is on Lena’s face, as she looks at an image of Kara, flying through the air, a ball in her hand, is apparently exactly what Meg had been hoping for.

    “That’s what we want,” Meg says, taking her iPad back and grinning at Lena. 

    “Huh?” Kara looks between the two women, apparently oblivious. Lena’s not filling in the blanks, thankfully Meg isn’t either.

    “I’ll send this one off and we’ll talk later,” she says, tapping her screen. “Hope to see you again, Lena,” she adds before closing the door behind her.

    “Sorry about all that,” Kara says, getting back to packing her things up.

    “It’s fine, Kara. I know you have other commitments. You looked good out there, do you like it?”

    “The filming and promo stuff?” She asks and Lena nods. “It’s not something I ever dreamed of doing, it’s kind of cool.”

    “Being famous?” Lena quirks an eyebrow, notes the way Kara grips tightly to the strap of her backpack.

    “I’m not famous,” she laughs and Lena thinks Kara might actually believe it. Lena scoffs. “I’m not,” Kara adds, a little more flustered.

    “Uh, huh, yeah,” Lena teases. “Nobody knows you and you are totally capable of walking outside this building and not having a fan come over asking for a photo or wanting you to sign something?”

    Kara laughs then, loud and true. “Okay, you got me. The world loves me. Everybody knows my name and when that ad goes out, it’s going to be so much worse.”

    Based on the image Lena’s just seen, it probably is, but she thinks Kara might like the fuss, “oh, because your ego needs more attention,” Lena jokes as they make their way out of the building. “Maybe we should commission your statue now?”

    “You know, that’s a really good idea. Get it done whilst I’m at my hottest,” Kara plays along, curling her arm, making a show of flexing her bicep and Lena’s a job to do, to fight the urge to wrap her fingers around the firm muscle and squeeze.

    They’re halfway across the quad when Lena realises she’s nowhere to be and has just been walking and chatting with Kara for no reason. It’s easy, these moments they keep sharing. She should probably think about why, sometime.

    Kara turns to look at her, “you want to get a coffee?”

    “I…” Lena realises where they are and it’d be so easy to walk inside with Kara, continue this conversation, dragging out more minutes, just them, but this is a world where everyone will look at Kara the second she walks in the door. “Not sure my mother will be too impressed if I get caught drinking coffee with you a second time,” she says, for want of any reason to stop herself.

    Kara’s brow scrunches, “what’s the issue with your mom?”

    “Another time?” Lena says, trying to show how tiresome the whole conversation would be. “Maybe after a few whiskeys…maybe even a whole bottle?”

    “I’ll hold you to that,” Kara says, she’s smiling at Lena in that easy way again.

    “Until next time,” Lena starts to walk away.

    “Tune in tomorrow?” Kara calls out to her.

    “Doubt I’ll have time,” she shouts, over her shoulder. She’ll find time. When she glances back one last time, she has a feeling Kara knows she’s going to, too.

    —

    Fall break creeps up on her. She’s back in Metropolis, having lunch with Lex and not looking forward to the coming days back at the mansion, when she gets a text from Kara. It’s nothing really, just a photo of a cute dog. She knows Kara is in the Cayman Islands, playing basketball, enjoying some sunshine and definitely not studying. There’s no reason for her text, or the ones that follow. A third notification has Lena sneaking another peak at her phone. She laughs at Kara’s comment, about how awful it is to be able to focus on the game and not take any tests, under a photo of her sitting with all her books, attempting to look studious. Lena’s about to call her out on the fact she can see the paused game film on the iPad on the table, when her brother coughs to get her attention.

    “What has you smiling like that?” Lex asks, taking a sip of champagne. He’s spent the last few minutes on a conference call with a new client and Lena hadn’t even realised he was done. “Or should I say, who?”

    “Jack,” she lies, flipping her phone over. “He’s giving running commentary on his Thanksgiving disaster. Did I tell you his parents have decided it’s the perfect time to learn to sail? Jack can’t even swim.”

    Lex laughs and Lena breathes a secret sigh of relief. Mentioning Kara would only lead to questions and talk of business that she could do without.

    She doesn’t look at her phone again until she’s back in their family home, alone in her room. There’s another photo from Kara, this time it’s of her warming up. She’s wearing a black vest, her toned arms glistening with sweat, her focus on something in the distance as she tosses a ball up in the air. Lena has to take a breath and wonder if Kara knows how good she looks? If Kara had intended it to be a thirst trap, it’s having the desired effect, not that Lena’s telling her. In fact she doesn’t reply, but she might look at the image a few times over the next few days.

    —

    Back on campus and in need of a detox after a few days with her family. Lena agrees to an evening out, without asking what the plan is, which is how she ends up at the bowling alley. The place is busy, loud and possibly smells worse than Kara’s gym. She’s softening the pain with weak beer, which is marginally more tolerable than the questionable whiskey sour she started with. 

    They’re getting setup to play, when a buzz of excitement catches the rest of her housemates’ attention. Lena’s too busy punching their team details into the console to notice what is happening, until Jack causally says, “your jock’s here.”

    It takes her a second to register what he’s said and when she follows his gaze, she’s met with the sight of Kara, and the rest of the Wildcats, making their way across the floor. Lena’s about to look away when Kara’s eyes snap to hers and there’s that little smirk.

    Lena’s not sure why it feels like her two worlds are colliding, or why it’s left her considering her options for a swift exit. It’s just different seeing Kara like this, outside of their usual environment, both surrounded by all their friends. 

    Maybe Kara feels it too, because after a nod of recognition, her focus turns to her teammates and Lena sighs with relief as her own game gets underway.

    —

    Not that her awkwardness, stops her looking over every so often. Unsurprisingly, Kara’s good at bowling and her performance has the rest of the wildcats chanting, “Danvers, Danvers!” as she takes her turn. Kara makes a show of it, lapping up the attention, bowling a clean strike, before turning back to them and punching her fist in the air.

    “Your turn, Luthor,” Jack thrusts a ball into her hands and she tries to remember the entire point of her coming along to this thing.

    She steps up to the line, draws her arm back, snaps the ball down the lane, strike. 

    Unlike Kara, she doesn’t jump, or pump her fists in the air. She just turns around and walks back to her seat with an air of nonchalance that her mother would be proud of.

    She sneaks a peek to where she knows Kara’s sitting and her breath hitches when she catches the way the blonde is watching her, jaw slack and eyes wide.

    Lena quirks an eyebrow in question and Kara snaps out of her daze, nodding slowly, a sure sign she’s impressed and if that makes Lena feel a little warm and fuzzy inside, well, as with most things Kara, she’s not telling a soul.

    —

    Jack appears with another pitcher of beer and Lena drains a glass, if only to get her thoughts to shut up for once and it kind of works. 

    The evening is fun. She’s with her friends, she’s got the top score and Jess has pointedly told her that she has to come out more often, at least five times.

    Lena’s considering it, if not just for moments where she catches Kara Danvers watching her. Not that Kara’s subtle about the looks she’s giving.

    At the start of their second game, Kara holds the ball a little longer between her palms, making sure Lena’s watching. She bowls a strike, Lucy high-fives her, but Kara’s looking at Lena, her head tilted to one side, a secret challenge? Lena’s more than up for it.

    “I didn’t know we were supposed to share social diaries,” Lena quips.

    “I didn’t know you had a social diary,” Kara laughs.

    “I’m exceptional at everything I put my mind to.”

Laughing, looking relaxed.

    It’s all too casual and makes Lena smile with interest.

    “Who, Jack?” She pretends to not catch on.

    She can’t resist the chance to stir a little, playfully rolling her eyes she says, “he did say he’d beat you up, if you weren’t a girl.”

    “For real?” Kara scoffs, she’s all incredulous and it’s kind of cute. Not that Lena finds her cute. She’s attractive, she’ll admit that much, but cute? Okay so maybe… “like to see him try, I’d—”

    “He’s not my boyfriend,” Lena cuts Kara off before things escalate beyond a gentle teasing.

    “Ex?” Kara asks, her brow lifting in question.

    “A friend, a very good one. I’m very much not attracted to him,” Lena holds Kara’s gaze, “or any man.”

    “Good to know,” Kara’s lips curl into a faint smile. 

    Lena could challenge her, tell her that she’s not interested in athletes, specifically ones with the name Kara, but Kara’s smile is kind of contagious and she has that strange feeling in her chest again.

    “Any other questions about Jack or my sexuality?”

    “Nope, think those will do for now,” she raises her hand, sticks up a thumb, “Lena is not with Jack,” then a finger, “she’s into women.”.

    “What about you?” Lena figures it’s time she got a direct answer herself. 

    “I thought it was obvious,” Kara says and then her eyes drop to Lena’s chest, albeit briefly. She looks hungry, it’s a look that makes Lena’s breath catch.

    “Amazing what a sprinkling of doubt can have the general public believing.”

    “Well, you aren’t the general public,” Kara edges a little closer.

    “No, I’m not.”

    “And I did kiss you, only one time, but I think you got the idea.”

    “You like girls?” Lena says and it surprises her just how breathless she sounds.

    “Some girls, more than others,” Kara swallows thickly, her eyes darting to Lena’s lips.

    She could kiss her right now, find out for a second time what it feels like to have Kara’s tongue in her mouth. It’s tempting, more than tempting and at the least it’d give her the chance to prove it was good the first time, not that Kara’s comments about it, are still on her mind or anything.

    A glass smashes behind the bar, startling them both and bringing Lena back to her senses. She takes a step back, notes the disappointed look on Kara’s face before she scowls at the bartender, who is cursing away to himself. 

    As if on cue, Lena’s phone starts ringing, Sam’s face filling the screen, “my rides here,” she says, avoiding Kara’s puppy dog eyes and knocking back the last of her scotch, “I’d better go, but thank you for the drink.”

    “Goodnight,” Kara says, Lena smiles at her, wonders why it feels so hard to walk away all of a sudden. She’s halfway to the exit when Kara calls after her, “Luthor?” She looks back in question, “this isn’t done.”

    “Hm?”

    “Think I deserve a chance to even the score.”

    “What if I want to revel in my victory forever? Luthor’s aren’t known for giving their competitors a chance of a comeback.”

    “Yeah, but you’re different, you believe in fairness and it’s only fair if we make a real game of this,” Kara sucks her lips between her teeth, waiting for Lena’s reply.

    She could walk away, never look back, but the idea of this game of theirs, not being over, appeals for a reason beyond her. “Until next time,” she says and Kara’s resulting smile is one she won’t forget easily.

    Chapter 8: circumstance

    Chapter Summary

    a loss, a rescue.

    Chapter Notes

    miss me?
    It's only been three weeks, but I know I've missed this. been away on a trip and took some time out from everything. This is me trying to get back into the swing of writing. I definitely need the break it gives from my everyday whirlwind of a brain!

    The days after Thanksgiving, slip by all too quickly. She sees Kara three times for tutoring, but the sessions are short, thanks to Kara’s current schedule and then there are the few moments when they cross paths around campus. Not that they really spend much time together, just a hello in passing, or a wave of acknowledgment across the quad. 

    It’s almost like the conversation at the bowling alley never happened. Lena would think it didn’t, that it was all in her head, if it weren’t for the moments when she catches Kara looking at her. Those brief glances, the small stutters, the nervousness that bubbles to the surface every single time they go their separate ways, is why Lena know it’s not just her feeling this thing, whatever it might be. 

    Even so, she keeps trying to convince herself, it’s all down to the circumstance they find themselves in and sure to fizzle out the second their lives go back to normal, if not sooner, because Kara’s focus will soon be on leading her team towards the NCAA champs and Lena has never had time for any distractions from her studies.

    It’s the reason why she’s going to ignore those soft smiles and easy laughs and it’s how she can pretend her body doesn’t tingle every time Kara’s fingers brush against hers. She can also lie to herself and say she’s only keeping an eye on Kara’s games because Jack’s love of basketball is contagious. It’s not because she misses seeing Kara, or that she actually enjoys watching the way she bosses the court or how mesmerising it is to see Kara in her element, playing the game she so clearly loves…it’s definitely none of those reasons. 

    She’s also not thinking much about how Jack doesn’t even ask her if she wants to watch anymore, just makes space on the couch and waits for Lena to join him.

    —-

    The Wildcats lose on a Saturday. 

    Lena’s as stunned as Jack as they sit watching the team trying to process what is happening. Cat Grant has never looked more terrifying in the moments leading towards the final buzzer and when Kara’s head drops, Lena knows it’s over. It wasn’t Kara’s best performance, something the commentators have made a point of, over and over. 

    “It’s not like Kara to miss those,” the woman is saying as they show a replay of an earlier shot and then a mistimed pass.

    “KD on a blip, could this be the slide? We saw it last season,” the co-commentator adds. “The Wildcats looked the real deal, but then lost steam and key players. Making a trip to the Final Four beyond anyone’s expectations, but this year surely can’t be a rep—”

    Lena hits mute, Jack stares at the screen, then turns to Lena, “not exactly the way to get in the mood for later,” he pushes himself up and off the couch.

    Lena had forgotten all about their plans, but she can’t think on them, not when the game still has her bothered. 

    “You know it wasn’t only Kara who was off out there,” she says, scrolling through the latest social media posts, to see what everyone else is saying. “Lucy Lane gave the ball away three times in the last quarter and I don’t know why she thought she should pass it towards Leslie when Nia was wide open. Can she not read the screens?”

    “Oh, Lena, honey,” Jack grins widely, “you are so a fan.” She scoffs, even if it is a little bit true. “And you’re also right, the whole thing was ridiculous, why would she do that?” Jack shakes his head. “Would have been the turning point.”

    “And the commentators blame Kara, like she’s the only player on the court,” Lena exits the app. Tired of all the useless comments she’s read.

    “Maybe she needs a Luthor in her corner? You’d definitely scare the commentators with one of those cold hard looks of yours,” Jack jokes, bumping into her side as they move towards the kitchen. Lena’s contemplating the idea as she pulls out a pan and fills it with pasta, whilst Jack rummages in the fridge, grabbing the ingredients for their go to pre-party dinner. Whilst they chop and cook, they chat about the game and Lena has more than a few words to say about the ridiculous expectations everyone seems to have on Kara, which makes Jack smile at her softly a few times, not that she’s going to read too much into whatever that means.

    —

    By the time they head to the party, the game is all but forgotten, at least amongst her housemates, who are already a few drinks ahead, mostly because Lena refused to taste test the punch and is now avoiding a round of tequila. It’s not that she’s not drinking, just she doesn’t want to drink anything that’s going to leave her curled up in a ball in her bedroom for the entirety of the following day, which she will not admit has anything to do with the fact she’s seeing Kara tomorrow at all. 

    The trouble with slipping out of the kitchen when the shots were being poured, is that now she’s stuck in the corner of a packed lounge, a sickly sweet concoction of a drink in her hand, whilst a boy, she’s spent the better part of fifteen months avoiding, is talking to her about why his family have a better plot on the island than the Luthor’s. 

    She’s bored, irritated and just as she’s thinking of a way to excuse herself from this conversation, Kara Danvers and the rest of the Wildcats make a surprise entrance. Much like the rest of the room, Lena’s eyes are drawn to the commotion instantly. 

    Kara is lingering quietly at the back of the group, Nia at her side. It’s Lucy who is making her presence known, alongside Leslie at the front of the pack. The pair happily letting everyone know they are in desperate need of a drink, whilst the other players smile and exchange a few words, even if the interactions are a little subdued after the shock loss. 

    Kara keeps her head down, shoulders slumped, looking like she’d rather not be there at all. A feeling and a look, Lena knows all too well. It’s obvious that the disappointment from earlier, is weighing heavy on her as she accepts a beer. 

    Lena’s forgotten that she’s blatantly staring, until the moment Kara looks right at her, her gaze not wavering as she lifts the bottle to her lips and drinks.

    Lena hasn’t heard much of what Maxwell Lord has been saying, even before she got distracted, but when his palm lands on her forearm, she tries to back away, “how about it? we get to work on building a partnership our families could be proud of,” he says and Lena looks at him in disgust, trying to free herself from his clammy hand. Max doesn’t release his grip, if anything it tightens and as Lena tries to yank herself free, her eyes meet Kara’s for a second time and the next thing she knows, Kara’s crossing the room.

    —

    “There you are, babe,” she says, moving beside Lena.

    “Don’t call me that,” Lena says in reply, uncomfortable with the term and the heat of being so close. Kara visibly baulks, but it’s in the cutest way. Like if Lena chastised her, she’d be all kinds of a fumbling dork.

    “Sorry,” she mutters, and oh? That is kind of cute. Lena’s drank too much, it’s all confusing and even more so when Kara throws her arm, around Lena’s shoulders, like that’s something they do?

    Lena doesn’t jerk away, she’s too startled by the easy gesture and the way she could so easily fall into Kara’s side. 

    She’s warm, soft, inviting. Lena inhales deeply, losing herself in Kara’s scent and the affection in her eyes.

    It’s when Maxwell coughs, that she catches on to what Kara is playing at and realises it’s in her best interest to go with it.

    “I was wondering where you got to,” Lena says, smiling up at Kara.

    “Got distracted by Leslie goading Mike into drinking the death shot.”

    “The death shot?”

    “Unfortunately, you are going to learn all about it soon enough,” Kara chuckles, her hand firm and warm on Lena’s skin.

    Lena’s not really sure what that means, or why she actually thinks this promise will come to fruition. A promise of spending more time together and that’s almost as confusing, as how good it feels to have Kara’s arm around her shoulders, holding her close. She remembers the reason for Kara’s closeness and turns to glare at Maxwell, who looks incredibly put out by the intrusion.

    “Who’s your buddy?” Kara gives Max her usual charming smile, but Lena can feel how tense her body is. 

    “Max Lord,” he sticks out his hand, Kara purses her lips as she looks at it, not letting go of Lena and simply raising her beer bottle towards him.

    “From where I was stood, looked like you needed to keep your hands to yourself,” she says with a coolness that has Max taking a step backwards.

    Lena has to bite back a smile. It’s not often that anyone can make Maxwell Lord look threatened and it’s what makes her lean into Kara’s touch, resting her hand on the firm muscles of her abdomen. Kara twitches, just the slightest, in response and when Lena looks up at her, it’s less affection and more…well something else. Kara wets her lips and an ache, Lena’s desperately been trying to ignore, resurfaces. She probably needs to put some space between them, but not when Max still has something to say.

    “Unlucky today, Danvers. Looks like the rumours are true, you’ve got too many distractions off court these days,” Max says with a condescending sneer that has Lena instantly rounding on him and letting all her earlier frustrations spill out without a second thought. 

    “Kara isn’t the only player on the team, if you paid attention, you’d notice.”

    “I noticed the way she couldn’t hit a three pointer in the fourth.”

     “She was trying to create plays,” Lena defends, irked at the way he’s looking at them both. “It’s too easy to critique her game, there were countless errors from—” she stops at Kara’s tightened grip and when she glances at her in question, she’s surprised to see Kara shake her head.

    “Guys like Max here, they don’t listen. The only opinion they have, is the one the media feeds them.”

    Max scoffs, “I can see exactly what your problem is.” He’s giving Lena this strange once over, which is definitely contrary to the way he had been attempting to hit on her only minutes before.

    “You know how it is, Luthor’s always like to cause trouble,” she raises an eyebrow at him, watches him shrink further under her cold stare. It’s quite satisfying, if she’s honest. To be able to make a small man like Max Lord find himself lost for a comeback. It possibly helps that Lex stole a lucrative deal from right under his grandfather’s nose only last summer, but either way, it’s good to know, the whole family aren’t over it. “Now, if you’ll excuse us?” Lena turns her back to him, not waiting for an answer.

    Her eyes are on Kara, who watches Max walk away, then looks down at Lena with a soft smile, “remind me never to get on the wrong side of you.”

    “Definitely wouldn’t recommend it,” Lena says, taking a proper look at Kara now. She looks pretty with her hair in loose waves, a little mascara making her blue eyes seem impossibly more so and her outfit choice, of a sleeveless pale blue crop top, over navy track pants, is simple, understated and still ridiculously hot. Lena tries not to linger too long, but the way Kara is smiling at her, says she’s failed. “You’ve seen what I’m like when I get angry,” she says not really thinking about what she’s saying, until she notes the way Kara swallows, edging temptingly closer. 

    The action sparks a memory of what happened the last time she was angry with Kara. A kiss so good, she’s not been able to stop thinking about it and right now, she knows she could do it all again, but this is a party, they’re surrounded by people, some with their phones in hand, probably already snapping photos of the star player. Lena can’t risk even thinking about being so careless and quickly takes a step out of Kara’s hold.

    Kara frowns, momentarily, but then her easy smile is back.

    “Take it, you two have history?’ Kara nods to where Max is now chatting with his friends. The group keep looking over, obviously talking about her and it puts Lena even more on edge.

    “Only in business,” she tries to put another step between her and Kara, but is hindered by the existence of a wall. “Why did you step in?” She asks, a little flustered as she imagines the fallout of Lillian hearing anything Maxwell Lord may have to say.

    “Because you looked like you were two minutes away from chucking your drink in his face, or worse,” Kara laughs. When Lena keeps staring at her blankly, she sighs. “He put his hands on you,” she says, like that’s explanation enough and at Lena’s continued silence she shrugs. “And because we’re friends. It’s what friends do. They help when they see the other one needs it. I’d have done the same even if we weren’t friends.”

    “We’re friends?” Lena questions and perhaps it sounds a little too disbelieving as Kara looks at her in wonder.

    “Yes,” Kara says after a pause. Like it’s the most obvious thing. “You ate my pizza, it’s pretty much a lifelong commitment between us now.” Lena’s still struck by the easy assertion as Kara laughs lowly. “And friends, get friends a drink after they’ve rescued them from the creepiest guy on campus, you coming?”

    Kara nods towards the kitchen and Lena figures she may as well just go with it for once.

    —

    “I’d avoid the punch,” she tells Kara, who had been eyeing it curiously. “Jack had a hand in the mixing, it’s the kind of drink that is probably going to strip your stomach lining.”

    Kara pulls a face and inspects the bucket of ice, fishing out two bottles and passing one to Lena, “beer it is then.”

    “I didn’t expect to see you out tonight,” Lena says and Kara gives a glum shrug.

    “The others needed a few hours away from it all.”

    “And what do you need?” 

    It’s an innocent question, but Kara picks something else from it, her eyes go wide and then she slowly gives Lena a once over. A grin appears, it’s smug, followed by a twitch of her eyebrows, “you offering?”

    Lena bats at her arm, unable to hide her own smile, (which she will definitely blame on the alcohol and not the way being around Kara makes her feel all warm and fuzzy.) “seriously? You lose and all you can think about is sex?”

    “Helps burn off restless energy,” Kara says and Lena’s an idiot because she starts to think about how much energy Kara might have to burn? It does not help that Kara is now looking at her, very much like she’s having a similar thought. It’s another acknowledgment of the attraction that simmers between them, not that Lena can process it in the moment.

    “And you said your reputation was all talk,” Lena teases, trying to stop this from going any further, considering where they are.

    “It is,” Kara protests, but her cheeks are pinking and Lena does not want to think too hard about the rumours about Kara Danvers on tour. “Sorry,” she says, a little softer. “You did ask though.”

    “I did,” Lena laughs, rolling her eyes at herself. “But it’s not quite what I meant.”

    Kara’s fighting back a smile now and doing a terrible job of it, “shame,” she says quietly, but Lena hears it. She drops her gaze to the bottle of beer in her hand, but doesn’t get a chance to say anything in return, before Kara answers the intended question. “I would’ve been happy with pizza and Netflix, but we win together, we lose together. So here I am,” she shrugs. “Turns out, it’s exactly where I want to be.”

    Lena’s eyes snap up then, caught in Kara’s last comment. How she has this effect on her, with just a few words is causing her brain to malfunction and her heart to flutter. This feeling, is one she can’t blame on the alcohol, even as she takes a slow drink of her beer, her eyes not leaving Kara’s.

    —

    “Lena!” Sam breaks the moment when she bashes into the doorway, cursing under her breath at the impact. “I lost Jack.”

    Sam obviously hasn’t looked very hard, because Lena can see Jack sitting by the fire pit. She points him out and earns a giggle from Sam in reply. “How much punch have you had?”

    “Just two cups,” Sam slinks past them, helping herself to another. “Why are you drinking beer?” Sam eyes the bottle in Lena’s hand and then her eyes land on Kara. “Oh, wow, Kara Danvers.”

    “Nice to meet you,” Kara offers her usual warm smile, but her eyes flick to Lena in question.

    “This is Sam, she’s drank more than two cups of punch.”

    “Have not,” Sam protests, but looks incredibly guilty. “And it’s nice to finally meet you too,” she addresses Kara. “Why Lena’s been keeping you under wraps is—”
