    “Sam,” Lena grits out her name, she’s an idea where this conversation is going. “It’s not like you met any of my other tutoring assignments.”

    “Nope, but they also didn’t buy you flowers,” Sam says and Kara’s cheeks pink, almost as much as Lena’s. “You two are cute,” Sam chuckles, knocking back her now refilled cup.

    “How about we get you some air and see how Jack is?” Lena says, hooking her arm in Sam’s and turning her to face the backdoor. “Thank you for the rescue,” she says to Kara.

    “Anytime,” Kara tips her beer in reply. “Oh, and Lena?” Kara calls out, just as she gets to the door. “Thanks for sticking up for me, good to know you were watching…again,” there’s a small knowing smile on Kara’s face.

    Lena rolls her eyes, but laughs, she can’t deny it now anyway, “I meant it, some of the others need to up their game.”

    “Don’t worry, Coach already chewed them out, we’ll be back stronger next week,” Kara says.

    “I don’t doubt it,” Lena means it too. She’s learnt enough about Kara, these last few weeks, to know she’d put in all the work needed. 

    “Cute,” Sam mutters, reminding Lena of her presence and the need to get her best friend outside.

    “Air,” Lena tries to get the door open.

    Sam stumbles on her way out and it’s an effort for Lena to steady her, “I think I need more punch,” Sam says.

    “You definitely do not,” Lena swipes the cup from her hand and hears Kara’s gentle laughter in the background as the door closes behind her. 

    Lena doesn’t catch sight of Kara again that night, mainly thanks to Sam’s inability to hold down a fourth cup of punch.

    Chapter End Notes

    sitting on a plane, scrolling through all the tv and film offerings, noting how little queer content there is out there and heading straight back to fanfic...
    Hoping for some swift progress with this one...fingers crossed.

    Chapter 9: questionable decisions

    Chapter Summary

    Lena makes up her mind, then changes it.

    Chapter Notes

    There is no debilitating hangover the next morning, just a dry mouth and a craving for a couple hours more sleep. Lena shirks off both with a quick workout and a breakfast smoothie. She watches Kara’s post-game interview; thinks about the things she said only last night and considers the idea of asking Kara out. Which genuinely surprises her at first, but then she realises she’s only kidding herself with this whole keeping her distance charade. She likes Kara and it’s not in a friendly way.

    Lena’s good at wrestling with her own self-doubt though and spends far too much time battling with the complexity of dating someone she’s tutoring, talking herself out of it, a hundred times over. 

    Maybe she’s scared? It’s not an emotion she’s familiar with. She’s never been afraid to chase something she wants, but with Kara? It’s all different. She can’t explain it, has no words that quite fit.

    Kara just makes her feel and Lena’s really not sure what she’s supposed to do about it.

    Even if she did ask Kara out, it would be complicated. Kara’s popularity, makes even a quiet cup of coffee an event that’s bound to make its way onto Instagram and Lena really does not want a light shining on her dating life, no matter how much she likes Kara.

    Torn between what she wants to happen and how she’s still trying to protect her privacy, she decides to put any thoughts of putting her heart on the line, until after the Christmas break. Giving herself a chance to have a bit of breathing space and an opportunity to see if her earlier thought about it all fizzling out, could be accurate.

    If after a few weeks, she still feels the same, then she’ll bite the bullet and ask Kara out. 

    Until then, nothing has to change.

    —

    Her decision made, she makes her way into the training centre, feeling like a weight is lifted. She takes the steps up to the athletes’ study suite, whilst the now familiar sound of balls bouncing on the hardwood, echoes down the corridor. 

    Fixing herself a drink, seeking the warmth for her fingers as she wraps the mug in both hands, she moves closer to the viewing gallery, giving herself a chance to watch, without being seen by anyone down below.

    From her spot, she can see how hard Cat is working the team. Yelling at them, critiquing every play. The coach’s mood has definitely not improved overnight and it’s Lucy who is facing the brunt of it, constantly being pulled aside and berated. After her performance the day before, Lena can understand it. 

    Kara is at the other end of the court and unlike the others, she’s not just going through the motions, but focussed and working through every play, competing for every ball. Leslie throws her hands in the air, but Kara steps to the side, puts the ball up and goes again. Relentless, as practice continues.

    —

    It’s easy to get lost in watching; the thumping of the ball, the squeak of shoes, the never-ending comments from the coaching staff, until her phone ringing startles her and the sight of her mother’s face on the screen, quickly sours her mood.

    She takes a breath and answers the call, already dreading the fallout from whatever Maxwell Lord may have been stirring up.

    Thankfully, rumours and whispers haven’t spread in the space of a few hours and Lillian is only interested in finalising Lena’s Christmas plans.

    “Lex will collect you,” Lillian says once she’s gone over Lena’s schedule. “I’m tied up with a business matter and won’t be back until after 6pm, but could you try and make sure the staff have everything set? I don’t need a repeat performance of the Easter chaos.”

    “Of course, anything else?”

    “I mean it Lena, there can’t be any distractions, not like—”

    “I’ll give it my full attention,” she quickly cuts her mother off, before she can list all Lena’s recent failings.

    “Unlike your studies,” Lillian says, pointedly. “Have you reconsidered this silly notion of yours, yet?” she pauses, waiting for Lena to answer. The silence drags on and Lillian huffs, “I know you like to help the less privileged, but that’s something we can do through donations. Wasting your time tutoring a basketball player isn’t exactly anonymous. Persephone Lord wonders why you’d need the money?” Lena’s stomach sinks at the mention of Max’s mom. Rumors always travel fast between the matriarchs of Metropolis and it’s surely only a matter of time before Lillian hears some twisted version of what went on last night, Max is not the kind to take rejection lightly. 

    “It’s not about the money,” Lena says for the hundredth time.

    “I know,” Lillian’s tone is resigned. “I had perhaps hoped you were trying to fund a secret drug habit?”

    “I’m not taking drugs,” she says, praying it brings an end to this conversation.

    “Shame,” Lillian says dryly. “Rehab is far more socially acceptable these days.”

    Lena’s not surprised her mother would take this view. Having a child in recovery would give her something new to talk about at the country club, ridiculous as it might sound to anyone who doesn’t know what it’s like to be a Luthor. The whole conversation is a timely reminder of what she’s about to face over the next few weeks and she tiredly runs her fingers through her hair, wishing this wasn’t her life right now. 

    “Sorry to disappoint you.”

    “No need to be flippant,” Lillian scolds her, making Lena wonder, if she can ever win. “You’d do well to listen to me, but if you must make your own mistakes, don’t say I didn’t warn you. Leave the sport stars to Lex, Miss Danvers may seem like a hot prospect, but these players are all the same, leaving a trail of destruction in every city they visit. You don’t need your name associated with a nobody out of Midvale.”

    Lena’s has a sudden panic then at the mention of Kara’s hometown. Unsure why Lillian would know, she asks, “have you had her investigated?”

    “Do I need to?” Lillian’s reply has Lena closing her eyes tight. She could do without this side of her mother right now.

    “I made a commitment. You understand how important it is, that I honour it?” Her mother hums, which is as close to acceptance as Lena is probably going to get. “I walk away now, there will be questions and from what I’ve heard, Coach Grant has influence, even in Metropolis.”

    “She might like to think she does,” Lillian scoffs, but Lena knows it’s true. Sport is big business, whether her mother likes it or not. “Fine, do as you will. Your brother seems to think it’s all for the greater good.” Lena sighs at the reminder of Lex’s agenda, which is a whole other problem she is not getting into right now. “Do try to keep, whatever else it is you do, off the social media platforms? I don’t need to fend off these kinds of questions at the next gala. Do you understand?”

    “I understand,” she says, earning another hum of satisfaction from her mother.

    “Then I’ll see you in a few days,” Lillian ends the call.

    Lena lets out a frustrated growl in response and at that exact moment, Kara walks in the room.

    —

    “You okay?” Kara asks, it’s well-meaning of course, but she’s stepped straight into the line of fire. Something she appears to realise, the second Lena looks at her.

    “Do I look okay?” Lena waltzes towards her chair, snatching at the back of it, sitting down with little grace, before raising a single brow at Kara. “Can we start?”

    “Sure,” Kara doesn’t even flinch, dropping her bag to the floor. then slumping down in the seat opposite, her eyes on Lena the entire time. 

    Lena taps her pen against her pad, frustration oozing out of every pore, “maybe get your notes?”

    Kara folds her arms across her chest, still eyeing Lena curiously, “was that your mom? Sounded kind of—”

    “Do you make a habit of listening into private conversations?” Lena snaps, angry at everything her mother had to say. Annoyed with herself for not setting boundaries in this arrangement, frustrated that she cares about Kara and what her mother thinks only stings all the more.

    “Only if the gossip is good, or you know…” she shrugs, “if they’re talking about me.” Kara says drily. Lena ignores her, opening the file she’s prepared for them to work through. 

    Kara gives in and gets out her own notes. 

    —

    Twenty minutes of frosty exchanges is about Kara’s limit it seems and after another curt instruction from Lena, she places her palms flat on the desk, “let’s take a break,” she says, pushing herself to her feet and not waiting for Lena to agree.

    Lena huffs and twists her pen in her hands, whilst Kara fixes herself a drink in the nearby kitchen. When she returns, she places a cup down in front of Lena.

    “Tea?” Lena looks up in surprise.

    “You think I live off those energy drinks?” Kara winks. Lena still feels baffled.

    She takes a sip, it’s perfect. Kara’s pretending to not be watching her, but Lena can feel it. “Thank you,” she says softly.

    “No problem,” Kara’s smile has Lena’s chest doing that weird fluttery thing all over again and she tries to wash it down with another sip.

    Kara doesn’t ask any more questions and it turns into a productive study session. For half an hour, she forgets all about how annoying Lillian Luthor is, until an email notification flashes up on her screen. 

    Subject: Itinerary.

    “My mother is difficult,” Lena confesses as she glances over her detailed Christmas schedule. “She made another point of how unhappy she is about me tutoring.”

    “What’s wrong with tutoring?”

    “I don’t need the distraction and I don’t need the money,” Lena can hear Lillian’s distain at people thinking she needs to fund her way through college. “And I don’t need to be associated with…” she doesn’t need to repeat what her mother said, she sees the moment Kara works it out.

    “With basketball players or me in particular?”

    Lena’s not sure, if she’s honest and still has a question mark over whether Lillian has a file on Kara stashed somewhere in her office. Either way, Kara is definitely a huge distraction and that’s the problem her mother really has. “Your popularity hasn’t bled into my mother’s circles I’m afraid,” Lena deadpans.

    “Only a matter of time. Sure all those rich moms of Metropolis will be fawning over me the second I destroy MetU next month. They always fall for the Danvers charm,” Kara says with an assured confidence.

    “You think a lot of yourself,” Lena’s not in the mood for hearing how amazing Kara is or how many mom’s, apparently throw themselves at her. 

    “I was joking,” Kara says needlessly. 

    Lena takes a breath, meets her eye, “sorry, this is definitely all me.” Kara nods in agreement. “Five minutes talking to my mom and I turn into a brat.”

    “Bratty, Lena,” Kara teases, not fazed by Lena’s mood swings.

    “Oh, you wish,” Lena gets up and rinses out her cup. She doesn’t see the way Kara tracks her every movement, or the nod of her head. “Thanks for the tea,” she says when she’s done. “And for listening.”

    “Always,” Kara’s smile is genuine. It’s all too easy and inviting and Lena really is on the precipice of telling her she likes her and asking if maybe this is something Kara might want to explore? Being vulnerable is not her strong point, so instead she gives a closed mouth smile and packs up her things.

    —

    She takes one step outside and runs back under cover.

    “What the heck,” Kara’s beside her, looking up at the sky in stunned surprise. Dark swirling clouds loom overhead and those few drops that Lena had tried to run from, have now turned into a torrential downpour.

    “You think the gods are angry with us or something?” Lena shivers under the shelter. There had been rain forecast, but nothing to prepare her for this. Her coat is hanging in its place in her wardrobe, which is of no use to her right now.

    “Here,” Kara’s delving into her kitbag, pulling something out and handing it to Lena. “Put this on and then we can run for it.”

    Lena takes the offering, pulls it over her head without question and sinks into the warmth of the thick hoodie. It’s too big for her, it smells like Kara, it’s perfect. “Thank you, not that it’ll keep me dry in this,” she looks back out across the tarmac where the rain is hammering down, leaving a stream of water rushing down the sidewalk.

    “Golf cart!” Kara says like it’s the best idea she’s ever had. She’s already jogging towards the back of the centre before Lena can think to stop her.

    She returns a minute later, behind the wheel of the NCU buggy, a huge grin on her face, that Lena is definitely mirroring as she jumps in and lets Kara drive her home.

    “Thank you,” Lena says as they come to a stop, it’s still raining, but at least she’s not as cold as she would have been, thanks to Kara’s ridiculously soft and comfy hoody. She reluctantly tries to take it off, but Kara quickly bats her hand away.

    “Keep it, it looks good on you and you still need to get to the door.”

    Lena looks out at the rain and then at Kara, whose hair is flat and shoulder’s damp from where she ran for the golf cart. “If you want to come in, I can find something dry for you to put on and fix you a coffee?”

    Kara puffs out her cheeks as she checks the time, “I’d love to, but if I don’t get this back on campus and shift myself into the gym, I get fined.” Lena’s look of alarm has Kara laughing. “Seriously, forfeits, fines. We’re all on thin ice, after the loss, as it is and I may not be as allowed to borrow this cart as I made out,” she says in almost a whisper.

    Lena shakes her head, unsurprised at the last revelation.

    “Then I’ll let you go,” Lena says, even as she lingers in the cart, not quite ready to say goodbye, which seems to be becoming a common occurrence. Kara’s smiling at her like maybe she feels the same way.

    “I’d really much rather get coffee with you,” she says, soft, taking a deep inhale of breath and then letting it go. Lena feels it in her chest, a new kind of ache at having to separate, especially when it’s the last time they’ll do this, this semester.

    “Can’t have everyone blaming me if this losing streak continues,” she jokes, biting down lightly on her bottom lip. The way Kara tracks the motion is making her pulse quicken, but then blue eyes snap up to meet hers. 

    “I really don’t like losing,” Kara says, lowly. Something else hidden beneath those words, making Lena feel hot all over.

    “Next game, for sure,” Lena say without doubt.

    “Now that is a challenge,” Kara laughs again and at Lena’s blank look, she shakes her head. “You really don’t follow at all? Huge game Friday? We play the undefeated Angels?”

    “Don’t tell me, fierce rivals?”

    “Exactly,” Kara grins. “They’ve never beaten us here and I’m not ready for that stat to be undone.”

    Lena picks up her bag and slides out of the cart, “good luck, not that I think you need it.”

    Kara’s face lights up at Lena’s confidence, but then her eyes soften, “I hope your day gets better, or at least that your mom doesn’t give you any more trouble.”

    “I hope so, too,” Lena hesitates on the sidewalk. “Thanks again, for listening.”

    Kara starts the cart back up, “I meant it, anytime.”

    The rain is still falling as Lena darts towards her front door, holding her bag over her head, not that it’s much use. She turns around just as she gets the door open, sees the way Kara’s watching her and offers a wave goodbye before rushing inside and out of the rain.

    The idea that whatever she’s feeling is going to fizzle out over break, seems more and more unlikely.

    Chapter End Notes

    a speedy update? very excited to be back writing this, so hopefully I can keep it up.
    Think Lena might need to go to an actual game soon, don't you? maybe before they go home for the holidays...

    Chapter 10: on the court

    Chapter Summary

    it tastes different.

    Chapter Notes

    Not comfortable having to do this, but a brief pause to address the issue with the person, who seems to enjoy hating on so many stories and keeps coming back to this one to shout their frustrations about things I'm not writing about: I do read the comments. I did answer a couple of times earlier on in this fic. I won't bother with them ever again, they have an agenda and should really write their own works to get what they want. it's such a shame to have this anger directed into the fandom all the time and sad to think of the impact they might have on other writers.
    unfortunately there is no way to stop them aside from not allowing guest comments and I choose to have these as I know not everyone is comfortable having an account on here.
    If you see any more of these comments on my work, just ignore. I plan to.

    To everyone else: thanks for the support. I'll keep writing my own stories in my own way, for as long as I can.
    I try to reply to comments, but sometimes other things get in my head. always know I appreciate them and it's always good to know there are people reading along and hopefully enjoying.

    Moving on...Let's get back to some supercorp.

    With tutoring wrapped for the semester and no official reason to see Kara again, Lena’s wondering if it’d be too weird if she showed up at Kara’s place to return the hoody?

    It’s an excuse. A good one. It’s probably also incredibly obvious that she has ulterior motives and knowing her luck, Lucy Lane would open the door, snatch it from her hands and the entire premise of her trip would be wasted.

    Stuck on how to fulfil her need to see Kara, before she leaves for Metropolis, she’s gifted a solution thanks to her brother, of all people.

    “You need to go to the game, Lena,” Lex tells her over the phone on a grey Thursday afternoon. “I put our name on that building for a reason. It makes us look good to be associated with a team like the Wildcats, it looks even better if they see your face on the side-lines.”

    Lena’s heard this before and somehow, she’s always found a way out of it. Mostly because what Lex wants, hasn’t aligned with Lillian’s views of what Lena should or should not be doing at NCU.

    Lex isn’t really asking though. There’s a pressure that comes with being a Luthor; social obligations, public appearances, missing out on a planned evening of study or a night of binge watching the latest series of trash tv. 

    For once, she actually wants the excuse to attend one of Kara’s games, not that she’s telling Lex.

    “I’m not exactly a fan of sports,” she says, not wanting to concede too easily because that would definitely lead to a thousand questions.

    “I don’t want you there to cheer and whoop. It’s business. You sit, you represent.”

    She can practically hear Kara saying, “come watch, what’s the worst that can happen?”

    The worst, is she might like it. She might get lost in watching Kara Danvers take charge on the court, fuelling the fire that’s been burning deep within her. 

    Seeing Kara excel at the thing she’s truly good at, live and up close, is a somewhat terrifying prospect.

    “I can probably go after break,” she says with an air of nonchalance that belies the way her pulse is racing.

    “Tomorrow night,” Lex says, clearly irritated by her evasive answer. “It’s the biggest game of the season, make sure you’re there,” his order given, he ends the call.

    Lena has a choice. She knows this.

    She could ignore Lex, sit at home, alone.

    Or she could scratch her own itch and show her face.

    —

    Friday morning is like any other. Lena gets out of bed, goes for a run, drinks a green smoothie and looks over her revision notes. Time passes, she takes a break for lunch, drinks coffee and is left tapping her pencil on a textbook, trying to kid herself into thinking this is how her day is going to play out.

    Her phone screen lights up, a message from Sam asking if she wants to join them for pregame drinks. 

    Lena puts her books away, walks to her closet and makes a decision.

    The sound of the Wildcat chant hits her, the second she pushes her bedroom door open. 

    She makes her way to the kitchen, where her three housemates are gathered around the island, Jack banging his bottle of beer on the table top as he sings louder than the others. He’s wearing his lucky jersey and has his face painted, blue and red.

    The moment he catches sight of Lena, he stops singing, his jaw dropping comically slowly.

    “What time do we leave?” She says when all eyes turn to look at her. She’s wearing Kara’s hoody, it has a huge Wildcat emblem in the centre of it. It may also still smell like Kara, she doubts anyone else would know.

    She also has her hair tied back in a tight ponytail and a determined look on her face as her friends continue to gawp.

    It takes a shove from Sam to wake Jack back up, “right, love, yes, five minutes?”

    “Time for one of these then,” she picks up one of the beers and drains it. In need of some kind of intoxication to help her get through this.

    —

    “You’re really coming to a game?” Sam says in mild disbelief, as they walk towards the arena.

    Lena looks at her, sees the curious surprise. “Lex insisted,” she says in way of explanation. Sam doesn’t need to know that he’s been insisting Lena do things for most of her life and she’s not complied. This is the simplest explanation and it helps her to ignore the nervous energy that is making her fingers tingle.

    “Thank you, Lex Luthor,” Sam grins, linking her arm with Lena’s and tugging her forwards. Lena wonders if it’s partly because she thinks Lena might change her mind, either way, Sam doesn’t let her go until they’re inside and making their way court-side.

    She’s hit by a wall of noise and stunned to see so many people already in their seats, as they follow Jack and Jess. The band is playing, the cheer squad already pepping up the crowd and everyone else is talking excitedly as the hum of expectation crackles in the air.

    Jack’s complaining about how they’ve missed the warm up, but the second the lights dip, he’s on his feet, chanting along with the rest of the arena.

    Lena stays seated, watching the players tunnel as the team run out. Until she catches sight of the person she’s here to see.

    Kara runs in, bouncing a ball as she goes, then tosses it, to one of the team handlers. She pulls off her practice shirt, takes a seat and waits to be introduced alongside her teammates. She looks good in her jersey, Lena knows this, but it hits different than on the screen in her lounge. 

    This is Kara Danvers on court and in the flesh. Her toned arms, glistening in the low lights. 

    The second her name is called, the crowd roars and Kara gets to her feet, taps hands with Lucy and Nia then moves into the team huddle.

    —

    They tip off and Lena knows, within one play, that showing her face was a bad idea. 

    Being attracted to Kara, growing to like her, thinking maybe she might be bold enough to ask her out at some point in the future, is one thing. Witnessing her on-court persona up close, is another.

    Off court, Kara is funny, likeable, thoughtful. She’s listened when Lena’s needed her, shown up as a friend, cared and it’s all those little things that have Lena’s lips curling into a smile every time she thinks of her.

    The person she’s been getting to know, is very different to how Kara Danvers acts in front of the camera and nothing like how she is once she has a basketball in hand. 

    On court, Kara’s destructive, impressive, commanding and Lena can’t stop watching her every move. She wants her. Wants to be destroyed by her. The thought alone has Lena feeling all flushed from head to toe, which isn’t ideal when she’s in a packed arena, Sam asking her a question, she can’t decipher, thanks to her laser focus on Kara Danvers.

    Everything before this has been easy to rationalise. Her thoughts were fine, excused as curiosity with a dashing of longing, but to see Kara like this? In her element, moving a ball around the court and outfoxing the opposition; it’s quite simply, exhilarating. Kara clearly loves the game she plays and Lena’s fucked.

    —

    The first quarter is strewn with errors. Poor defence and missed shots that lead them coming in 17-11 behind. Kara is not happy, Cat is yelling. Sam is complaining loudly and Jack is gripping Jess’ hand tightly as they rip apart the last play.

    Lena watches in rapt silence, whilst Cat’s outburst continues. She’s not seen Kara berated like this, notes the impassive look on her face as she’s yelled at. Lena’s half a mind to cross the court and tell Cat to stop. Kara’s working hard, it’s just not clicking and when the rest of the team are determinedly putting in a lacklustre performance, it seems unfair to target Kara in this way. Not that Kara seems fazed. She nods, stands, put’s her closed fist into the air, alongside those of the other players and then they’re back on the court.

    It’s two minutes into the second quarter when Kara sees her. She’s just been knocked to the ground by an aggressive guard and is being picked off the floor, by Leslie and Nia, when their eyes lock. Kara falters as she gets to her feet and only looks away when Lucy tugs at her arm. She moves to the free-throw line. Takes a breath, puts the ball up and misses. Lena hears the gasp beside her and some rumblings of concern from her friends.

    Kara turns around, slaps Lucy’s offered palm and moves back to her mark for the second shot. This time she looks over to where Lena’s sitting, takes a breath, returns her focus to the backboard and throws the ball up. It takes an age to come back down, when it does, it’s accompanied by the beautiful swish of the net and the crowd erupts.

    Lena’s on her feet, her fist clenched as she pumps it into the air. Kara’s running backwards, moving into defence, she looks right at Lena, raises her hand and points.  

    It’s fast paced for the next few minutes, almost as fast as Lena’s heart is racing.

    —

    Turns out that was just Kara getting warmed up. She dominates for the rest of the game, scoring 12 points in the third alone, tearing the other team apart, making assists, blocks, steals and screens, in what Jack repeatedly calls, a Kara Danvers masterclass. 

    When the Wildcats walk out eventual winners, Lena’s never felt more exhilarated and the image of Kara turning to the crowd and roaring, after she’d scored a buzzer beating three to tie the scores in the second, is imprinted on Lena’s brain as they exit the arena. 

    She’s hot all over, craves more and has no idea what to do about it.

    “We go to Al’s,” Jack takes her by the hand, seemingly able to read the lost look on Lena’s face. “Big win, big party.”

    Lena can’t argue, she’s caught up in the fever, even as they push their way into a packed bar and fight their way to the front.

    “You loved it?” Jack says, his beer sloshing out of his glass as he tries to smash it against Lena’s.

    “It was fun,” she admits. The noise, the crowd, the whole experience, but mainly the sight of Kara Danvers on a court, doing the thing Kara Danvers does. After witnessing it first hand, Lena can almost forgive her for not putting the work into her studies. Almost.

    “They sometimes come here after,” Jack adds and from that moment on, Lena’s got one eye on the door.

    —

    The crowd’s thinned out a little by the time the team arrives, but it still causes a stir amongst the patrons. Lena spots Kara the second she walks in, high-fiving a few people but not paying too much attention to her surroundings. She’s wearing, grey jeans and a navy button down, over a white crop top. Her midriff is on display and it’s quickly becoming Lena’s biggest weakness, more so thanks to the cheap beer swirling in her stomach. Kara still has her hair tied back, but she’s taken out her contacts and is wearing glasses. Lena’s slightly fixated by this change to her appearance and can’t stop looking. 

    Like she knows she’s been watched, Kara’s head turns and she searches out the crowd until her eyes lock with Lena’s. The world keeps moving around them, but Lena wouldn’t know. All she knows is the way Kara’s looking at her and she’s never felt more seen. Her body feels warm, in the most pleasant way, her throat dry, her breath short.

    The stare off is broken, when Kara’s barged into by Nia Nal, a drink shoved in her hand and a few words, exchanged. Kara leans in, says something in Nia’s ear and then a second pair of eyes are on Lena. Recognition flashes across the other player’s face, before she gives Kara a telling smile and starts to drag the other players away with her.

    Kara moves to cross the room, but that won’t do, not for Lena. Sam is right beside her, oblivious to what’s happening outside of her orbit and Lena would like to keep it that way. Making a snap decision, she turns and walks away from her friends and towards the pool table.

    “You came?” Kara’s a mix of awe and something else as she catches up.

    “Heard a rumour, it was a night I couldn’t miss.”

    Kara takes a second to speak, too busy checking Lena out, “you look good in my clothes.”

    Lena looks down, remembers just what she’s wearing and has to fight back a blush, “I’m all for fitting in.”

    “You always stand out,” Kara says breathlessly. For a second they just stand there, probably both with similar looks on their faces, neither knowing just what to say. Kara swallows, shakes her head and finally finds her words. “What did you think?”

    “You played well,” Lena gives a coy assessment, but it still brightens up Kara’s whole face. “And you were right, definitely something to experience court-side.” She’s been to basketball games, has sat and observed and two hours have quickly passed her by, but nothing compares to what she’s just witnessed.

    And now they’re standing in a dimly lit bar, where the soles of her shoes are sticking to the floor and Lena’s wondering why this feels so exciting. 

    “You liked seeing that?” Kara’s cocky grin returns, but it doesn’t last, her words a little softer when she says, “I liked having someone to show off to.”

    Lena takes a sip of her beer, drinks in the way Kara’s looking at her. She feels hot from it, wanting. This probably isn’t the time, definitely is not the place. Her friends are close by, anyone could turn the corner and spot them together. Sam would know, she just would and Lena’s promised herself she’ll use the holidays to get a handle on things. 

    It’d be all too easy, to take Kara by the hem of her shirt, pull her close, slide her palms across the bare skin of her stomach, lose herself in another wild moment…

    Any chance of acting on those thoughts is gone with their latest frustrating interruption.

    “K.D,” James says in greeting. “Playing?” He asks, picking up a cue and handing one to Mike, who appears a little more aware of what they’ve just walked into.

    “Or we could go and get another round,” Mike says, edging away, but James is already racking up the balls and just gives him a dubious look in reply.

    “How about it?” Kara asks and it takes Lena a second to work out what she means.

    “Sure,” she says. It’s just a game of pool. A totally normal thing for friends to do and the perfect reason to spend more time with Kara.

    Mike claps his hands together in delight, “new blood.”

    Kara rolls her eyes, picks up a cue and passes it to Lena, “you’re with me, right?”

    Lena’s not sure why such a simple phrase has her feeling like she belongs. It’s a nice feeling, it’s even better when she discovers Kara’s good at pool. Even more pleasing when she sees the look on Kara’s face, after Lena pots three in a row. “What? You thought I’d never played before?”

    “Remind me to never underestimate you,” Kara laughs, James is seething, Mike is distracted by the arrival of a tray of drinks.

    “It’ll be at your peril,” Lena says, looking up at Kara and winking, before sinking the black.

    “Rematch,” James says, already racking the balls up. 

    They keep playing, Lena and Kara’s streak continues. James gets over himself for the most part, helped by the arrival of Lucy and Nia and another round of drinks. Lena’s having fun and when Sam and Jess make their way over, she for once doesn’t panic. It’s easy. The two groups of friends, merging as one, not that Lena really notices all that much, not when most of her attention is on Kara and the game they’re playing.

    They stand together and watch as James breaks, their thighs touching in the small space beside the table. It’s cosy and Lena can’t help but press a little closer, she hears Kara’s breath hitch, notes the way her cheeks have warmed and does not miss the look in her eyes when she next takes a shot.

    Nia and Lucy get tired of watching James’s floundering and challenge the undefeated pair in a new game. It’s close, but finally their dominance comes to an end, Lena handing her cue over to Kara, who places it back on the rack, “bowling and pool,” she says, shaking her head, but not hiding the huge smile on her face.

    “A woman of many talents,” Lena says and it’s amusing to see the way Kara’s brain processes that comment. Lena’s so close to breaking her own promise and doing something she can’t take back, but they’ve drank too much and she holds back.

    Kara opens her mouth, then closes it. Lena raises an eyebrow in question.

    “Car’s here,” Jess says, walking past, her head down as she taps away into her phone.

    “I guess that’s me…” Lena looks over her shoulder to see Jack and Sam heading towards the exit.

    “Do you, uh…” Kara hesitates, looking uncertain as she glances to where Lena’s friends are. “I wish you didn’t have to go,” she says, it’s quiet, honest and leaves Lena’s heart racing.

    “Me too, I had a lot of fun tonight. At the game, too.”

    “Have I made a fan of you?” Kara teases.

    Lena shakes her head, takes a lungful of air, with a half smile she says, “I’m too drunk to answer that question.”

    Kara laughs, a low gentle sound, pleased by Lena’s answer none-the-less, “well, I hope it’s not the last time I see you in the crowd?”

    “Always a possibility,” Lena smiles, a flirtatious tease to her tone as they continually hover close to each other, neither seemingly capable of stepping away.

    “I should catch up with my friends,” Lena says, trying to keep it together.

    Kara’s quiet, her gaze flicking from Lena’s eyes to her mouth and back, “yeah, of course,” she says, taking a step back. The spell suddenly broken as she shakes her head. “Have a good night and I’ll see you next week.”

    “It’s break,” Lena reminds her.

    “Yeah,” Kara says in a daze. “Any plans for Christmas?”

    “Family things,” Lena really doesn’t want to think about the long list her mom sent through. “Obligations. You?”

    “Home for a few days, then balling.”

    “Really?”

    “Yep, obligations,” Kara winks and it’s enough to make Lena laugh, which appears to be the desired outcome. “Not too much time off from here on out.”

    “Happy holidays,” Lena says and for some strange reason, she finds herself stepping forward, leaving a gentle kiss at the edge of Kara’s cheek.

    “Happy holidays,” Kara says in a whisper.

    Chapter End Notes

    Next:
    paths cross in Metropolis.

    Chapter 11: wish

    Chapter Summary

    Metropolis

    Chapter Notes

    I've no idea why I'm writing about Christmas in the middle of a summer heatwave, but here we are.

    A warning that Lillian isn't the best of parents here at all.

    Metropolis is one of Lena’s favourite places to be at Christmas. The huge tree in the centre of the park, the colourful lights and displays on every street and the people walking about, meeting up with friends and family or taking their children to meet Santa. It’s magical, beautiful, full of happiness and excitement of what’s to come. She could spend hours walking around, as she does her last-minute shopping, except she doesn’t have hours. She has a schedule and a mother who won’t let her forget it, if she’s late. 

    Instead she gets to view the beauty of the moment, from the back of her family’s Rolls Royce. A barrier to the world she wants to live in, much like the name Luthor, happens to be, at least in Lena’s eyes.

    She tries not to think too hard on the misfortunes of being a Luthor during the stop start ride through the busy streets, which is easy enough, given that her thoughts drift to Kara, as they so often do, and that goodbye kiss. It was only a peck, but still, Lena has a habit of getting hung up on kissing Kara Danvers. She thinks of the resulting smile on Kara’s face, her smooth skin and bright eyes.  

    She wonders what Kara’s doing now? If Christmas with the Danvers’ is as cosy as Lena imagines it to be? Hot chocolate by the fire, a huge dog sprawled out on the rug, whilst the family laugh and joke and play games? Or perhaps Kara is outside, shooting hoops, keeping herself in shape? She unlocks her phone, opens Instagram and the first post is Kara’s. 

    She’s in a set of red and blue flannel pyjamas, a basketball under one arm, a drink in the other as she grins at the camera. Lena double taps the post and closes the app with a quick swipe of her thumb.

    Opening her text thread to read over the last one Kara sent, a simple; Happy Christmas. Lena can’t fight the way it warms her heart, especially on a chilly day where she’s supposed to put on a face and support her mother’s latest project at a fundraising dinner. She tries to cling onto that warmth, when the car finally comes to a stop, outside City hall.

    —

    “You’re late,” Lillian says in greeting, not listening to Lena’s mumbled excuse about the traffic, too busy offering an air kiss, whilst the venue staff hover close by. “I don’t ask much of you, but is it really too much to be on time and show you support a cause close to my heart?”

    Lillian has too many causes close to her heart for Lena to keep track of, being a loving mother, definitely isn’t on the list though.

    “Where do you need me?” She asks, knowing it’s all Lillian really wants from her.

    “In there, making yourself useful,” Lillian says, giving Lena a quick once over, brushing away an imaginary speck of dirt from her shoulder strap. “I knew this was the right dress for you,” she says, smugly. A reminder of yet another choice, which was taken out of Lena’s hands. An entire wardrobe full of clothes, set out for all the events of the holiday’s, in a typical controlling move, that Lena is just too tired to fight. Her only respite, coming once she’s left to her own devices of an evening, curling up in her favourite chair, snuggled up in Kara’s hoody. She can’t wait until she gets the chance to do just that later, but first she has a room to walk.

    The End of Year Gala is a mainstay of the festive period and the key date in her mother’s diary. Lena’s well aware it comes before every other Christmas activity she’d ever wanted to do as a child, even their family meal was full of talk about it and now she’s part of the show.  

    The hall is packed with all the usual faces. The famous, the wealthy and the influential elite of Metropolis. They all look older, have whiter hair and have probably got a few million more dollars in their bank accounts compared to the last time Lena saw them, but it’s not the worst place to be and despite her mother’s fastidious nit-picking, she usually enjoys the evening and the way it raises everyone’s spirits. 

    She doubts enjoyment is on the menu this year, mainly because Lex up and left, citing a problem in the Tokyo office, and now Lena has to go solo. Playing the role of ever-loving daughter, is definitely a lot harder, without her brothers witty commentary and she desperately misses his love of keeping them both sane with a glass or two of his favourite whiskey.

    In Lex’s absence, Lena gets to working her way through the list of people she knows her mother wants her to talk to. She smiles and laughs, tells stories of her time at NCU, answers questions about her future plans, almost like she’s reading from a script. It’s what Lillian expects from her and it’s an easy way to keep on her mother’s good side, if she even has one. 

    Two glasses of champagne have made it all somewhat bearable, the night is passing easily and then she hears the voice of the last person she expected to be at an event like this, “Lena?”

    “Kara?” she turns around in surprise, a smile already tugging at the corners of her mouth, made wider at the sight of Kara Danvers, wearing a gorgeous deep blue suit, over an open-buttoned white shirt. Lena’s eyes drift along the gap, until they rise up to meet Kara’s.

    “The one and only,” Kara’s smiling at her, soft, warm, pretty. The yearning, Lena had hoped she could leave behind on campus, is now very much here and very real and also within about 200 metres of her mother, which can never be a good thing.

    “What are you doing—”

    “The Metropolis Youth foundation,” Kara points to the banner behind Lena’s head, “Making sure no kid goes hungry this Christmas. Although, kind of conflicted about the luxury of this gala, considering what we’re here for.”

    Lena knows the feeling. A lavish dinner, excessive amounts of alcohol, dresses that cost as much as a year’s rent. A side of Metropolis society that has always left her feeling uneasy, more so when they say they’re all there for a good cause. “The wealthy do love to contradict themselves and hypocrisy is always on the menu,” she says drolly.

    Kara snorts out a laugh, “at least there’s good company for a change.” 

    Lena thinks back to last summer, about the events where she and Kara crossed paths, how she’d made a point of ignoring her and now, only a few months later and so much has changed. 

    “It’s my mother’s event,” Lena says, hoping Kara understands, that they definitely can’t hang out now.

    “Oh,” Kara says, casual as ever and then the implication seems to register. “As in, your mom is here?” 

    Lena nods, “and if I don’t fulfil my duties, she’ll come looking.”

    “A drink?” Kara says, ever hopeful. “After you’ve done the duty thing?”

    “I…” Lena hesitates, glancing over her shoulder, half expecting to find her mother watching, but no-one is looking at her, only Kara.

    “Come find me, if you can,” Kara says, light and without pressure. 

    Lena nods again, takes a breath and then gets back to doing what she knows she has to do, if she wants to keep Lillian Luthor off her back and to have any hope of a minute with Kara, which she very much would like.

    —

    It’s a long night, made somewhat easier thanks to the brief glances she catches of Kara across the room. The superstar player is proving popular amongst the who’s who of Metropolis. Chatting easily with the other guests, cracking jokes and looking like she belongs, but whenever she makes eye contact with Lena, the room feels charged and Lena has to suck in a breath just to gain some composure.

    “Is that Ms Danvers?” Lillian asks, from over Lena’s shoulder, her tone curious, rather than disparaging and it instantly has Lena feeling weary.

    “Apparently, you both believe in the same cause,” Lena says, trying to sound as disinterested as she can muster.

    “Maybe your tutoring arrangement, isn’t as troublesome as I first feared,” Lillian muses. “Lex certainly doesn’t seem to think so.”

    Lex’s interest has been troubling Lena since the summer and more so, now she’s gotten close to Kara, making the urge to keep a distance between someone she truly likes and her family, stronger than ever. “That’s because Lex has dollar signs in his eyes.”

    “Nothing wrong with seeing the potential in every connection you make. Maybe your little foray into mediocrity at NCU will prove worthwhile after all,” Lillian flags down a server, takes two glasses of champagne off a silver plated tray and passes one to Lena. “Take care of business and try not to get your feelings all in a muddle, like you did with the Rojas girl.”

    It’s the punch Lena had been expecting and yet it still hurts. Her mother doesn’t offer comfort or care, just scolds and belittles. Lena’s heartbreak is an embarrassment, her college career, questionable. Her life choices, not becoming of a Luthor. “No distractions,” she grits out, frustration written all over her face, repeating the line her mother has given her far too many times over the years.

    Lillian steps back, studies Lena with a look of disdain, which is quickly replaced by a false smile, ever-conscious of anyone who may be watching the interaction between mother and daughter. “Get it together,” she says quiet, but firm. “I don’t need you embarrassing yourself, again.”

    “No, we wouldn’t want that,” Lena mutters under her breath. A memory of the night, she felt like her whole world was crashing down, consumes her. The look on Andrea’s face, the gasp in the room, the horror in Lillian’s eyes, before she took Lena by the hand, into the privacy of her father’s office and made her feelings very clear. 

    Her muttering, only serves to irritate Lillian further, not that a bystander would know. Her expression barely changes as she lowers her voice, “I’ve put up with your little games, tolerated your need to break tradition, accepted you will never quite measure up to your father’s name, but I won’t have you speak to me like this in public.”

    Lena knows not to react, she really does. It’s always been about preserving her own sanity and avoiding conflict is key to survival, but she’s had a drink, she’s ragged from the emotional turmoil of the last few weeks and she’s so tired of hearing the same shit, “the deal didn’t fall through because of anything I did, if you’d listened to my side and not just what Andrea told you then—”

    She’s cut off by Lillian’s tsk, “don’t remind me.”

    “You could at least—,” she tries to argue, but stops herself, at the ice cold look in her mother’s eye.

    “If I’ve not made it clear before; the day you grow out of this phase, cannot come soon enough. Now, close your mouth and try to remember how to behave,” Lillian says, dismissing any further argument Lena may have, before turning on her heel and walking away. Her message sent.

    —

    Lena’s stuck in the same spot, unable to process what just happened, when she feels a gentle touch at her lower back.

    “Hey,” Kara says, so softly, it’s obvious she witnessed the whole incident, even though it’s unlikely she heard any of the words exchanged.

    Lena looks at her, sees the concern, the gentleness in her expression and it’s an effort to fight back the sting of tears. Kara takes the still full glass of champagne from Lena’s hand, places it on a nearby table. “How about we get you a drink, you actually like?”

    Lena doesn’t answer, just lets Kara guide her, with the lightest of touches to her elbow, towards the edge of the room, out of the double doors until they’re away from the hum of the party. She seats Lena down and then disappears, returning a few short minutes later, with a tumbler of whiskey. “Not allowed the whole bottle,” she shrugs, passing it to Lena. “Apparently they have rules here.”

    “Too many,” Lena sighs, taking hold of the glass. “Thank you.”

    “Anytime,” Kara watches her, as she takes a sip. “Your mom really is a piece of work, huh?”

    “That’s one way to describe her,” Lena laughs, but it catches in her throat and her shoulders shake.

    Kara’s hand lands on her back, rubbing smooth circles, “you want to talk about it?”

    “Not much to say. she thinks my sexuality is a phase, but that’s not the worst of my sins. I’m also a disappointment, a burden inflicted on the family and a reminder that my father was not the perfect husband she tries to make him out to be.”

    “She’s not your birth mother?” Kara asks.

    Lena shakes her head, “Lillian’s the saint who took me in and raised me as her own,” she says with a bitterness she rarely shows. “Every time she looks at me, she sees the woman Lionel loved.”

    “My parents are dead,” Kara says with no preamble. “It sucks.”

    “Yeah,” Lena passes Kara the glass and waits for her to take a drink. “You ever feel all alone in the world?” Lena asks and for some reason, it’s her own question and the resulting look on Kara’s face that finally has tears falling down her face. “God, I’m sorry. I’m a total mess,” she wipes at the tracks, but Kara’s hand comes up to stop her, the pads of her thumbs replacing Lena’s, wiping away the traces. “I should clean up before my mother—”

    “Don’t,” Kara’s words are quiet, barely above a whisper, her palm warm on Lena’s cheek, her thumb gently brushing at her cheek. “You’re not a mess, you’re beautiful.”

    Lena scoffs, “I’m kind of a mess.”

    Kara chuckles, low. “Okay, maybe a little.”

    Lena drops her eyes to the floor, shy under Kara’s hot stare. 

    It’s Kara who speaks next, her words still quiet as she says, “not with you.” Lena looks up, her brow crumpling in confusion before Kara adds, “I don’t feel all alone with you. You make me feel…” she sucks in a breath, her eyes never leaving Lena’s, “I’ve never met anyone quite like you.”

    “Same,” Lena says, her voice shaky.

    In the back of Lena’s mind, she can hear an alarm bell ringing. It’s not the place, not the time, but Kara’s looking at her, in a way she hasn’t before and Lena’s not sure what to make of it.

    She can’t think, her heart is beating so hard, she’s sure something must be wrong with it. She can’t worry about it now, though, not when she’s caught in this moment and a wave of tenderness takes over, mixed with a feeling she can’t put words to.

    Kara’s eyes are the bluest she’s ever seen and her hand is so soft, delicate, where it lightly cups Lena’s cheek. The breath she takes is shuddery and it’s making Lena want her all the more.

    There’s no doubt about Kara’s intention, or the unspoken question in her eyes. Lena swallows, nods, holding her breath as Kara edges closer, her gaze dropping to Lena’s mouth. Kara keeps inching forward, until their lips finally meet in a kiss, so sure, yet achingly soft, making Lena’s eyes flutter closed.

    Kara’s hand presses a little firmer, pulling Lena into her, slanting her head to one side, and with a searching swipe of her tongue, she has Lena parting her lips, whimpering the second Kara licks into her mouth, gentle, unsure? It’s too much to process and Lena’s not capable of thoughts, all she can do is kiss back and there’s something about it that feels so fragile which has her left shaking the second Kara pulls back, breathless, her lips slightly parted even as her eyes flicker open. The second they meet Lena’s questioning gaze, Lena can barely remember how to breathe. She’s lost in Kara, she has been for weeks.

    Without hesitation, she reaches forward, grips at Kara’s shirt and pulls her back in, kissing her like she’s the most precious thing on this Earth. The words she’s not been able to find, the feelings she’s been suppressing, no longer important, not when she’s kissing Kara. It’s good, it’s better than good. It’s not like the heated moment on the gymnasium floor, it’s so different, slow. Kara’s as into as Lena and she wishes she could do this forever, but is left reluctantly pulling back to catch a breath, her chest heaving.

    “Lena,” Kara says with a shaky breath, but the sound of a door swinging open has Lena scrambling backwards, in a hurried attempt to get to her feet.

    “Shit,” Lena whispers, the panic taking over, knowing there is only one person who would come looking for her.

    “I’m sorry, I…” Kara says, but Lena shakes her head fiercely, hoping for once it will silence Kara. It does the trick, the blonde drops her head and Lena’s focus returns to the sound of heels on the hard concrete flooring, her heart pounding so loudly in her chest, she’s sure Kara can hear it.

    Close by, Lillian clears her throat and its then that Kara looks up, her eyes wide as she registers what is happening.

    —

    “If you actually made an appearance at one of my events, you’d be able to talk to her yourself,” Lillian says and Lena can easily guess it’s Lex she’s speaking to. “You have too much faith in the girl, Lena’s a habit of—if you heard what she said, no, tiresome…”

    It goes quiet and Lena half wishes she could hear the other side of this conversation. She looks back at Kara, puts up her hand and mouths, “stay here,” before taking a breath and turning the corner, stepping right into Lillian’s path.

    “Lena?” Lillian says in surprise, tucking her phone back into her purse. “Why are you loitering out here?”

    “Getting some air,” Lena lies, conscious of Kara being only metres away, but thankfully out of sight from her ever watchful mother.

    “I was about to come looking for you, it’s about time I met your new friend,” she says, but Lena’s too flustered to reply, leaving Lillian to roll her eyes, assuming her daughter has no idea who she means. “Miss Danvers,” she adds, looking tired.

    “We’re not friends,” Lena says all too quickly.

    “Whatever you may or may not be is of no concern,” Lillian says with that familiar air of disinterest that Lena’s become accustomed to. “Lex thinks we should use the girl’s popularity to our advantage and if we set up a photo op, with some of our core backers, it would work in both our interests.”

    Lena shouldn’t be surprised, but she’s no desire to subject Kara to any of it, “She’s, uh, um,” Lena glances back to where she knows Kara is and tries to think as fast as possible. “Kara had to leave. Game prep.” 

    “What a shame,” Lillian sounds like she couldn’t care less.

    “You know how it is, always practicing, never studying,” she says, taking a step back towards the crowded room.

    “She’s not keeping you from your own work?”

    “Nope, I’d never let that happen.“

    “Good,” Lillian actually sounds relieved, but then she pauses at the doorway, turns to face Lena, “So, there’s no substance behind the rumours Max Lord’s been stirring up?”

    Suddenly, the earlier conversation makes a lot more sense and it’s no surprise to hear, Max went crying straight back to his mother. 

    “I barely tolerate having to work Kara through the most basic of problems,” Lena says, somehow managing to keep her features impassive, even as her mother studies her closely. “You know how the Lord’s are?” She shrugs, perfecting an air of nonchalance that would rival her mother’s. “Max is very much like his grandfather, weak and intimidated by a strong woman. I used Kara as an excuse to escape him at a party. He was talking about alliances.”

    “Unlikely,” Lillian scoffs and Lena’s ever grateful for the bad blood between the two families if it diverts the conversation away from Kara. “The whole family is repulsive,” Lillian continues. “Did I ever tell you about the one time I got stuck in an elevator with the father?”

    Lena opens the door and steers her mother back into the party. 

    —

    It takes longer than she’d like to placate her mother, talk briefly to two donors and then find her way back out into the corridor.

    She’s a little out of breath when she returns to the same spot, to find Kara pacing, “I’m so sorry, she can be a lot at the best of time, but Christmas is the worst.”

    “It’s cool,” Kara pauses mid-step and gives a light shrug of her shoulders.

    “It’s not,” Lena studies Kara curiously, hovers the tiniest bit closer, remembering just what they’d been doing before Lillian had so inconveniently interrupted them.

    “No, no, it’s not,” Kara looks up then, her eyes softening, as she swallows thickly. “She’s not very nice to you.”

    “I’m used to it.”

    “Doesn’t make it okay,” Kara says and it’s sweet to hear how annoyed she is by it, but then she sucks her lips between her teeth and gives Lena a half smile. “I should go.”

    “What?”

    “Yeah, you know, training and getting prepped for the game tomorrow. Travel always has my head in a spin.”

    “If you’re sure? I mean—”

    “Yep, I got hit with a few messages while you were gone, kind of an unofficial curfew and I’m way past it. It was good to see you,” Kara’s backing away towards the exit and Lena’s too stunned by what is happening, to react quickly enough. 

    “Can we…”

    “Enjoy the rest of your break,” Kara says, pushing the door open and swiftly making her way outside into the bustling streets of Metropolis.

    Chapter End Notes

    Did it take 10 chapters for there to be a second kiss, only for Kara to have to leave?
    what is happening.
    next up: some feelings, some repercussions.

    Chapter 12: external forces

    Chapter Summary

    after the kiss
    Kara's overwhelmed. Lena's in a spin.

    Chapter Notes

    There have been a total of two messages from Kara, since their second kiss. One saying she’s okay and apologising for running out. The other, a simple thumbs up, in reply to Lena confirming the time for their current session. The second one, has Lena questioning her own memory of what happened and it leaves her loathe to text anything else, knowing it’d only stem from her own insecurities. 

    With a need to get on with life, she decides it’s best to put Kara’s short responses, down to the fact it’s been winter break and the Wildcats have played back-to-back away games. That said, she still thinks about the moment their lips met. the look in Kara’s eye, the smile on her face, a tenderness shared in the seconds before they kissed. Her heart flutters and it’s hard to bite back a smile, even when Jack walks in and dumps his bags on the table in front of her.

    “Can you get some more wine on your way back?”

    “More?” Lena looks up, one eyebrow raised as she questions the need. “It’s not until Friday!”

    “Best we stock up now and yes, always more, this has to be the party everyone remembers.”

    “If you give them more to drink, they’re more likely to forget it ever happened,” she shakes her head.

    “Ab, but then comes the folklore and the stories that evolve from the broken memories, it will be the best party anyone ever went to and it’s going to be my name they think of.”

    Lena knows he’s not joking, more so when the alert pops up on her phone screen with the money he’s just transferred, “just wine?”

    “And two more bottles of vodka, thank you,” he swoops low to press a kiss to the top of her head. “Is Kara coming?” He asks, when he sees Lena’s tutoring notes.

    It’s a simple question and yet Lena’s stumped at how to answer, “you invited everyone,” she says. 

    Jack raises both hands in reply, “wouldn’t be the best party if it was exclusive to the four of us.”

    Lena laughs in reply, ruffling his floppy hair on her way out the door.

    —

    Despite the lack of communication, she can’t deny the air of excitement, mixed with a sprinkling of nervous energy, as she makes her way towards the training facility, knowing she finally gets to see Kara.

    Lena’s early, as usual, but it looks like practice has ended ahead of schedule, because the first thing she sees, is Lucy making her way out of the building, head down and phone in hand, closely followed by Nia and Leslie, who are both laughing and messing about. Nia catches sight of Lena and waves over to her, enthusiastically. “Lena! Good break?”

    “Yes, you?”

    “The best, glad to be back.”

    “Aren’t we all,” Leslie sighs. “Not that I enjoyed that workout, but at least it was KD getting the worst of Coach for once.”

    The comment has Lena looking between the group, but no explanation comes as they continue on their way. “We’ll see you at the party,” Nia says over her shoulder. 

    “Can’t wait,” Leslie adds.

    “Your boy Jack is certainly his own hype man,” Lucy acknowledges Lena with a simple nod as she looks up from her phone. 

    Lena laughs off the comment, knowing all too well, how hard Jack has been pushing this party, but thoughts of the party and how messy it is all going to turn out, are soon forgotten once she walks into the building, because something is different and it takes her a second to realise what.

    Usually, the first thing she hears is sneakers and thumps as basketballs hit the floor, so it takes her by surprise to catch the sound of raised voices carrying down the hallway. She keeps walking, assuming Cat’s berating someone for a missed layup, or something equally not important, but then she hears Kara arguing back.

    “I told you, I’ve a handle on things.”

    “Good, because you’re too close to let this slip. No distractions, not like last year.”

    “That wasn’t—”

    “I don’t care, Keira. I demand focus, you play for me, for this college. It’s all that counts.”

    “You never have to question my commitment on court,” Kara snaps back and Lena’s startled by how agitated she sounds.

    “It’s not on the court that bothers me,” Cat says, pointedly. “And I do understand it was your Christmas break, but you still represent this team. If you cross a line again…”

    Lena can hear the threat in Cat’s voice and wonders just what line Kara’s crossed. She opens her instagram, looks at all the latest Kara based posts, but it’s all the same as usual and nothing that could merit her getting on the wrong side of the Coach.

    “It won’t happen again,” Kara assures.

    “Good,” Cat says, with a finality that is soon followed by a door being closed. 

    Kara stands there for a moment, gripping the door handle as she takes a few breaths, looking like she has a million things on her mind.

    “Hi,” Lena says and Kara instantly turns around.

    “Lena!” She says brightly, stepping forward, but then her smile falters just a fraction. “Can you give me two minutes?” she asks, not waiting for Lena to reply before hurrying towards the locker room.

    Lena makes her way to their study spot, fixing herself a tea and fishing out Kara’s usual energy drink from the cool box, before taking a seat and waiting. 

    When Kara finally reappears, she’s changed into a grey, Wildcats, sweatsuit and is a little out of breath.

    “Sorry, forgot my books, then Coach got stuck into me,” she mumbles, sitting down and unpacking her bag, then fiddling with her drink bottle, seemingly doing anything to avoid making eye contact. Lena watches her, unobserved for a good minute, before Kara finally looks up. 

    “Everything okay? I heard the bawling out.”

    “Yeah,” she gives a tight-lipped smile, looking like she’s not going to say anymore, so Lena opens her notebook ready to begin, before Kara speaks again. “I skipped a team meeting, for a personal matter, but she got all mad, thinking it was a sponsor deal, which was probably because James didn’t pass on my message and then I wasn’t my best in our last game, which kind of got in the way at practice…” she takes a deep breath, “and then there was some other stuff and well, yeah...”

    Lena can see how troubled Kara is and wishes she had some way of taking some of the pressure off her shoulders.

    “She works you too hard,” Lena says truthfully. Bothered by how Kara, and the rest of the team, are expected to act like they’re professional athletes, when they’re not even getting paid. She’s half a mind to remind Ms Grant of it, the next time she sees her.

    “It’s how we win,” she says defensively, like it’s justification for everything she has to go through. Lena looks at her for second, wondering what is going on underneath the rote reply. Kara fidgets under the scrutiny, “Did you want to get started?” She asks when the silence drags on.

    “Sure,” Lena sighs and does just as Kara wants. It’s a quiet session, unlike the ones before break. Kara returns to giving short answers and has no interest in messing about. She’s not her usual distracted self and although it was exactly what Lena wanted from Kara when they first started working together, she finds she kind of hates the change.

    —

    “Thanks and sorry again about being late,” Kara says as she stuffs her books back into her bag. It’s then that Lena’s hit with the sudden realisation that Kara’s about to leave.

    “Can we talk?” she asks, her heart hammering with nerves, unsure at what is going on.

    “I should really get to the gym, make up for missing some of the work over break.”

    “Kara?” Lena pleads, not willing to let this be the way things go.

    “What?” Kara says back, with a terseness that surprises Lena. She can’t fight the way it makes her eyes sting, just the tiniest.

    “I think we need to talk about what happened,” she perseveres in spite of how uncomfortable it feels. “You kissed me?”

    “And, I shouldn’t have,” Kara says through a heavy exhale, there’s heat in her eyes as she continues. “Is that what you want me to say? Same as before. Just another big mistake? I get it, I do. You’re busy, this is a job, I crossed a line. We spent too much time together and it’s nothing, right?” Lena stands in stunned silence as Kara’s rapid outburst continues. “So don’t worry about it. You were right the first time and this one was on me and I’m sorry. We can forget it happened and move on.”

    “Is that what you want?” Lena’s not sure what is happening.

    “It’s what I need. I’ve too many things going on right now and this is just…” she throws her hands up, looking more tired than Lena’s ever seen her. “I don’t have time, I can’t…basketball is my world, I…it’s all I’ve dreamed about.”

    “And when you wake up?” Lena sounds a bit like Lillian and could kick herself for it.

    Kara seems equally troubled and just looks at her, like she’s trying to figure something out, “I know you think it’s nothing, but it’s what I’ve wanted my whole life. I’ve worked for this and it’s not a game, not a fantasy. I’m serious, it’s what I want. It’s all I’ve ever wanted.”

    It’s Lena’s turn to take a breath, it does nothing to calm her racing heart or to stop the nagging thought that this is Kara’s way of drawing a line between what they are and what they can be. Two kisses, two mistakes. She can’t help but feel a fool for getting carried away in her own feelings and questions if she’s simply fallen for Kara’s practiced charm. Letting herself get carried away and probably on her way to being just another of the star players many, rumoured, conquests. 

    “Forget it ever happened,” Lena snaps, surprising herself at how easily she falls back into their old pattern of behaviour, with hostility simmering to the surface, like an old friend.

    “Good,” Kara’s just at sharp in return.

    They’re staring at each other now, neither able to drop whatever this is.

    “Great,” Lena juts her jaw out, quickly putting her things away, all set to storm off. Kara’s still watching her as she pushes the chair out of her way.

    “You’re really something, you know that?”

    “Oh, I do,” Lena seethes with the bitterness of Kara’s rejection. She has to leave, put some space between them, she has to. “I’m also something you’ll never have.”

    “You think I want you?” Kara scoffs, incredulously, but her eyes betray her, as they run slowly up and down Lena’s body. It may not be in the way Lena wanted, but yes, she has a feeling Kara wants her.

    She felt in in that kiss. Sees it in the way Kara looks at her and knows there is so much more to what is going on between them in the tender moments and late night conversations they’ve shared, but right now she’s angry and lashing out is what she does best.

    Lena has a choice in this moment, to walk out and not look back or to scream in Kara’s face.

    In the end she does neither, instead she looks Kara dead in the eye and with an air of assuredness, that she definitely doesn’t feel, says, “oh, I know you do.” 

    Kara just stares back at her. Her chest heaves as she lets out a breath, then wets her lips. Lena sees the conflict in Kara’s eyes, but is not going to wait around for any kind of answer, especially when she’s not sure one is coming or that it’d be one she wants to hear.

    Grateful that the rest of the team have long left, she rushes out through the empty hallways, reaching the front door, before she’s left gasping for breath and doing a valiant attempt at holding back burning tears. She’s about to leave, but hesitates at the sound of a door, slamming shut. It’s followed by the distinct sound of a ball being shifted around the court. She doesn’t need to look, to know it’s Kara. It’s always basketball for Kara, she said as much. Lena’s angry at herself, mad at Kara, annoyed with whatever else is going on and wishes she could smash a ball against the floor to work out her own frustration, but instead she’s left feeling like an idiot and resorts to her own tried and tested coping mechanism and calls Jack, knowing he’ll already be celebrating something, somewhere nearby.

    —

    “On a Tuesday?” Jack’s delighted to have Lena there, even if he can’t quite believe it.

    “If you had to spend Christmas with the Luthor’s, you’d understand,” she mumbles into her cocktail. It’s always a good way to get out of answering too many questions. All her friends know the might of her family and how desperately she’s been trying to distance herself from them during her time at college.

    “Thankfully, my dear ones chose snow over water this time, it’s a shame you couldn’t fly out to join in. Sam had a blast.”

    “I saw,” Lena was quite envious as their group chat filled with clips of Sam taking to the slopes, whilst Jack always seemed to have a beer or a shot in his hand. The drunken fun of two friends, making Lena wish she got even half that happiness during her own break, right up until Kara had shown up at the gala and left a smile on Lena’s face for the rest of the holidays. A smile that feels so foolish after their argument this afternoon.

    “But now we’re back, normal service is resumed,” Jack brings her back to the present, clinking their glasses together. “And this semester is going to be the best yet.     “And that’s her?” she asks, Jack nods.

    “Kara doesn’t look too happy to see her,” he says in this soft, comforting way. Like he thinks Lena needs some reassurance she’d not asked for?

    Not wanting to think about it, Lena drains the remainder of her cocktail and quickly orders another, because no way is she going to spend the night thinking about Kara or her ex. She has a much better plan, which involves getting drunk and forgetting.

    —

    “This is going to be the night, no-one ever forgets,” Jack announces once they’re all gathered in the kitchen. He’s a glass of liquid in his hand, that has Lena questioning his assertion with a single raised brow. “Taste test,” he continues, unperturbed.

    She’s nervous. It’s understandable. The last time she saw Kara, they kind of…well it was an argument. An argument that ended with Lena’s comment, about how she knows Kara wants her. Where do they go from there? Lena’s really fucking confused. 

    Kara’s been playing basketball, Lena’s gone to class, Lex has messaged her, three times and Lillian has called, once. She’s ignored them both, ran fifteen miles and resisted the temptation to message Kara.

    Not that Kara’s reached out, which is more of a problem, because up until Christmas came around, Lena had gotten used to Kara’s nonsensical texts, her random photos and quick questions about school work, which probably weren’t needed, but had kept them in constant contact. The complete silence is jarring and has Lena realising just how much she misses Kara and the easy way she’d become part of Lena’s life. 

    Knowing she’ll see Kara at some point tonight, has Lena taking the second glass of punch that Jack hands her and draining it, hoping the potent concoction will calm her nerves. She’s not sure it makes any difference at all, because she still spends the next hour, glancing towards the door, waiting for any sign of Kara and the basketball team, thankfully everyone else is enjoying themselves too much to notice.

    Chapter 13: observations

    Chapter Summary

    Kara shows up, Lena can't stop looking

    Chapter Notes

    Kara doesn’t make a big entrance, not like the rest of the team. She simply slips into the circle and does all she can to go mostly unnoticed, which isn’t all that easy when you are the player everyone is always talking about. Lena certainly can’t miss her, or stop looking over. Kara’s dressed in a loose-fitting shirt, on top of a sports bra, with pants that cling to her waist and only serve to accentuate her abs, which leaves Lena hoping she doesn’t look as thirsty as she feels in that moment.

    “I am,” Kara takes a step back and the sudden space between them, feels like a ravine.

    “Hmm,” Lena hums. Unconvinced by Kara’s evasive way of answering and letting it show.

    Kara shifts again, “I get the odd flare up. Nothing to worry over.” 

    “Okay,” Lena says, accepting Kara’s truth, even if she knows she’s still going to worry.

    “Okay,” Kara repeats, then she gives a tight smile, “so, um, sorry about the other day, things got heated and I lost my cool after what happened with Coach and, so…well, I just wanted to say sorry.”

    “Same,” Lena’s not sure how to take back what she said, but she would like to move past it. It’s possible her feelings for Kara aren’t reciprocated and it feels shit, but she likes Kara and would hope they could be civil at the least. “I shouldn’t have snapped back and I don’t want you thinking I don’t care about your basketball career, because I do and working this hard to make your dream come true is admirable.”

    “You know, I almost didn’t come tonight,” Kara admits.

    “Why?”

    “Wasn’t sure it’d be ok,” Lena figures it’s a fair comment, considering how things have been.

    “It was just an argument,” she tells Kara, still trying to convince herself it wasn’t a big deal.

    “Yeah?” Kara gives this searching look and it has Lena’s belly doing a strange little dance. She’s not used to feeling so uncertain. A nervousness that feels like a dare and has her clinging on, to find out what it could lead to.

    “Yes,” she says, sounding a lot more confident than she feels. “I’m glad you came.”

    “Me too, I wanted to see you,” Kara says and it’s so open that Lena finds herself taking a shaky breath, powerless to resist the gravitational pull, that sucks her back into Kara’s orbit, all over again.

    “I didn’t like how things went down or the way I handled myself,” she looks down at her feet and shoves her hands in her pants pockets. “I was mad at some stuff and got in my head about what you said to your mom.”

I was only trying to keep my mom from using your face for her own ends…” she’s somehow closer to Kara now, her hand hovering in the space between them. “My family would use you and our friendship to make that happen. Trust me, it’s best they don’t know about us.” Her choice of words only serves to turn up Lena’s inner turmoil, whilst Kara tilts her head curiously, “I…we’re friends, Kara. Whether I tell my mother, or not, is another matter entirely.”

Because I think you know me well enough to know I like you,” Lena may like her in other ways, but the idea Kara thought she didn’t want her at this party, or that they aren’t even friends seems silly. They may have slipped back into arguing this week, but Lena knows that fire is fuelled by the sexual tension between them, not that Lena’s going to explain that point, to a slightly pink cheeked Kara. “Is that why you left the gala?”

You were upset and I got caught up in it and then I kissed you, which wasn’t what you wanted and—”

    “You did?” Kara looks so surprised that Lena can’t hold back a light chuckle.

    “I did,” she says, grinning as Kara’s whole face brightens into a huge smile.

    “Lena, darling,” Jack ruins the moment and it’s no surprise to find he’s already slurring, considering what she saw going into his cup earlier. He plants a sloppy kiss at her temple, his arm clumsily wrapping around her shoulders. “Good party?”

    “The best,” Lena says, one eye on Kara, who looks bothered not just by Jack’s appearance, but also his closeness.

    “Best night ever,” he says, lurching forwards in his quest for the bathroom.

    “Jack keep a lot of his stuff in your room?” Kara asks, glancing around. She may be trying to sound cool, but oh, no Lena can’t miss that she’s irritated,

    “I guess you really should go,” Lena slowly runs her palm down Kara’s arm, until their fingers meet.

    “I’ll see you, after I get back from Gotham?”

    “You’ll see me.”

    Kara inhales deeply, a smile slowly forms and then she kisses Lena again, swift and over all too quickly, “goodnight, Lena Luthor.”
