    Kara squeezes Lena’s hand, leans in one last time and then answers the call, “hey, Al, yeah, just leaving…”

    Lena opens the door, whispers, “good luck at Gotham.”

    Kara shoots her the winningest smile and it’s what has Lena feeling like she’s floating for the rest of the night, even though she never sees Kara leave.

    —

    Two days later, Lena’s on the couch, Jack beside her, still nursing the worst hangover he’s ever had, whilst they get to witness Kara tearing into the Gotham Gophers. It’s Kara’s most dynamic performance of the season, she’s bossing the court and records a season high in steals and assists. Lena’s never seen anything like it and god, is Kara at her most attractive when she’s like this. Hitting a perfect fade-away middy to wrap up the win. If the kiss they shared has anything to do with it, then Lena’s happy for them to keep practising for as long as Kara needs and maybe, just maybe, it’ll get her and the Wildcats, all the way to that national championship.

    Chapter 14: stolen moments

    Chapter Summary

    after the party, time to sneak about and enjoy.

    Chapter Notes

    With the Gotham trip out of the way, Lena’s eagerly looking forward to seeing Kara again, even if it is just for their scheduled tutoring session. She would have perhaps hoped they’d have managed to meet up sooner, but with Kara’s basketball commitments and Lena’s hectic schedule, their interactions have been limited to a few texts. 

    Their lives are busy, a fact that is unlikely to change anytime soon and even though they may be destined for very different paths in the future, it doesn’t stop Lena thinking about what a relationship with Kara could look like.

    She stops herself, realising she’s getting ahead of the whole situation, three kisses and a lot of mixed up emotions is where they are at and she reminds herself of this, as she pushes the door open to find Kara already sat at the desk, head down in her books.

    It’s a sight she’d never expected when she first committed to tutoring and it’s one that has her smiling to herself as she clears her throat, alerting Kara to her presence.

    “Hey,” she looks up, a smile slowly tugging at the corners of her mouth and then her eyes drift downwards… ”you good?” Kara asks Lena’s chest.

    “Very,” Lena replies, crossing the room and taking the seat next to Kara, pretending that Kara’s reaction wasn’t exactly what she was hoping for and sticking to the reason she’s being paid to be in the room. “I drew up a revision plan for your next test and think we should go over a few things in preparation.”

    “I did,” Lena says coyly, not wanting to give away too much about how that last performance affected her.

    “I was thinking about you when I put—” whatever she’s about to say gets lost in the sound of the door being shunted open.

    “Danvers, you got the keys?” Leslie asks, not caring if she’s interrupting.

    “Here,” Kara tosses up a keychain and Leslie catches it, just as Nia arrives.

    “Back again, Lena? Seems unfair that you got stuck with this loser.”

    “Hey,” Kara grumbles, but there’s no heat in it as Nia gives her shoulder a squeeze.

    “Only kidding,” Nia laughs, pulling snacks out of the drawer in the kitchen. “I know you’re smart.”

    “I am,” Kara says smugly, then looks over at Lena and it’s amusing to watch how quickly doubt flickers across her face. “Am I?” she asks, quietly.

    “Smart enough,” Lena says, trying not to laugh as Nia rolls her eyes.

    “See, I’m all focus, don’t you question it again, Nal.”

    “Hmm yep, we all see the change,” Nia says, stuffing half a protein bar in her mouth, whilst shooting a wink at Lena.

    “You better give a shout out to Lena when you pick up that MVP trophy,” Leslie flops down on the couch, uncapping a bottle of soda with a hiss.

    “I will,” Kara says under her breath. “But could you both leave?” she waves both arms in the direction of the door.

    “Oh, this is your personal study suite now is it?” Leslie grumbles, but she’s already being tugged up and off the couch, by a merciful Nia.

    “You know the rules, can’t let the captain of the team flunk one more test or we’ll all pay.”

    “Coach was never really going to drop her,” Leslie says, but then she looks between Nia and Kara and realises it’s no joke.

    “I meant for the game,” Lena laughs.

    “Yeah, sure you did,” Kara winks and it’s too much to not be able to see her in person right now.

    “Night,” Kara says softly, and Lena ends the call.

    Chapter 19: for you

    Chapter Summary

    in the frenzy. all the good things can rush by.

    Chapter Notes

With a freedom to act on the tension that has been simmering between them for the last few months, neither holds back in their endeavours to learn more about the other, building an intimacy that Lena’s never experienced before.     Chapter 20: options

    Chapter Summary

    Feelings, satisfaction, and life plans, can be all consuming.

    “I…” Lena hesitates, it’s a good question and it’s something Kara’s already given her pause for thought on. “Maybe you don’t know, but I think it helps to enjoy each moment, for what it’s worth, much like you do with each shot you take, every win, every loss. Makes a lot of sense to live life the same way.” 

    Kara appears to like this answer, nodding as she shifts closer, “yeah, I like that.”

    Lena thinks it’s about time she did the same, acknowledging her own achievements as she goes, because she’s knows she’ll never get any recognition from her family, unless it directly affects them. She’s all for living in the moment and wanting to enjoy the time they have together, she asks, “do you have long?”

    “I’ve got about thirty minutes until I have to get back,” Kara leans in, her lips pressing against Lena’s. A kiss which is now so familiar and still as devastating as the first one they shared on the floor of the gymnasium.

    “Then we’d better make the most of it,” Lena says and Kara does not disappoint.

    They both startle when her phone starts blaring out an alert.

    “Sorry, that’s me,” Kara whacks the screen a few times to try and turn it off, then reluctantly climbs off of Lena’s bed. “This is definitely where I’d rather be sleeping, but there’s no way Leslie will cover.”

    “Focus on tomorrow,” she takes hold of both Kara’s hands, swinging them in the space between them, not quite ready to let her go. 

    “Only if you focus on having fun?”

    “I’ll do my best, but god, if you see me with sunglasses on inside the arena, you’ll know Jack has all but destroyed me.”

    Kara laughs, her eyes crinkling in the corners and she’s so pretty, that Lena can’t look away, the laughter fades and her smile softens, “I’ll be looking out for you.”

    —

    Kara keeps her promise, spotting Lena just before tipoff and thankfully she’s not in dark glasses, but the look Kara gives her, makes Lena’s heart skip a beat and she has to hope none of her friends are watching her too closely, right now, because she’s sure her feelings are written all over her face. Jack’s hand comes down on top of her shoulder and he hands her a cup of beer, “drink, we’ll need it.”

    She takes the drink, thinking it’ll help cover her face, but Jack’s reasoning is also accurate, because the game is scrappy, with Kara being double-teamed whenever she gets the ball. Nia and Lucy get the opportunity to take advantage of the space available and both score season highs, whilst Kara’s assist tally hits double figures. It’s not until the 4th that she starts to put on a show herself and with the opposition now giving away free throws, whenever they foul, Kara’s either on the line or finally has some space to shoot.

    The Wildcats win, everyone is on their feet. Next stop, Final Four. Lena’s never known a feeling quite like it, even though she knows it’s far from over, she’s enjoying the moment, much like Kara appears to be down on the court.

    —

    Kara messages her a couple of hours later and Lena knows they won’t get much time together, so she slips out of the bar and rushes back to her suite. She finds Kara loitering in the corridor, the same baseball cap pulled down low, but it’s not really doing much of a job of disguising who she is, not in a town, where anyone could recognise her.

    “Hey,” she says, so very quiet as Lena approaches. A door opens nearby and Lena’s quick to unlock her room, pulling Kara inside without being seen.

    ”You did it,” she says and is met with the biggest grin she’s ever seen on Kara’s face.

    “Pretty cool, huh?”

    “Pretty fucking amazing,” Lena can feel the rush in her chest even before Kara closes in and kisses her. It’s fast, frantic. Both desperately trying to discard every item of clothing before they land haphazardly on Lena’s bed. Kara’s full of adrenaline, Lena thinks she might be too. She can’t get enough of the women lying beneath her and when Kara tries to take charge, Lena’s quick to put her hand on her shoulder and push her back down onto the bed, “not tonight. Let me show you, how watching you makes me feel?”

    Kara captures her bottom lip between her teeth, her eyes darkening as she nods once and Lena needs no further invitation. 

    She knows what Kara likes, where to touch her and how to have her teetering on the edge until with a final swipe of her tongue, she’s shaking in Lena’s arms, “that…” she’s breathless, her eyes searching Lena’s and Lena can’t help it, she has to kiss her, hears the startled surprise when Kara tastes herself on Lena’s tongue, loves the way Kara responds by tangling her fingers in Lena’s hair. She climbs into her lap and Kara’s fingers make quick work of bringing Lena towards her own release.

    “I like it when you win,” Lena eventually has to climb off of Kara.

    “Me too, better when I get to celebrate like this, with you…” Kara’s watching closely as Lena pulls a tee over her head. “People might question why you have so much Wildcat merch at some point,” she points out the logo on the recently acquired shirt.

    “Let them,” she can’t care, not when she’s feeling perfectly blissed out. 

    Kara looks contemplative for a second, then asks, “do you know when you’ll be there?”

    There, being the Final Four and Lena’s now realising she probably needs to check the jet is still on hand to whisk her, and her friends, across the country, “day after tomorrow, You?”

    “We’re going straight there in the morning. Cat wants us to get settled, so we leave early,”

    “Take it you can’t sleep over?’

    Kara shakes her head, “nope, I’ll have to get back to my room soon. Coach probably won’t check, but I can’t not be at morning call.”

    “And then what?”

    “Thankfully, no practice tomorrow, just travel and then the circus begins.”

    “It’s full on?”

    “Yeah, kind of crazy. Media and sponsors and…yeah,” Kara looks tired just thinking about it. “I don’t know if we’ll be able to meet up as easily. I’ll text you though.”

    “I’ll be waiting.”

    Kara kisses her, soft, tender, like she’s saying something she can’t put into words and Lena clings on a little tighter as she says goodnight, then sneaks back out of the door and to her own room.

    It takes Lena a minute or two to get it together, knowing she needs to get back to her friends and not leave this weekend with them thinking all she does is disappear and miss the party. 

    She finds them where she left them, all a little more worse for wear, but none the wiser about where she’s been. The party is loud, the Wildcats appearing to have picked up new fans amongst the throngs of people attending the weekend and Jack is at the heart of it, until he isn’t. 

    Finally, it all gets too much even for him, leaving Sam, Lena and Jess to try to get him back to the hotel.

    “I love yous,” Jack slurs, which might be cute, if Lena wasn’t struggling to get him to move forwards.

    “We love you,” Sam giggles, not quite as drunk as Jack, but not far off it.

    “And in the morning we are going to need mimosa’s,” Jess declares, far too loudly. Lena shoots her a look, but the other two are nodding.

    She can only imagine the state of them, when they finally get on the jet, but she can’t argue and actually gets sent back to her room, with Sam and Jess happy to make sure Jack gets home safe. Lena collapses into her bed, tired and achy, but happy. She can still smell Kara on her pillow and sleep easily takes over.

    —

    She wakes with a start, unsure of her surroundings and it takes her a moment to realise someone is knocking gently on her door. She stumbles across the room, half expecting it to be Jack on a mission to get them all to do more shots, so is surprised to see Kara through the spy hole.

    “Hi,” she says opening the door, not caring what she looks like.

    Kara looks her up and down and has to hold back a chuckle, “late one?”

    Lena quickly runs her fingers through her hair, but imagines it’s a fruitless endeavour, “very! What time is it?”

    “Early,” Kara says and Lena ushers her inside. “I can’t stop, I just…” Kara’s dressed in her Wildcats training kit, she looks snug and soft and Lena finds herself wrapping her arms around her in her sleepy daze. “Came to say goodbye,” Kara’s words are muffled as she presses her face into Lena’s neck. 

    They stay like that for a good minute and Lena hates to let go, but the way Kara’s phone starts buzzing, in her track-pants pocket, tells her it’s definitely time. She moves her hands, from where they’ve been clinging onto Kara’s back, up to her face, taking a second to look at her, “you are definitely worth getting out of bed for,” she says and Kara’s face lights up and then she’s kissing Lena, like she’s all the time in the world, despite the fact she’s supposed to be boarding a coach for the airport any minute now.

    “Much rather be getting back into your bed,” she says, when Lena gently pushes her back.

    It reminds Lena of the comment Kara once made about how she likes her breakfast and this is not where Lena saw things going between them, all those months ago, but now she’s here and it feels like it was probably written in the stars or some other romantic shit, that she can’t dwell on because Kara’s halfway out the door, before she turns back, planting one more kiss on Lena’s lips.

    “I’ll see you soon.”

    “You will,” Kara says. Whether they can grab a moment alone, in the next few days is still unknown, but whatever happens, Lena already knows that seeing Kara play, will be worth the trip and she really hopes it ends with a trophy being held aloft.

    Chapter 22: pressure point

    Chapter Summary

    Final four, battles off and on the court.

    Chapter Notes

    College hoops are back!!! I'll try to get this done before March comes around again.

    Eight becomes four and a trip to the opposite side of the country, has Lena wondering how any of the players cope with such a demanding schedule of playing so many games in quick succession and then having to fly for hours, just so the tournament organisers can put on a showpiece.

    Kara’s not complaining, too focused on the task at hand, even though she’s had her knee wrapped in ice, whenever she’s flown. Her efforts to disguise it under loose-fitting track pants, haven’t gone unnoticed and the press are happy to speculate about her level of fitness whenever they shove a microphone in her face. 

    “Just a precaution,” Kara tells the reporter, as she’s pulled aside for a few words during a shoot-around. “It swells up after a game, I put the ice on, we’re good.”

    “Your fans will be pleased,” the reporter barrels on. “How do you feel about the team’s prospects? Playing the Angels is always a fierce encounter and we all know how last year ended.”

    “Yeah, we’re good. Studying film, getting prepped.”

    “And to finish your time at NCU with a championship is the dream?”

    “One game at a time, Holly. Can’t win the champs, if we don’t focus on Friday,” Kara’s charismatic charm has the reporter chuckling back at her.

    “Well, good luck. Play well,” Holly ends the interview and Kara jogs away, catching a ball and shooting a logo three in the background, before running towards Lucy. The pair bounce up into the air, bumping their chests together and Lena fights to suppress a smile when Jack catches her eye.

    The taxi comes to a stop outside their hotel and Lena swipes the app closed, grabbing for her hand luggage, exiting the car behind her friends, pausing when the noise of the lingering crowds hit her.

    “Oh, we’re going to party,” Jack turns back to her in delight, then hooks his arm in hers, steering them towards the lobby.

    —

    Despite Jack’s plan to party, the last few days of indulgence have actually left him needing a break and their first twenty-four hours is, thankfully, quite sedate. The four of them, taking the chance to enjoy some sun, relaxing by the pool and in Lena’s case, avoiding anyone who might recognise her as the youngest Luthor.

    It’s proving a lot harder than she’d expected, because this is the showcase of college basketball and everywhere she looks, she can see another sign directing her towards, “Tourney Town presented by LuthorCorp.” She had no idea Lex was this invested, but as she looks at the weekend schedule, the brand, and her last name, is everywhere. She’d add it to the list of things she’s missed lately, but this is not something that would have passed her by, no matter how wrapped up in Kara she’s been. The only reason she didn’t know LuthorCorp were involved, is because she wasn’t told and that is never a good thing when it comes to Luthor business.

    She may have spent the last few months being evasive whenever Lex tried to get info on Kara, but it still strikes her as odd, that her brother hadn’t mentioned this deal when she asked to borrow the jet, or at any other point during their recent catchup.

    All her questions are answered a second later, when an email lands in her inbox. Hidden behind the usual weekly update is a causal request to show her face at one, or more, of the player appearances, if she can spare the time. Lena’s half a mind to ignore it, but these things never go away and she needs to know what her brother is up to.

    “Lena!” Lex answers the phone with his usual warm greeting.

    “Why?” She asks, cutting straight to the point.

    “I take it you got the email,” Lex says, not waiting for her to confirm. “We talked about this, it’s time to raise your profile within the company. Luthor’s lead,” he says and Lena can pick out the distinct sound of him typing away on his keyboard, not even bothering to give this conversation his full attention. “…it’s a huge investment. We want people to see greatness on the court and think, LuthorCorp.”

    “I imagine, they’ll mostly be thinking about whether their team wins or not.”

    “Besides the point,” Lex says through a smile. “We need a new outlook and being associated with success is game changing. It’s just some photos, Lena. Maybe say a few words, nothing too challenging. I’ll have Eve, send you an outline.”

    “Sounds like I don’t have much choice,” Lena knows how these things go and it’s some relief that she packed something other than the numerous Wildcats branded clothing, she’s procured from Kara over the last few months. 

    “Well, you are the one who commandeered the jet,” he says in a way that tells Lena, this was always the plan. Everything comes at a cost.

    Her chances of going unnoticed, continue to be an issue though and much like when she first arrived, the lobby is packed. Everywhere she looks there are reporters, tv crews and a million wannabe influencers using their phones to catch and stream every second of what is happening off court, leaving Lena feeling a lot less anonymous than she’d like to.

    She’s got her head down as she types a message to Kara to let her know to sneak in one of the side entrances, when she hears an all too familiar voice and comically ducks for cover behind the nearest pillar.

    A man dressed up in a hotdog costume gives her a strange look, which would almost make her laugh, if her own predicament wasn’t so ridiculous, because no way is Andrea Rojas in the middle of an interview with a reporter, who Lena vaguely recognises as working for The Planet, but nope, that is exactly what is happening and Lena can hear every word she’s saying. 

    “My father has always been a fan and with the tournament being hosted by our partners, Obsidian had to get involved, although I’m here for more personal reasons. You know, my love for the Bullhorns, isn’t restricted to just football. Go Horns!” She says, loudly, in a rallying call, which has another group chanting loudly in reply.

    Thanks to her mother, Lena had known what school Andrea went to, did hear about her relationship with some football player and had done all she could to forget that information as quickly as she heard it. She could kick herself, for not putting two and two together, but unlike Jack, she wasn’t all that focused on the bracket and hadn’t exactly been bothered to look at who Kara’s team might end up playing in the latter stages of the tournament and even if she had, she wouldn’t have seen this coming. 

    She tunes back into the conversation, just as the reporter says, “and we’ve heard a rumour about you becoming a future Mrs Rogers?”

    “No comment,” Andrea’s reply is coy and has Lena rolling her eyes. This is the same person she fell head over heels for? It’s weird to think about how much she’s changed since back then. 

    “Understood,” the reporter is doing a wonderful job of kissing up to Andrea and Lena already intends to add him to Lex’s list of problematic interviewers. “But you can say if Russ will make an appearance this weekend, right?”

    “Absolutely, he wouldn’t miss the chance to cheer on the girls, we’re a family, that’s what college is all about.”

    Lena’s heard enough and uses the cover, offered by a passing group of fans, to escape. She strides purposefully towards the elevator, jabbing the button to her floor, only taking a breath when the doors finally close. Clinging to the idea, that if Andrea is busy doing some kind of public relations exercise, with her boyfriend by her side, she definitely won’t have time to get in Lena’s way. Definitely, probably, hopefully.

    “This is my favourite,” Kara seems unfazed by all the paraphernalia and digs out a collectible figure. It’s supposedly of Kara, but Lena can’t see the likeness. “Leave it in the box, might be worth something one day,” she jokes.

    “Not my Tourney Town,” Lena pauses midway through scanning the menu. “But I am going to have to be a Luthor for a few hours this weekend.”

    “Yeah?”

    “Not ideal, but borrowing the jet comes at a cost. Lex has plans for me to raise my public profile.”

    “Oh, bet you’re over the moon about it,” Kara teases, coming to sit down beside Lena. 

    “You know me,” she laughs, leaning into Kara’s side. “Love to be front and centre. Pasta?”

    “Mm, yep, can I get two portions?”

    “Whatever you need,” Lena says, placing the order.

    “Long enough?”

    “Yes,” Lena answers. “I’d not have mentioned her, but I’m already doing all I can to avoid anyone spotting me, but this…” Lena tries to make light of it, throwing her hands up and smiling at Kara, who returns it, albeit a little less bright than usual.

    The question takes Lena by surprise, “I’m here for you, to support you.”

    “So long as you’re—”

    “I am.”

    “You’ll be at the game?”

    “Wouldn’t miss it. Not even if I was forced to sit next to Andrea.”

    “Let’s hope not,” Kara scoffs.

    “You know, I love that you’ve still not a clue about basketball.”

    “Not true,” Lena defends herself. “I know you’re very good at it and seeing as I’m only interested in watching you, why should I learn about anyone else?”

    “Like watching me, huh?”

    “You know I do,” Lena plays into it, climbing into Kara’s lap and all talk of basketball is put aside until Kara has to leave for the night.

    Before they can think about playing the Bullhorns, the Wildcats have to get past a hotly anticipated rematch with the Angels, the team who knocked NCU out last year. 

    Lena hadn’t paid much attention when the two faced-off during the regular season, but now she knows, Kara is about to come face-to-face with her long-time nemesis, Selina Kyle, she’s feeling more on edge than usual.

    Jack’s not helping, his nerves making him talk incessantly about the convoluted history between the pair, “both on the US team, but SK never got a look in, they’d never even talk about her if Kara hadn’t missed a year. It’s crazy, but the press loves to build a rivalry, think it gets the fans more invested.” Lena wants to point out that it’s working, because Jack’s certainly bought into it. “They showed up last year and hit every weakness in the team.”

    “This year, is not last year,” Lena says, thinking about how Kara keeps saying something similar. 

    “I know, I do,” he looks up at her with a downturned smile. “I did warn you; basketball is life.”

    “You did,” she says, taking a breath and wondering if she’s really prepared for how much it could change hers. “If it helps, Kara’s feeling confident.”

    “You’ve seen her?” Jack asks eagerly.

    “Briefly, during the Tourney thing earlier,” Lena says, which is a half truth. She did walk past Kara on her way to one of the fan sessions.

    “And why did no-one tell me,” Jack pouts.

    “Next time,” she promises. “She’s promised to sign one of those figurines for you.”

    Jack’s pout turns into a huge smile, “knew you tutoring Kara was going to work out well for us all.”

    Lena closes her eyes, wishes she could tell him, all the ways tutoring Kara has changed her life, but for now she can’t quite find the words and when she opens them, Jack isn’t even looking at her, because Sam and Jess have arrived.

    She lets go of a breath, wonders how long she’s going to keep her feelings for Kara a secret and with a single shot of vodka, for a beat of courage, they all make their way to the arena.

    —

    By the end of the third, the Angel’s lead by five, in what has so far been a scrappy, stop start encounter. It’s not been the easiest or the most entertaining spectacle as of yet, with both teams trading possession and buckets, steered mainly by a flurry of errors and questionable calls from the refs.

    Jess has been shouting so much that her voice is hoarse, Sam and Jack are trying to get a Wildcat chant going and Lena’s ignoring it all, because her heart’s beating a little bit faster as the game builds towards its climax and her eyes are on where the team are being talked through the next play by Cat.

    Kara’s on the edge of the huddle, a towel hanging loosely around her neck and an unreadable expression on her face. Cat says something directly to her and Kara nods, takes a drink from her water bottle, then puts her hand into the circle as the cry of “Wildcats” rings out and then echoes around the arena.

    Only thirty seconds of play pass, before Leslie gets tangled up, trying to win a rebound. She comes down at an awkward angle, her ankle giving way, just as her foot hits the ground and the resulting yelp of pain, silences the crowd.

    Lena flings out a hand, grabbing hold of Jack’s arm. Cat rushes forward to check on her player. Kara, Nia and Lucy are all crouching over Leslie and when Kara stands and turns away, Lena knows it’s bad.

    It takes a few minutes to get Leslie safely up and off the court. Once she’s carried away to receive medical attention, her teammates come back together in a huddle, with Kara leading the rallying call before the refs get play back underway.

    There’s a new force driving the Wildcat’s from that point on, they press every possession, are quick in transition and dominate in defence. The team edges out in front and with fifteen seconds on the clock, Kara moves down the court with the ball, trying to eat up the time, but Selina Kyle isn’t ready to give up, sticking out her right hand and somehow batting the ball out of Kara’s control. 

    She races up the court, goes in for the easy layup and then, from what seems out of nowhere, Kara’s hand comes up and blocks the shot. The roar she lets out is loud, matched by the crowd behind the bucket, but it’s not over. The Angels still have possession, but no time-outs and have to move quickly, inbounding the ball right into the waiting palms of Kyle, who shoots a fraction of a second before the buzzer sounds and as the ball rises up into the air, Lena holds her breath. It takes an age for the ball to come down, bouncing back up off the rim, until it falls a second time just wide and the arena erupts.

    Everything is a blur from that moment on, Jack picks Lena up, but her eyes are on Kara and the others as they let go of all the frustrated energy and allow themselves a moment to enjoy the win and the satisfaction of having booked their place in the finals.

    “They did it,” she says to Jack, still unable to get her head around what she’s just seen.

    “Oh, they really, really did,” he drops Lena back onto her feet and suddenly she’s being wrapped up in a group hug as Sam and Jess smash into them.

    There is no doubt, it’ll be a long night of celebrating for her friends and Lena joins them for a couple of drinks, knowing Kara will be caught up in enjoying the moment with her teammates, alongside her usual media obligations.

    Kara’s text arrives at the exact point, Jess tries to convince her to have another drink and as way of explanation, she waves her phone at her friend, “thanks to my brother, I’ve got to be up early.” 

    “Lex owes us all,” Sam slings an arm around Lena’s shoulders, still ridiculously giddy over the win. “I’m only tolerating this behaviour, because he’s got a nice jet.”

    “Uh huh,” Lena juts an elbow into Sam’s ribs. “Do not repeat those words to him, he’ll think it’s a proposition.”

    Sam’s eyes widen, then she’s laughing, “sorry, but no.”

    “No need to apologise, I’d definitely prefer it if none of my friends, tried to hook up with my big brother.”

    “It was an accident and I thought we weren’t going to mention that ever again,” Jack shoots Lena a wink.

    “You didn’t?” Jess says in surprise.

    Lena shakes her head, more than happy to leave Jack to explain that incident on his own.

    —

    Away from the crowds, she waits for Kara, watching the highlights of the earlier game on her iPad, before skipping through to the press conference and is halfway through listening to what Cat has to say about Leslie, when she hears a light tapping at her door.

    “Is she okay?” Lena asks the second she pulls it open.

    “She will be,” Kara says, through an exhale of breath. “It was…” she shakes her head, looking almost as pale as she had on the court earlier. “I…”

    “Hey, hey” Lena steps closer, her hands gripping Kara by her upper arms. “It’s going to be okay, she’ll be okay.”

    Kara gives her the sweetest smile in return, but Lena can see the tears forming behind her eyes, “sorry, just, it’s never good seeing a friend get hurt.”

    “Brings back memories?” Lena hedges, having heard how tough Kara’s journey has been, but knowing it’s very different to go through it.

    “Yeah, you know, not that it’s about me, but seeing her like that was,” Kara puffs out her cheeks, “she’s going to be okay, they hope it’s just a sprain, but…”

    “Fractures heal, scars remain,” Lena says and Kara closes her eyes. 

    “Recovery is tough,” Kara still looks lost.

    “She’s got you all to help her, she’s going to be okay. Are you?”

    “Yep, yeah, takes a bit to get my head around what happened out there and how easy it can all be taken away, but, we made the final,” Kara says, a half smile pulling at the corners of her mouth.

    “Yeah, you did. One step closer.”

    “I’m so scared,” Kara says in the next breath. “I’ve wanted this my whole life and now? What if?”

    Lena pulls her back into her chest and feels the way Kara’s body sags, letting the stress and pressure go for just a few moments.

    When she pulls back, she quickly wipes at her eyes, “sorry, sometimes all the emotion builds up.”

    “Understandable,” Lena runs her thumb gently across Kara’s cheek, wiping away the last traces of her tears. Kara leans into the touch. “I know it’s scary, but you’re going to go out there and do all you can to win this. Do it for you, for your teammates. All that you’ve been through. It’s got you to this moment and you will make it count on Sunday.”

    Kara’s smile broadens, “okay that was good, mind if I pinch it for my next team talk?”

    “Be my guest.”

    “You know, I don’t exactly know what you’re going to do after graduation, but I’m absolutely certain you’re going to be the very best at it.”

    “Running some sub-division at LuthorCorp,” Lena shrugs as they walk further in the room and Kara takes a seat on the couch. “Living the dream, playing by Lex’s rules.”

    “Why do it?” Kara asks, like Lena’s a choice. “I get that it’s your family business, but don’t you get a say in what you do?”

    “I suppose,” she says, knowing it’s crossed her mind a million times or more. The reality of who she is, what she wants to be and how she’d like to make a difference, not just at LuthorCorp, but in so many ways is what’s driven her all this time, not that she’s shared any of those hopes and dreams with her family. They have their own idea, a rigid path, a legacy to fulfil. She’s a feeling Kara would understand. “One day, maybe. You know how these things are, have to bide my time before I throw a curveball in over thanksgiving dinner.”

    Kara nods and Lena’s easily distracted, thinking about the current curveball she’d like to throw at this year’s event by bringing Kara with her. It seems so far away, but she can almost picture it.

    “Leslie’s not coming back tonight, got medical observations and…,” Kara fidgets with the sleeves on her jacket, looking up at Lena with a hopeful grin, “can I stay?”

    “Here?” Lena’s surprise, causes a small crease to appear between Kara’s eyes, which she quickly wants to erase. “Yes, of course, yes. You’re always welcome, but what about the rules, won’t Cat come looking?”

    “She’s got bigger things to worry about and a whole heap of plays to walk Nia through.”

    “One more game.”

    “One last game,” Kara says, with a wistfulness that catches Lena by surprise.

    It hits her then, the rumours, speculation and comments on some of Kara’s social media. It’s all making sense as she says, “you’re going into the draft. The draft, which happens to be next week.”

    “Yeah, crazy it’s all so quick. Haven’t exactly had much chance to think about it. What with all the games, but…” Kara’s looking at Lena strangely now. “Are you okay?”

    “Yes,” Lena’s voice comes out a little more shrill than she expected, she knows she sounds anything but ok.

    “Sure,” Kara pushes herself up off the couch, until she’s standing right in front of Lena. “We talked about this?”

    Lena’s pretty sure, they didn’t, but there’s also a chance that she tuned out during a few of the basketball related conversations. “I might have been distracted,” she admits.

    “Distracted?” Kara’s head tilts to one side, fully aware of what has had Lena distracted, whenever they’ve been alone. “I definitely talked to you, I’d just got back from the sportswear shoot,” she’s grinning now, stepping further into Lena’s space. “They’d taken me for a dinner…”

    “I remember,” Lena says, her voice as breathy as it had been the night Kara walked in wearing a white button up, tucked into black pants and, a loose black tie around her neck.

    “You need a reminder of the other things that happened that night?” Kara says and all talk of draft picks and final games are the last thing on Lena’s mind. She replies with a searing kiss and Kara makes good on reminding them both, just how much fun they’d had.

    Chapter End Notes

    Up next: one last shot at a title.

    Chapter 23: for the ring

    Chapter Summary

    Everything on the line and a championship to play for.

    Chapter Notes

    Kara leaves early the next morning, in hope of getting back to her room before anyone might come looking and Lena doesn’t even try to get anymore rest, instead she takes a shower and sets about preparing herself for the long day ahead.

    Knowing she won’t get through it, without at least three cups of coffee, she takes up Sam’s offer of meeting for breakfast and heads downstairs.

    “I don’t know how Kara does it, I’m so nervous,” Sam says, plonking two glasses of orange juice on the table. “How about you?”

    “Excited?” she says, not sure why, but there’s little she can do to affect the outcome of the game tomorrow and although Kara had a moment of vulnerability last night, for the most part she’s been cool about the basketball side of things.

    “I heard a rumour about Andrea being here,” Sam pops a forkful of pancake in her mouth and chews on it, whilst Lena thinks of how best to respond.

    “She’s engaged, Russ Rogers.”

    “The quarterback?” Sam’s eyes widen. “Guess that’s one way to salvage the family fortunes. You heard about the deal her dad made going sour?”

    Lena’s heard plenty about it, along with a few further comments from Lillian, during this morning’s video call, “my mother actually smiled.”

    “You Luthor’s are strange,” Sam jests, prodding her fork into a sausage. “You should eat too; Jack will have us all in the bar by lunch.”

    “Not for me today,” she says and it’s then that Sam takes in her outfit.

    “I did wonder why you looked all corporate, what’s today’s fun?”

    “Me standing awkwardly in a room full of basketball players,” Lena pulls a face.

    “You never look awkward,” Sam says softly.

    “In my business causal, whilst everyone else is in sweats?”

    “You’d still stand out, even if you were in sweats, own it.”

    Lena’s not all that comfortable with the reality that she somehow always stands out, but she takes Sam’s advice and twenty minutes later, she walks into the arena, like she owns it, which she possibly does, considering half the room has either the Luthor name or logo, firmly stamped on it.

    —

    It’s not long before she’s ambushed by a far too enthusiastic member of Lex’s publicity team, “Eve insisted I walked you through the day. We begin with…”

    Lena tunes out, she’s more than capable of handling whatever they throw at her. She’s less capable of doing it, with Kara Danvers smirking at her, from across the room.

    Although the distraction is more than welcome once she begins the tedium of talking to all the potential investors and people of note for the next hour. She’s then steered towards a group of reporters and gives a few words about LuthorCorp’s plans to invest in the growth of women’s sports. 

    Once she’s done, Lena stands to the edge of the court, arms folded across her chest as she watches Kara putting up a few shots. Kara shifts the ball from hand to hand, then turns on the spot, evading her defender and putting the ball up and into the bucket. It’s a sweet shot, one Kara would possibly call her favourite, and she follows it up, by winking at Lena as she jogs past her. The gesture, reminiscent of the time they worked on the outreach project and then visited a local community club. A day Lena never expected to happen in her life, where they bridged a gap between them and it set them on a course for everything that came after.

    The shoot around continues and Kara puts on a show, making ridiculous passes and running rings around the kids who desperately try to stop her.

    “Show off,” Lena says, just loud enough for Kara to hear, failing to hide her own smile when Kara looks back at her with a grin.

    “You love it,” she shouts back, getting hold of the ball again and twisting until she can get her feet planted, shooting a perfect 3 pointer, then coming back over. “Want a go?” She says, then asks the watching group, “you all wanna see Miss Luthor take a shot?”

    For some unknown reason, the kids around them, seem more than happy to see Lena do something she’s definitely incapable of and before she can put a stop to it, Kara’s handing her the ball and guiding her towards the free-throw line.

    “Kara, no,” she protests feebly, regretting baiting someone so competitive.

    “Come on, you know it’s all about fun at these things. Give it your best shot,” Kara’s hand is at Lena’s lower back, a calming touch, which has her planting her feet, much like Kara had done moments before.

    She takes a breath, twists the ball in her hands, feels her heart beating a little bit faster in her chest and then tosses the ball up towards the bucket. It hits the backboard, then clunks against the rim, before dropping through the net. Kara’s delighted laughter, is warm and rich, as she retrieves the ball, “damn, wish I’d known you could do that sooner.”

    “I’m a woman of many talents,” Lena says, a satisfied smirk on her face as Kara tosses the ball to one of the kids.

    “Yeah, yeah you are,” her words quieter now and for a second it’s like they’re the only two people in the room. It doesn’t last long.

    “Kara, Kara,” a young girl yells out from the sidelines. 

    Kara smiles over, then looks back at Lena, “that’s my cue. I’ll see you after my lunch thing?”

    “You will,” Lena’s already looking forward to it.

    —

    “I can’t stay,” Kara sighs, she’s still curled into Lena’s side, their clothes discarded all over Lena’s room, not caring about anything, but being close to one another the second she walked through the door. “Coach has a games night planned and then I’m going to need to rest.”

    “Save that energy for the game,” Lena runs her fingers down the side of Kara’s ribs, eliciting a shudder from the blonde, who nods in reply. “You feel good?”

    “I feel amazing,” Kara says. “More so after watching you take that shot earlier.”

    “You’re never going to forget it?”

    “Never,” Kara’s eyebrows shoot up. “Very hot, very awesome. Pretty girls who can shoot hoops are something else.”

    “If only I’d known how to get your attention,” Lena teases in reply.

    Kara’s hand comes to cover hers, holding it in place on her waist, “you always had my attention.”

    A comment like that one, has Lena kissing Kara with all she has and it’s another thirty minutes before Kara’s pouting and reluctantly pulling on her training pants. Lena follows her to the door, “this is it then?”

    “I guess so,” Kara lingers, taking Lena’s hand in her own and swinging it in the space between them, “big day tomorrow, if you hadn’t heard.”

    “Good luck, believe in yourself and enjoy it.”

    “I’m going to try.”

    “If it helps, I believe in you.”

    “It does,” Kara says, it’s then that she sucks in a breath, searching Lena’s eyes like she’s about to say something, but can’t find the words.

    Lena brushes her thumb across Kara’s cheek, smiling softly back at her, assuming the nerves of what lies ahead are finally getting to her and then she grips hold of the edges of Kara’s hoody, pulling her down until their lips meet, hoping it says all the words she can’t find. 

    “Good luck, Kara Danvers,” Lena says, through an exhale of breath. “Next time I kiss you, you’re going to be a National Champion.”

    Kara ducks her head, a rare show of bashfulness? “Extra incentive,” she says, blue eyes boring into Lena’s and making her heart do that wild little fluttery thing it does.

    “Always here to help,” she says and Kara’s gentle laughter fills the corridor as she heads towards the elevator.

    —

    After freshening up, Lena joins her friends for the evening, not surprised to find Jack is already out drinking.

    Sam’s in the midst of curling her hair, whilst Jess looks decidedly bored, sitting on the edge of the bed, scrolling through her phone, “Mike’s at Calhoun’s, says it packed, but he’s got a table.”

    Lena fails to hide a grimace, which causes Sam to chuckle, “why don’t you go and meet him and Lena can sit here and praise my relentless quest for perfect waves?”

    Jess leaps off the bed in an instant, “I’ll text Jack to meet us there,” she says, pulling the door closed without waiting for an answer.

    “And, now I have you alone,” Sam puts down the iron and grabs her phone, shoving it in front of Lena’s face a few seconds later.

    Lena watches a clip of her and Kara from earlier in the day and simply says, “work.”

    “Work?” Sam repeats, an accusatory tone that has Lena shoving the phone back towards her.

    “Yes, believe it or not.”

    Sam’s not satisfied, looking up something else on the phone and then handing it back to Lena, “this was on the NCU sports channel earlier.”

    Lena, then gets to witness her and Kara on the court, the free-throw and the resulting conversation. It’s easy to see why, Sam is giving her the look she is right now. “Kara got me to take a shot,” Lena shrugs.

    “She sure did,” Sam purses her lips, waiting for Lena to say something and when she’s met with silence, she huffs, “come on, you can’t tell me that’s not a thing,” she looks kind of confident, but as Lena stares back at her, the confidence fades. “Really?”

    Lena can’t keep hiding and she can’t outright lie to Sam, not anymore, she slowly shakes her head, unable to keep it to herself a second longer, “it’s something.”

    “Yes,” Sam literally punches the air, which leaves Lena slightly startled. “I knew it, but then you got all…” Sam’s talking to herself now, or at least Lena thinks she must be. “And oh, then I thought no way, because Lena, well you never go after what you actually want, so when Jess said it wasn’t and we all know Jack…” she stops talking and comes to face Lena. “How long?”

    “A while,” she admits. “Please don’t say anything.”

    “Why?”

    “Because what we’re doing works and I just…” Lena sighs, “I like it, no outside pressure.”

    “No Lillian?”

    “Mainly, yes,” Lena knows she can’t keep Kara a secret forever, but she’s not ready for the inevitable showdown with her mother, not just yet. “I don’t even know if Kara wants it to be that kind of a thing.”

    “Really?” Sam looks genuinely surprised. “I’ve seen you two together, I always thought she was crazy about you.” Which is news to Lena. “It’s why I thought there was something going on, but I don’t know, Jess kept telling me not to read too much into it, which was probably her deflecting with the whole Mike thing, because that’s still going on and definitely not a fling.”

    “Talking of, we’d better go and join them,” Lena starts towards the door and Sam grabs her purse, following close behind.

    “Which gives you a pass for tonight, but tomorrow, I want all the details.”

    “I promise.”

    It’s a relief to have someone else in on her secret and she will be forever grateful that Sam keeps to her promise, not mentioning it at all, even though the drinks keep flowing and she gets to enjoy a proper night out with her friends. It’s funny to think that basketball is the reason they are all here, having one last hurrah before college reaches its climax, but somehow it makes perfect sense.

    —

    Less than twenty four hours later, Sam has a few more details and a lot more questions to ask, but is happy to leave them for another day, because first, there’s a matter of a basketball game to get through and this one is the big one.

    With tip-off fast approaching, the TV crews are still on the court, catching players and coaches for pre-game interviews, whilst both teams get warmed up. The arena is packed with a mix of NCU and Bullhorns fans and it’s ridiculously loud as the excitement builds in the countdown to the final game of the NCAA championships. 

    Jack appears, just before the start, passing around drinks and taking his usual spot, right beside Lena. An unspoken superstition, that neither of them will acknowledge, but it’s been the same order, for the last three games and even before that, when it was just them on the couch. Jack to Lena’s left, always.

    Kara’s down on the court, going through her usual routine under the low lights, unbothered by all the commotion around her and focussed on the task ahead, because this is everything she’s worked for, this moment, this chance.

    It’s not until the team introductions begin, that Lena has to catch a breath, gripping hold of Jack’s hand. Kara gets the loudest reception and Lena’s not just being biased, Kara is the player everyone’s talking about and it’s more obvious as to why as the game gets underway.

    After a slow start, from both teams, it’s the Wildcats, who finally spark to life and this time out, they’re relentless. Led, by a fired up Kara, the team are chasing everything, challenging every shot, winning rebounds and forcing mistakes. It’s fast and frantic and the Bullhorns are already on the back foot, unable to handle the tempo.

    This game is nothing like the matchup with the Angels, with the Wildcats quickly going out in front and not letting up the pressure.

    Kara’s controlling the court, helped out by Nia, who has adapted to the changes made in Leslie’s absence and is having the night of her life. She’s currently on a hot-streak bucketing every three, she attempts and then working back to defend the arc. 

    There’s no stopping Lucy either, she’s living up to her reputation as a demon in defence, pressing every single play and coming up with steal after steal. 

    Everything the Wildcats do, pays off, whereas the Bullhorns can’t find an answer, desperately trying to retain possession and conceding too many fouls, leaving their coach looking up to the skies in despair.

    During a break in play, Lena catches Andrea looking right at her. There’s a flash of recognition and then Andrea’s eyes drift down, taking in the jersey Lena’s wearing, before she leans into Russ Rogers’ side and whispers something in his ear. 

    Right on cue, the Jumbotron cuts to the couple and Andrea plays up to being in the spotlight, jutting out her hand to show off the huge ring on her finger. Lena glances to where Sam is desperately trying not to laugh.

    “Classy,” Jack mutters into his glass.

    Lena can’t care, her focus returns to the court and Kara’s dominant performance. It’s no surprise to watch her pull away from her guard and dribble into the paint. She makes for a jump shot, gets hit by a flailing arm, just as she releases the ball and the foul is called, before Kara hits the hardwood. 

    It’s in the aftermath, as Kara lays on the ground, her fists clenched as she yells, “and fucking one!” That Lena knows they won’t lose.

    Lucy darts forward, yells back, “hell, yeah,” then pulls Kara to her feet.

    Kara turns to the crowd behind the bucket, amping them up, she roars back at them, clenches her fists and flexes. It’s an image that won’t quickly fade from Lena’s memory.

    Kara takes a beat, moving to the free-throw line, receiving the ball from the ref, spinning it in her hands as she looks up at the bucket, then swiftly takes the shot. It’s good. 

    The Wildcats have a fifteen point lead. They don’t let it slip.

    When Cat subs Kara out, with only a minute left to play, the whole arena is on its feet. Lena’s got a tear in her eye and a huge grin on her face as she watches Kara trudge off, landing in Cat’s arms as the pair share a warm embrace. The release of pressure, evident in the way Kara’s shoulders heave. She turns to her teammates, walking down the line and embracing every single one of them. This is it. Kara’s last game for NCU and it’s exactly what she’s always dreamed of. 

    Kara Danvers is a National Champion and the Wildcats will lift the trophy.

    The clock ticks to zero, Lena has tears falling down her face, she takes one look at Jack and it makes it worse. The sense of pride, in seeing a group of people, they all genuinely care about, achieve the thing they’ve always dreamed of, is beyond anything she’d ever imagined and she’s glad to have her best friends by her side, hugging her tightly, whilst Kara drinks in the moment of her triumph with the teammates, coaches and managers that got them there.

    The celebrations continue on the court, cannons blast confetti up into the air, a trophy is lifted, players and coaches are interviewed and then Kara finally finds Lena in the crowd. 

    She has a word with James, who climbs into the stands to give them passes, enabling them to come down onto the court. 

    Lena’s about a metre away from Kara, when Lucy Lane suddenly rushes forward, hugging her tightly. Kara’s face lights up in amusement as she watches and once Lena is finally free from Lucy’s embrace, she steps towards Kara, only for Holly to beat her to it, pulling Kara away for another interview.

    —

    “They’re with me,” Kara says, the second she’s finished with her interview and spots Jess and Sam being blocked from getting any closer by the security team. 

    Lena’s hovering a few steps behind, her eyes meet Kara’s and it’s an effort to not walk over and land in her arms. She can’t though, because the second they’re let past the barriers, Sam and Jess beat her to it.

    She waits her turn, a smile on her face which grows the second Kara frees herself and moves towards Lena. 

    “You did it,” she says, unable to disguise the wonderment in her voice.

    “We did,” Kara sounds like she can’t quite believe it, either.

    Lena tugs at the shirt Kara’s got on, the one with National Champions printed in bold on the front. Kara glances down and smiles, like it’s almost starting to register, “you earned it, fought for it, made it yours.”

    “You’re gonna make me cry,” Kara’s bottom lip wobbles then and Lena quickly wraps her arms around her. Holding her close as Kara lets it all sink in. It’s a quiet moment, amongst all the madness. The two of them, shielded from all that is going on around them, by the circle of players and friends close by. Lena doesn’t see the way Sam steps in front of a camera, or how Jess gets the other girls together to create a distraction, blocking them from view. All Lena does, is hold Kara.

    “I’d really like to kiss you right now,” Kara says and god, does Lena want her to. She’d love to be anywhere but on this court, but it’s not about her. This is Kara’s moment and she won’t get in the way of it.

    “Me too,” she says, giving Kara’s bicep a gentle squeeze.

    “Who’d have thought secret hookups came with complications,” Kara says, her lips curling into a soft smile.

    “Cut the net!” Comes a cry from behind them.

    “Go, enjoy it,” Lena says. “Kiss me later?”

    Kara winks, then says, “count on it.“

    The words hit low in Lena’s belly and she can’t wait for that moment to come, but first she gets to watch Kara and her teammates climb the ladder to cut the net, much to the delight of all the people still huddled around them in the arena.

    —

    The celebrations continue, with all the friends and family waving the players off to the locker room, before spilling out of the arena and onto the afterparty. 

    When Kara arrives a short time later, she’s got the net around her neck, her championship baseball cap is turned to the side and the trophy is still being tightly held in her arms. Lena’s chest swells as she watches her from across the room, unable to stop the smile on her face as she does.

    It’s a night to remember and one that Lena’s glad to be part of. No-one questions her presence, or looks twice when she dances beside Kara and the others or when they find a quiet spot at the edge of the room where Kara tells Lena how happy she is, their hands linked and their bodies close. It’s not quite as close as either of them wishes to be and the urge to be alone becomes stronger as the night progresses.

    Lena’s on her way back from the bathroom, when Kara pushes herself from off the wall and takes her by the hand. “Want to sneak out?” she says, not waiting for Lena’s reply and leading her towards the stairwell.

    They’re barely through Lena’s door, before Kara finally kisses her. She uses the back of her heel to kick the door shut and is undoubtedly, full of enough adrenaline to keep them both up until the early hours of the next morning, if the way she makes quick work of relieving Lena of all her clothing is anything to go by. 

    Lena tries to do the same in return, tugging at the net still hanging around Kara’s neck, “a priceless piece of jewellery?”

    “Not taking it off,” Kara’s words are a little slurred, but her sentiment is clear.

    “Maybe for safety reasons,” Lena says, gently lifting it over Kara’s head, without protest, and hanging it over the back of a chair. “You can wear it all day tomorrow, but tonight—”

    Her words are cut off by Kara’s lips meeting hers in, a wet, messy kiss, “waited too long for this, better than a net,” Kara says, taking a second to breathe, her eyes searching Lena’s.

     Lena wraps her hands around the back of Kara’s neck, smiling up at her, “finally, a kiss from my champion.”

    “Mm, never going to get tired of hearing you call me that,” her lips move to Lena’s neck.

    Lena throws her head back, “Kara,” she says, half plea, half whimper.

    “Thing about taking home a trophy,” Kara says, running her tongue up towards Lena’s ear, briefly capturing the lobe between her teeth. “Makes me want to celebrate,” her words send a pulse of heat right to Lena’s core. “You want to celebrate, Lena?”

    Lena may somehow say the word, yes, but she’s not sure, because Kara’s already backing her towards the bed and spends the next hour, showing Lena exactly how good it feels to be a champion.

    Chapter End Notes

    Up next; The morning after. The draft. New beginnings.

    Chapter 24: centre stage

    Chapter Summary

    Being a Luthor. facing a future.

    Chapter Notes

    Lena’s not sure they’ve even had two hours sleep, when Kara’s phone wakes them. She rolls onto her side, watching Kara, whose face is lit up by the phone screen, as she scrolls through her messages, typing back a few rapid replies, “busy day?”

    “Looks like I’m booked up for the week, no-one’s factoring in, that I might need some time to enjoy what we just did,” Kara sighs, tossing the phone back onto the nightstand. She’s quiet a second, then turns to face Lena. “Come to the draft with me,” she says, like it’s the answer to whatever is going on in her mind.

    “Wouldn’t that be a sight,” Lena chuckles, assuming it’s a joke, until she sees the look on Kara’s face.

    “Would it?” Kara tilts her head to one side. “Like, really? What’s the worst that could happen?”

    Lena mirrors Kara’s pose, “you know exactly what it would lead to. The draft is your night, if I sit down to the table, the conversation changes. Trust me, I’ve seen it before. People would want to know, why I’m there? Who I am to you?” There’s a huge part of her that wants to know the answer to both of those questions, but Kara’s not looking at her, misses the hopefulness in her eyes as she speaks.

    “So, this is about being a Luthor?” Kara’s brow knits together. 

    “What else would it be about?” Lena had thought Kara understood the pressure that comes with having a name like hers, which was perhaps foolish. She’s had a lifetime of knowing the inner workings of her own family and still doesn’t get it.

    Kara shakes her head, “nothing, I’m just being…” she shrugs. “I guess I wanted to cling on to the moment, next week is just…” she puffs out a breath, looking overwhelmed at the prospect.

    “It’s all going to be fine. You’re going to go number one, I’m sure of it.”

    “It’s not, I…” Kara pushes the covers off and climbs out of bed, padding towards the bathroom, before she turns back to look at Lena. “This is it, Lena. In a week, it’s not only the conversation that’s going to change, it’s everything. Last night was the last time I play with my team and I don’t know if I’m ready to say goodbye yet, not to them,” she’s even quieter when she says, “not to you.”

    “Oh,” Lena’s perhaps naively been walking into this stage of Kara’s life, but the reality that it’s all but reached its conclusion, is slowly dawning on her. “You’re leaving,” she says and Kara’s head bobs in reply. Lena’s definitely not ready for this moment either and has to think quickly for a solution. “Well,” she says, trying to sound a lot more chilled about it, then she truly is. “I might not be able to sit beside you at the draft, but what do you say we make the most of the time we have up until then?”

    “I’d like that,” Kara’s resulting smile, has Lena climbing out of the bed and crossing the room towards her. She cups her face with both hands, searching her eyes for a moment, then kisses her gently on the lips. 

    “Me too,” she says and it’s enough, for now. 

    Kara pulls Lena towards the bathroom, “how about we start, by making the most of this huge shower?”

    —

    Kara’s schedule is chaotic in the coming days. Full of tv appearances and radio interviews. Recording my thoughts, the good days, the bad ones,” she says with a heaviness that makes her shoulders slump. “Sometimes I didn’t think I’d get back to playing, so to be going out there tomorrow, having won the Natty, it’s…” she swallows thickly, biting back some of the emotion that washes over her, but not capable of hiding all of it.

    “Yeah,” Kara’s lips curl at the edges.

    Kara’s bottom lip wobbles, “stop making me cry, Luthor,” she chuckles wetly, pulling Lena into her arms and holding her tight for just a minute.

    It won’t be long until Lena takes a return flight to NCU, whilst Kara soars into the sky, chasing her future, so Lena holds her back, just as tightly, because this is what it’s all been about. Tomorrow, the culmination of all Kara’s hard work, is about to meet its conclusion and Lena’s happy she gets to be there after all.
