    Chapter 26: power

    Chapter Summary

    The draft. New Beginnings. Changes.

    Chapter Notes

    Alone in the suite, Lena gets to work on making herself look presentable for the evening ahead, but it feels strange to get ready on her own, having spent so much time with Kara lately. She’s somehow found herself wrapped up in the life of a college basketball star, albeit without the gruelling schedule and psychical demands of training and playing. She’s flown around the country and lived the game, off the court. Being there in the quiet moments and she’s liked it. The ease of it all. No pressure and no comparison to when she’s expected to represent her family. She’s been allowed to exist in the shadows, whilst Kara’s light has shone brightly and tonight it’ll be at its brightest.

    At the same time, Lena’s foray into anonymity at NCU is most definitely over, thanks to Lex’s assistant putting out a press release about her attendance at tonights event, alongside a few notes detailing Lena’s future responsibilities within LuthorCorp. It’s nothing she’s not talked over with Lex before, but the announcement changes things. Her position is now official and it’s why, the moment she steps out of the car, her photo is taken and several reporters approach, asking a few questions about LuthorCorp’s investment in the event and the direction they’ve taken backing sport in recent months. She’s aware, this is exactly what Lex wants. Lena is to act as a figurehead in this venture of his. He believes having a woman, showcasing the Luthor name, whilst supporting female sports, is good optics. Lena agrees with him, to some extent, and answers the smattering of questions with the practiced poise she’s been trained to exude.

    There’s no fighting it, she always knew this is what would happen the second she accepted her fate. Being a Luthor, comes with the knowledge that every decision she makes, every word she says and opinion she gives, will be scrutinised by the press, those that take exception to how her family built their fortune and any competitor looking for an edge, at the least.

    In truth, Lena’s never truly been anonymous, even with a few years away from the Luthor machine. Her absence, temporary. An accommodation, reluctantly afforded to her by a frustrated Lillian. Something she’s reminded of on every visit she makes to the mansion and at regular intervals during their catchup calls. 

    Avoidance was a luxury, but she is Lena Luthor and if she wants to be the one who calls the shots, she has to be in the game.

    —

    Away from the camera flashes, she’s greeted by a cacophony of noise in the presentation area, where everyone is already filing in to take their seats. 

    The centre of the room is filled with tables, all set up for the draftees and their guests, whilst members of the public sit further back, having paid to come and watch the spectacle. To the side of the room, is a row of chairs and at the very front row, sit Kara’s teammates, all chatting, waiting until the big moment. Nia waves to Lena, but before Lena gets the chance to go and say hello, she hears the distinct sound of her older brother’s laughter.

    Searching him out in the crowd isn’t very hard, he’s centre stage of course, the real surprise comes in the fact that he’s got his arm looped in Selina Kyle’s. Lena’s considering finding a corner to hide in, when Lex spots her, “Lena!” He enthusiastically calls out, making heads turn, although very few find interest in the appearance of an heiress, when the room is full of up and coming basketball stars. Lex makes a show of zigzagging past the tables, bringing Selina with him. 

    “It’s been too long,” Lena kisses his cheek in greeting.

    “I know, video calls aren’t the same, how’s my baby sister doing?”

    “I’m well, I’ve a few things—”

    “You’ve met Selina?” Lex cuts her off, his eyes bright as he smiles at the basketball player beside him.

    “I’d remember,” Selina offers out her hand and Lena shakes it. “I’d have signed your contract a lot sooner, if you’d been the one delivering the paperwork.”

    “Something, I should know?” Lena turns to Lex, her eyebrow raised, not missing how pleased with himself he looks.

    “You know how these things are, Lena. We need a player who wants to represent the Luthor name. I found one.”

    Lena’s eyes dart towards Kara’s table, she’s not there yet, but Cat is close by, talking to a tall, blonde lady.

    “And Kara?” Lena asks, turning to face Lex, wondering how it can be so easy that he’s moved on with little fuss.

    “Never put all your eggs in one basket, her team flat out refused to speak to me, I’m not chasing,” he says, eyes roaming the room, lighting up once he spots his target. “Oh, look, it’s the commissioner, let’s get a team photo,” he’s eagerly pulling Selina along with him. 

    Lena watches her brother for a startled minute, unsurprised at how he seamlessly fits into this world.

    —

    It’s then that Kara appears, she’s dressed in a sparkling black pant suit, her hair down in flowing waves and when she catches Lena watching her from across the room, she stops walking, her lips parting in an unspoken, “oh.”

    Which is most definitely the reaction Lena had wanted, when she slipped into the figure hugging deep emerald dress she’d deliberately chosen with Kara in mind.

    It’s quite satisfying to be looked at in the way Kara is looking at her right now. A stunned surprise, a lingering heat. Lena’s heart picks up a beat, her chest rising and falling and Kara’s tracking the motion, like no-one else is in the room. 

    Lena closes the distance between them, “hi.”

    “Hi,” Kara says, sounding out of breath, her hands twitching at her sides. “You look beautiful. I didn’t expect to see you just yet or that wow, you…this…wow, it’s a gorgeous dress.”

    “Thank you and I love this on you,” Lena takes a step closer, her fingertips dancing along the lapel of Kara’s jacket. “Kara, I—”

    Kara takes a tiny step back, eyes wide with alarm, as she covers her chest and whispers, “I’m mic’d up.”

    Lena spots the wire and god, why does everything have to be so complicated? Kara’s of course being followed throughout the night. Every interaction, every person she talks to. There’s probably a camera on them, right now.

    “Absolutely,” Lena squeaks out in a pitch that she’s not sure she’s ever achieved before, it has Kara grinning back at her. She’s no idea what to do, and for some reason the next words slip from her mouth in some kind of faux posh British accent, “And good luck. All of us, from NCU, are rooting for you.”

    Kara does a valiant effort to not laugh in reply, “I’m so happy to have…” she shakes her head, her eyes sparkling with amusement. “…all of NCU on my team.”

    It’s really quite annoying that Kara is always somehow so perfect in all these stupid encounters of theirs and Lena really wants to kiss the smirk off her face, but there are camera’s and then there’s Lex. His obnoxious laugh carries across the room and Lena has to fight the urge to glare at him. Kara instantly picks up on her unease, following her eye-line to where Lex is still chatting to the commissioner, Selina Kyle at his side, “didn’t know your bro knew SK.”

    “Me either,” Lena says flatly, still surprised she wasn’t given an update seeing as she’s supposed to be the one pushing this agenda, “although it deals with one of our problems.”

    “Sorry about all that,” Kara says, keeping it as vague as can be when they know they’re being listened to, she jabs a thumb towards the table across the room, “So, uh, I’d better go sit, they’ll be starting soon.”

    “Sure,” Lena’s no idea what she’s supposed to do now. She watches Kara cross the room, warmly greeting Cat, her sister and mom and all Lena can do is observe from the sidelines, much like she did in all those basketball games, because she’s not here with Kara, she’s her secret lover and that’s all. Lena’s here for business. Kara’s family don’t even know who she is and it all feels a lot more complicated all over again.

    Lex sees her looking incredibly useless and beckons her over to the table he’s already seated at, alongside Selina Kyle, her coach and father. It feels so wrong to be there, but unless she stands up and declares her feelings for Kara in a room full of people, there’s little reason for her to get up and leave.

    The conversation around her is all about opportunities, contracts, development and she should probably pay some attention, but she can’t stop looking over to where Kara is sitting.

    Even from a distance she can spot the tell-tale signs of how nervous Kara is, her leg bounces beneath the table and Lena wishes she could be at her side, with the freedom to place her hand on Kara’s thigh, to whisper a few words to calm her, but she can’t and her own nerves build in return as the moment draws ever closer.

    Lex is talking about doing a reel for Selina’s instagram followers, when Kara’s head turns and she hones right in on Lena. For a second her brow furrows, once she registers the other occupants at Lena’s table and then she sucks in a breath. Alex leans into say something and Kara’s focus returns to the stage where the commissioner is now having her microphone pack fitted.

    It takes too long to get to the part they’ve all been waiting for and as the commissioner starts talking, Lena spots the way Kara’s sister, places a steadying hand on her back. 

    “With the first pick of the WNBA draft….”

    Lena holds her breath, hears the words spoken, even though her brain can’t process them. Kara’s slow to get to her feet, hugging her family first, then Coach, then she begins to walk towards the stage. There’s a smattering of applause from the tables around her and a muttered, “should’ve been ours,” from someone sitting to Lena’s left. 

    And, then there’s all the NCU girls, cheering the loudest of all. Lucy and Nia are on their feet. Leslie, who is on crutches, makes an attempt to clap, but it’s a struggle and then Lena catches the way Lex twists his glass of champagne between his palms, 

    He gives Lena a closed mouthed smile, then says, “she can go number one, but good luck to her. That team is going to need all the KD magic they can muster to even get a win. Put your money on the rookie of the year, being the girl at this table,” he raises his glass towards Selina, who looks a little sour over not being number one, but quickly forces a smile to her face.

    Lena’s attention turns back to Kara, who is now climbing the steps, having waved towards her teammates. The commissioner is beaming, when she hands over the jersey with a number one printed on the back. It will soon bear Kara’s name, the camera flashes take over for a few minutes, before the screen behind them, plays back an edit of all Kara’s highlights.

    “Can we get a few words?” Holly asks, holding a microphone in front of Kara’s face, once she takes a step off the stage. She does a quick interview, asking how it feels, whilst the images continue to play out on the screen. 

    Kara holds it together, for the most part, talking about how it’s taken a huge effort from behind the scenes to get her to this point. She thanks the staff, her teammates, “all the people rooting for me back at NCU,” her eyes meet Lena’s. “I’d not be here, if they didn’t believe in me and I owe my tutor a huge debt of gratitude for not giving up on me when it might have looked like I was only interested in playing with big orange balls,” there’s a smattering of laughter around the room, Lena licks her lips, a smile on her face. She’s never felt more proud of Kara than she does now, but the whole occasion is a lot more overwhelming than she’d imagined.

    “That’s something I admire about you, “ Holly says, “You could have made tonight all about you, but you are constantly making sure the people who helped you along the way, get the recognition.”

    “They’ve changed my life, I couldn’t have got here without them,” Kara’s voice cracks and Holly gives her shoulder a rub, smiling as she changes the subject.

    “We saw the watch party, they are obviously pumped to have you. How excited are you about starting a new life?”

    “It’s still surreal, but yeah, I can’t wait. I want to do the best I can. Let’s get it.”

    “Let’s get it,” Holly nods, “thank you, Kara.”

    Kara is whisked away, undoubtedly to give more interviews and soundbites, whilst the draft continues around them. Number two is a girl Lena’s never heard of and she fidgets with the tablecloth, not all that interested in who else goes where, only wanting to see Kara and say congratulations.

    —

    The opportunity arises a few minutes later, when Kara walks past their table and Lex gets to his feet, “Miss Danvers, congratulations on going number one.”

    “Thank you,” Kara says, shaking Lex’s offered hand.

    It takes Lena a second to catch up, she copies Lex’s gesture, holding out her hand for Kara to shake, “yes, well done, Kara. Never in doubt.”

    Kara smiles warmly, “like I said, all down to everyone at NCU rooting for me.”

    “Can’t say I’m not disappointed, I’d hoped Lena’s tuition could pave a way for us to work together,” Lex ruins the smile-off and Lena drops Kara’s hand.

    “Sorry about that,” Kara gives Lex a practiced smile, which turns fonder, once she turns her focus back to Lena, “although, I’ll always be grateful to Lena for helping me graduate.”

    “You really didn’t need all that much help,” Lena blushes and it’s some relief that Lex isn’t watching her too closely. “Just a nudge.”

    “A bit more of a nudge and Kara might be working with you, next year,” Lex scoffs, not hiding his frustration. “Missed a big chance, Lena, but at least I managed to step in and ensure we have Selina on our team, expect big things from that one.”

    Lex’s focus is on the other side of the room, where Selina is being interviewed. She’s wearing a baseball cap, with her new teams name on and is milking the moment for all it’s worth.

    “I look forward to when we play each other,” Kara follows his gaze, her answer is forced and Lena longs to move closer, to congratulate her properly, but she holds back.

    “I’m sure we’ll see you at a few games this season,” Lex adds, not really paying Kara much attention, now that his interest lies elsewhere. “If you’ll excuse us,” he says, motioning for Lena to join him. “We need to have a few words with the commissioner,” he’s walking away, but pauses at Lena’s hesitation. “Lena?”

    She can’t care about Lex or what else he might have to say to the commissioner, but knows she can’t get out of her responsibilities. She takes a quick breath, wishes she could convey to Kara how much she hates this part of her life, “time to be a Luthor,” she sighs.

    Kara chuckles, her eyes crinkling around the edges, “enjoy.”

    “You know I won’t,” Lena gives a wry smile.

    Kara nods, her eyes darting to where she still has a mic attached, “you’ll be at the party?” 

    “I will,” she says.

    “Then I’ll see you later.”

    Lena quickly catches up to Lex, earning a look of distain from him when she does.

    “You talk too much and don’t get enough done,” Lex huffs.

    “You sound like our mother,” Lena bites back and it draws an amused chuckle from him.

    She looks back over her shoulder, catches Kara watching from across the room, an unreadable expression on her face and again wishes, this wasn’t how this part of the night had to play out.

    —

    Finally, having listened to the commissioner and Lex trying to concoct a plan to increase the popularity of the WNBA, in order to bring it on par with the college game, she says her goodbyes and escapes to the party, being hosted by one of Kara’s sponsors.

    The place is loud and Lena instantly spies the entire Wildcat team, huddled together on the dance floor, bouncing up and down, hands in the air as the music plays.

    The bittersweet realisation that this is Kara’s send off, hits Lena hard and she diverts her path, straight towards the bar. 

    She’s halfway through a glass of wine, when Kara says from behind her, “you can’t hide here all night.” 

    She’s close enough that Lena can feel the warmth of her body and aches to lean back into it.

    “Oh, I don’t know, it’s a free bar,” she says, smiling at Kara’s reflection. “And, you know I don’t dance.”

    “Make an exception?” Kara’s palm grazes the side of Lena’s waist, tempting as always.

    Her body craves Kara’s touch and it’s why she instinctively turns to face her, not caring how she may look on the dance floor for once, “okay.”

    Kara takes her by both hands, walking backwards towards where everyone else is dancing, not dropping her gaze once. It’s a moment in the chaos. Kara holding her, moving their bodies as one, whilst the world revolves around them.

    There’s no camera’s here. No microphone hidden beneath Kara’s shirt, it’s just the team, their friends and Lena lets go of everything, that would usually hold her back. She dances with Kara, sings along to the songs, with the rest of team, even if she gets half the words wrong, not that anyone cares. This is a night for them all. Where they get to wish Kara well and celebrate all they’ve achieved together. 

    It’s Kara’s night and she parties, until there are only a handful of people left in the room and the music softens. For a few minutes, Lena sits to one side, watching Kara say those last goodbyes. Hugging her friends and teammates, making promises of futures, yet to be realised, before having a brief chat with Meg and then she turns to Lena with a soft smile, “ready to go home?” 

    Lena slips in snuggly under Kara’s arm, tightly wrapping into her side as they make their way out to the waiting car, sliding into the backseat and having one last night of their own.

    There’s something different in the way Kara’s looking at her when they enter their room and she feels it in every touch, in the soft brush of fingers over her skin and can hear it, in every whisper and each kiss. It’s gentle, tender, like Kara’s committing every action to memory and Lena’s doing the same in return.  No rush, just them. Kara peppering her body with soft kisses. It’s delicate, fragile.

    —

    The morning light fills the room, signalling a new beginning for them both, not that either of them says anything about it. 

    Kara keeps busy, making an attempt to reply to the messages she’s had overnight and Lena quietly packs her things, all set to check out and fly back to NCU, alone. She stalls as much as she can, not ready for this moment, wishing it wasn’t a goodbye.

    Kara’s quieter than usual, even when she suggests they go out for breakfast, leaving the packed bags behind them and not focusing on the inevitable. It’s a beautiful day, the sun is bright and it would normally be enough to put a smile on Lena’s face, but she can’t quite see the joy in it, not when Kara’s reading over her schedule for the coming days.

    They’re back in the hotel room, when it really hits Lena, “you just leave? Your home, all of it, everything?” The silent, “everyone,” sits heavily between them.

    Kara shakes out her shoulders, sucks in a breath and puts on a smile. It’s not unlike her free-throw routine, the seconds she takes to relax her body, eye on the bucket, blocking out all the outside noise, resetting herself. “Yep,” she says, the only sign of tension, coming in the way she tightens her jaw, even as she casually shrugs. “My team fix me up with a place to stay and Alex is coming down for a few days to help out.”

    Lena can’t picture this new life of Kara’s and she isn’t sure she wants to, not yet. “I guess I thought…” she shakes her head, it doesn’t matter what she thought. Kara’s no longer a college student. She’s about to start a real job and it’s all coming in fast. “It’s quick, is all.” She says and Kara nods, turning back to her bags.

    “Yep, kind of crazy I know. Meg’s taking care of a lot of the details, but basically I touch down and then we get set for the official unveil. There’s some meet and greets and a load of press obligations, right through to the start of the season,” Kara’s got her back to Lena, stuffing a few last things into her carry-on. “And a whole lot of practice in between. New start and a new team. We’ve got to learn how we play together, which is going to be the biggest challenge.”

    “A new life,” Lena’s standing in the middle of their hotel room, feeling like everything she ever wanted is slipping through her fingers and she’s no idea how to stop it. “They’re really invested in you?”

    “Mmhmm,” Kara hums, zipping up the bag. “This is how it is, they want to build the franchise around me,” she turns to look at Lena, shrugs a shoulder, like it’s that simple and Lena should understand, but how can she? None of it makes sense. Lena’s never really thought about what is expected of a first pick player.

    “Do you even know anyone?” she says, still stunned by the chain of events.

    “Played against a few of the girls, met the coach and GM, but yeah, it’s all new.”

    “Right,” Lena sits down on the edge of the bed, wondering why she’d not spent a minute to think about what Kara’s about to do, what she will become. It’s a huge deal, to leave all her friends, her family, start a new life in a city, over a thousand miles away. A thousand miles from Lena. “And you’re okay with this?”

    “It’ll be fine,” Kara says, coming to sit beside Lena. “I’ll be fine,” she takes a breath, almost like she’s convincing herself more than Lena. “They’re really throwing everything behind me. I can have Al come visit whenever she’s free and I’ll be traveling half the time. It’d be cool if you could make it to a game? I can get you seats,” Kara says, then seems to catch herself. “I know you’re going to be tied up with all the Luthor stuff, but maybe?”

    “Maybe,” Lena says, having absolutely no idea how or when she’d be able to get to see Kara or what it would even mean for them. “I’d like to. I…” she can’t promise, not when going to a game isn’t as easy as walking across campus anymore.

    “Different worlds,” Kara says in understanding.“Maybe it’s for the best, Meg thinks the next few months are going to be a lot more intrusive than what it was like at NCU. Everyone is going to be watching what I do, who I do it with.”

    The very idea of it, has Lena drawing in a sharp breath and it’s really not going to be much different for her either, not after Lex’s assistant put out that statement, “I can imagine.”

    “You know, I’m glad you were there last night,” Kara gives her this lopsided smile.

    “Me too, one thing to be thankful to Lex for,” she jokes and Kara’s eyes crinkle at the edges, but her smile quickly fades.

    “These last few months, this thing with us,” Kara says, her eyes searching Lena’s. “You had fun?”

    “I did,” Lena nods, “you?”

    “A lot,” Kara answers in a heartbeat.

    Her chest heaves when she sees the look in Kara’s eyes, knows this is it, the moment they’ve both been putting off for as long as they could, “God, this feels awful.”

    Kara takes hold of Lena’s hand, “I’m going to miss you.”

    “Me too,” Lena nods, “we’ll stay friends?”

    “I’ll always be your friend, Lena. I promise,” Kara says, quietly. “But, I’m also going to leave now.” 

    “Do you have to?”

    It’s then that Lena catches the tears threatening to spill from Kara’s eyes. She nods, swallows thickly, “for us both, yes.”

    Lena lets go of Kara’s hand, unable to move as she watches her pick up her bags and walk towards the door. Kara pauses, and then in one breath she’s turning, crossing the room and kissing Lena. It’s as pained, as it is beautiful. A goodbye, which leaves Lena shaking. 

    She can barely breathe as Kara turns for a second time. This time she doesn’t pause at the door, just tugs it open, then closes it behind her. 

    Lena sinks back down onto the bed, feeling like she’s just said goodbye to the one good thing she’s ever had in her life, not knowing a way to make their ending any different.

    Despite knowing this was always how it would end, she’s left wondering why it feels like the word, “stay,” is trying to claw its way out of her mouth, but Kara can’t stay and Lena can’t follow, she’s her own future to build and sitting court-side is not on the Luthor agenda.

    Chapter End Notes

    I know, I do. From the very beginning, they were always going to have to say goodbye.

    Chapter 27: Out of state, out of mind?

    Chapter Summary

    Time goes by, distance is a factor

    Chapter Notes

    I'm sorry if I made you cry. I sent hugs and got to work. figured you'd prefer a speedier update than usual.

    Back in National City, it’s a short ride to the house she shares with Sam, Jack and Jess and much like on the flight, she spends the whole time looking out the window, thinking of Kara, wondering where she is right now. It feels so empty to be in this city without her and it feels even worse to find no-one else is home. 

    Dropping her bags near the doorway, she opens the fridge, finds nothing that could take this feeling away and turns to a tried and tested coping mechanism, opening the first bottle of wine she finds in her room, traipsing back downstairs to pour a huge glass and then she scrolls through her photos, allowing herself a few more hours to wallow, before she has to say her own goodbyes.

    Jack walks in an hour later, freezes at the sight of her, slumped over the kitchen counter, halfway through the bottle and places his hand across his chest, “oh love, what happened?”

    “It’s harder to say goodbye, than I thought,” Lena admits and it takes Jack a second to work it all out.

    “To Kara?” He says, like he’s answering his own question.

    “To Kara,” Lena replies, feeling a twinge of guilt for hiding things for so long. “I was going to tell you, it was just…” she’s not entirely sure how to finish her sentence. They kept a secret for so many reasons, but the wine has fogged her brain and she can’t remember one good one.

    Jack grabs the bottle and pours himself a glass, “probably wise that you didn’t. I don’t think I’m capable of keeping secrets, especially ones that are as juicy as Lena Luthor banging the hot basketball player. I’m right aren’t I? You and Kara?” Lena nods. “Lena finally had hot, wild sex and somehow, she kept it a secret?”

    “Jack,” she gives him a shove, but he’s grinning at her in that lovely way of his and it’s that smile, which has her choking back a sob.

    He wraps her up in his arms and she clings on, allows herself this moment, because tomorrow she has to move on. It’s the only way.

    Once she sits back in her seat, a freshly filled glass of wine in front of her, it all feels a little more bearable.

    “Did she cheat on you?” Jack asks, his eyes narrowing suddenly. 

    “No, she, no,” Lena shakes her head vehemently.

    “But you broke up?” She can’t miss the traces of pity in his voice and it stings.

    “Can you breakup with someone, if you’re not dating?” Lena takes a breath and then another sip of wine. It does the trick of tempering down her emotions, albeit momentarily.

    “So, what were you doing?” He sounds almost as confused as Lena feels.

    Lena thinks for a moment, then remembers something, Kara once said, “hanging out?” 

    “Hmm,” Jack studies her curiously, swirling the red liquid around his glass. “But this has been going on a while, right?”

    “A few months,” Lena exhales slowly.

    “But not dating,” he affirms.

    “We’ve spent the last few weeks practically living together and then, the draft comes and she says goodbye, like it had always been the plan.”

    “Wasn’t it?”

    The question lingers between them, until Lena finally shrugs, “I never thought that far ahead. We agreed on casual and discreet, neither of us wanted outside noise. My mom, Kara’s fan’s.”

    “Can’t say I blame you,” Jack drains almost half his glass. “I saw the way she was followed around at finals. I’m genuinely surprised you two never got spotted though.”

    “We barely left Kara’s room.”

    “Oh, I bet,” Jack snickers. 

    “And that’s why we kept it quiet.”

    “Yup,” he smiles back at her. “Sneaking about and spending every minute together, sounds causal to me.” Jack says, his head moving slowly up and down. It’s a wonder he restrains from rolling his eyes at her. “Although, if it was just the end of some causal hookup, why do you look so devastated?”

    “I’m not, I…” she’s saved by Sam and Jess returning. Their arms full of groceries, which are quickly dumped onto the counter, and the next thing, Lena’s being hugged from both sides.

    “How are you holding up?” Sam asks.

    “Superbly, we Luthor’s know how to handle our issues,” Lena raises her glass, earning a soft chuckle from Jess.

    “Night in or night out?” Jess starts to put away the refrigerated items.

    “In,” Lena says in a heartbeat. She can’t face the world and knows they’d all end up at Al’s, probably alongside Kara’s friends and it’d make it worse.

    “You both know?” Jack asks the others.

    “It was kind of obvious,” Jess says, but from the look on Jack’s face, it wasn’t to him. “If you weren’t infatuated with Kara,” she adds.

    “Hey,” Jack lamely shoots back at her. “I’m just a big fan.”

    “We know,” Jess laughs.

    “And you?” Jack asks Sam, who guiltily looks to Lena.

    “I told Sam,” Lena admits.

    “I practically forced it out of her,” Sam gives Jack’s back a rub on her way past.

    “I’m sorry,” Lena says. “It was…we…”

    “Needed to have a bit of time to work out how you felt?” Sam helps her out of the hole, then puts her back in it. “How’d that go?”

    “I’m sitting here, with the bottle of wine, Lex gave me for my 21st birthday. I think it went about as good as it can.”

    “You’d better tell us everything,” Sam says.

    Jess opens another bottle of wine and the four of them sit around the kitchen counter, whilst, Lena fills them in on most of what went on with her and Kara. 

    “And you kissed her, first?” Jess seems most surprised by this confession, than any of the other revelations that Jack’s dragged out of her.

    “Kara can be so,” Lena makes an irritated huff, but it’s no use, the way her face lights up when she talks about Kara, has all three of them looking at her with matching expressions. “I stormed after her, she fell—we fell and then…” she remembers it all too well. The first kiss, the way Kara’s hands had gripped at her waist. How it made her feel. “Things were a bit rocky for a while, you know? I always thought she was kind of arrogant.”

    “We remember,” Jack rocks back on his chair. “Refused to even entertain the idea of watching a game. Wait, do you even like basketball?”

    “I…” Lena shakes her head, confused by his question and similarly by her answer. “Yes?”

    “But it definitely helped that Kara Danvers was the star attraction,” Jess teases.

    Lena doesn’t answer, it’s pretty obvious really. She got caught up in everything Kara and now Kara’s gone, “I’m leaving early,” she tells them. “I’ve a couple of weeks to finish up here, but then, it’s all go at LuthorCorp.”

    Her announcement sobers the mood and once she’s told them more about her plans, Sam takes her hand and gives it a squeeze, “it’s been a lot of fun. I’m glad we all met.”

    “Me too,” Lena closes her eyes and is again being wrapped up in a warm hug. She never knew how touch-starved she’d been in her life before, but now she has friends and she’s not letting any of them go, even if she’s leaving.

    “This isn’t goodbye, though,” Sam tells her, seemingly reading her thoughts. “You’re stuck with us now, we’re family. So you can go off and play at being a Luthor, but you’ve always a home, wherever I am.”

    “Same,” Jess kisses her on the cheek. “We love you, you know?”

    “I do,” Lena says, she understands why her friends feel the need to tell her as much, just before she returns to the sometimes suffocating world of the Luthor’s. “And, I love you, all of you.”

    “To our family,” Jack picks up his drink and a chorus of cheers ring out as glasses clink together.

    —

    For the next few days, she pretends she isn’t missing Kara in every moment she’s awake. Her efforts focused on packing up her things, tying up loose-ends and saying goodbye to the faculty she’s got to know during her studies.

    Her favourite professor gives her a hug and it’s why she’s feeling particularly vulnerable when Jack walks into the house wearing Kara’s new team colours, same name, same number but in the mint green and gold of the Sonic’s franchise.

    “Why?” She groans inwardly, and perhaps outwardly, because Jack’s raised one eyebrow as he stares back at her.

    “Basketball doesn’t end at college,” he says, with a wink, depositing two bags on the counter. Lena peers over his shoulder, unsurprised to find they’re full of his usual basketball snacks and beer, all set to tune in for Kara’s first game.

    “How could I forget,” Lena gives up on trying to get any more packing done, grabbing some work to read through, because she was always going to be watching, even if it’s only a preseason game.

    It’s hard to miss how happy Kara looks in the pregame footage, messing around on the court with her new teammates and coaching staff. She’s doing the thing she loves and it shows. It’s not that Lena expected to see some sign of Kara missing her or anything, just she kind of, the tiniest little amount, did. She shoves down the thought as quickly as she indulged in it and it helps when the footage cuts to Kara’s game highlights at the National Champs and then moves onto an interview, which Lena hadn’t seen before. She recognises Kara’s outfit from draft night and realises this must have been filmed when she was out of sight. She talks about her stylist, about her future and then the studio team keep chatting about her prospects for the season to come, with a split-screen, giving a close-up of Kara taking practice shots.

    Jack flops onto the couch and Sam comes in a few minutes later to join them, helping herself to a bowl of chips before her eyes fix onto the screen, “she’s definitely been in the weight room,” she says and Lena agrees. She can’t miss how toned Kara looks. It’s not helping her mood. 

    Rather than joining her friends, she watches from the kitchen counter, one eye on the game, whilst she scans over the proposal she’s about to run past Lex. Jack’s animatedly giving out stat-lines for both teams, when he suddenly exclaims, “woah.” 

    Lena looks up to see Kara on the floor, her teammates reaching out to help her up. The replay shows the unnecessary shoulder-barge, “she’s going to have a few bruises tomorrow,” Sam says through a mouthful of food.

    “It’s ridiculous, how many welcome to the “W” hits do they need to give her, dirty fuckers,” Jack pops the cap off another beer.

    “Good to see they’re scared of what she’s going to bring to the league,” Sam’s leaning forward now, resting her head on both hands.

    Lena agrees, for every play, they double team Kara. She’s also been whacked a few times in the opening minutes and it’s been an effort not to flinch with each and every impact. The game probably wouldn’t be so hard to watch, if Kara’s team were playing well, but they’re not.

    There are some talented players on the court, but nothing they do seems to work, the coach looks lost and the number of turnovers is getting ridiculous. She moves towards the couch, “Kara wasn’t lying when she said it’d take a while for them to gel.”

    “Let’s hope it’s soon, I’m not sure I can survive a whole season of this,” Jack tips his head back, draining half the bottle, jumping off the coach to grab another, when the game coverage goes to a commercial break.

    “Why do they keep saying she could have taken another year at college?” Lena asks, having heard the commentator mention it a few times in the opening minutes.

    “She was always going into the draft,” Jack says unhelpfully from where he’s getting himself comfy again.

    “Well, you could have told me that before I—” she stops herself, but it’s too late, Sam’s tilting her head to one side, calculating and then it happens. The moment she works it all out.

    “Before what?” Jack says, eyes narrowing at the sudden shift in the conversation.

    Sam looks at him and then at Lena, “before you fell in love with her,” she fills in the blanks. It’s soft. Too soft and caring and gentle. Lena’s eyes sting from the tears she will not cry.

    “I’m not, I’m…it’s just…”

    “Oh pet,” Jack’s ditched his snacks and is beside her. They’re both hugging her, when did that happen?

    And she is crying, real tears. This is not happening, but she can’t stop.

    “I’m an idiot,” she says, through a shudder, Sam squeezes her tighter.

    “You’re human, you fell for a girl, who, from what I’ve seen, is pretty crazy about you.”

    “And she left,” Lena waves towards the screen, because none of that matters when geography and reality comes into play. “She went into a draft, where she knew she’d be moving across the country and starting a whole new life and we never talked about what it would mean and…” she lets out a breath, because it’s all true. Kara knew the whole time. Where she was going to end up, what it all meant. This is the future, she always dreamed of and Lena just never fitted into those plans. Going over it all, again, won’t change a thing. Kara’s life is somewhere else. It’s not in National City, no longer at NCU and neither is Lena’s.

    Resolving to put an end to it, she dries her eyes, determined to focus on her own future. She’ll be okay, she has to be. “Ignore me, I’m being sentimental, it’s all these goodbyes. I’ll be fine, once I’m back in Metropolis.”

    She sees the looks her friends share, but they don’t challenge her, which is probably for the best, because she’s not all that sure she’s telling the truth.

    —

    Despite all the talk of moving on, she’s of course still in contact with Kara. Not the way it was before, but a few short messages and quick updates on what’s been happening for them both, which makes sense, they’re still friends and they made a promise to keep in touch. Something Lena’s glad of, on her last night at NCU. She’s almost at the entrance to Al’s, when she gets a new message. This one is a selfie of Kara in her rebel edition uniform, with a caption underneath, “thoughts?”

    Lena has a lot of thoughts, too many and it’s what has her making the split second decision to call.

    “Lena, hey,” Kara answers, sounding a bit flustered and Lena can’t believe how good it feels to hear her voice.

    “Hi, is it a bad time?”

    “Nope, I was just, uh, one sec,” Lena can hear rustling in the background and realises she’s been put on speaker before Kara speaks again. “Middle of changing, sorry. I’ve only just got home, where are you?”

    “I’m on my way into Al’s.”

    “Boo,” Kara whines and Lena can imagine she’s also jutting out her bottom lip, looking ridiculously cute. “I miss home,” she says, then a little softer. “Miss you.”

    It’s Kara’s words, knocking the air from her lungs all over again and god, does Lena feel the same. 

    “I miss you too,” she says, once she can remember how to speak. She hears the breath Kara takes and quickly changes the subject to something less emotionally fraught, “you’re settling in okay?”

    “It’s been eventful, good though, yes,” Kara brightens. “We’re having a team meal tonight, trying to get bonded off the court. It’s not like we were at NCU, but yep, got to try something to make it all click.”

    “You get on though?” Lena knows Kara’s quick to make new friends and hopes she is now.

    “Oh yeah, I mean, it’s basketball, so loads in common. There’s some interesting personalities in the group,” she chuckles and Lena can imagine after some of the crash outs she’s seen in the pre-season game. “How about you? You packed everything up?”

    “All set. I’m not looking forward to saying goodbye at all, think I might cry.”

    “Goodbyes suck,” Kara says and for a few seconds the line is quiet, perhaps Kara’s doing the same as Lena and thinking about their own goodbye? “Lena, I…” she pauses. “Sorry, that’s my ride. I’m going to have to go.”

    “Of course, I won’t keep you. Go do the whole bonding thing.”

    “Fingers crossed it helps. We need to get something working before the opener,” Kara says, she takes the call off speaker, but keeps talking, in the background Lena can make out the sound of a door opening and can tell Kara’s on the move.

    “Hmm, yep, I saw the game against the Dynamo’s.”

    “Bad?” Kara laughs, “actually don’t answer. I know we sucked,” another door opens and Kara says “hi,” to whomever she’s meeting. “Have fun tonight, promise?”

    “I promise,” Lena says. 

    “I’d better…” Kara sighs, another goodbye is on the cards and Lena hates it. 

    “Yeah,” Lena says, wishing they weren’t so far apart. “Oh,” she suddenly remembers the whole point of the call, “and you look good in your new colours, really good. Sam noticed you’ve been hitting the weight room.”

    “You bet I have,” Kara sounds ridiculously delighted with both pieces of praise.

    “I’ll let you go now, but good luck next week.”

    “You’ll be watching?”

    “I might be,” she can’t resist replying in the coy way she always used to. It was part of the fun of teasing Kara, even when they both knew, Lena was always watching.

    “Then I’ll make sure to put in a performance worthy of your attention,” she says in a way which has Lena’s mind wandering. She’s at Al’s now, but has half a mind to turn around, catch a flight and race towards Kara. “I’m really glad you called,” Kara says, soft. True. “It’s been good to hear your voice.”

    They say a quick goodbye and with the sound of a car door closing, Kara ends the call. 

    Lena slips her phone back in her pocket, thinking maybe this is enough. It could work, they can stay in contact. It’s what friends do and Lena’s okay with it, she is, really. She just has to pretend it doesn’t feel like there’s something missing in her life, or spend any time thinking about why her heart still aches, in the way it has done, since Kara walked out of the hotel room. Easy.

    She takes another minute to get herself together, before pushing the door open, smiling at her friends and doing all she can to be more like Kara. Living in the moment and enjoying every bit of her last night at NCU.

    —

    Arriving at her new office a couple of days later, she finds a lone piece of mail, waiting on her desk. Recognising the writing, she hurriedly tears the package open. Inside is a leather-bound journal, with the initials L.L, emblazoned in the bottom right corner. 

    She opens the cover to find a handwritten note: Danvers family tradition!! Good luck on your first day! Boss lady Lena is going to kill it! KD xx

    She holds the journal against her chest, taking a second for herself, feeling the comfort of having Kara’s support, ahead of the first day of this new reality.

    Chapter End Notes

    Next up: Lena takes a trip. Kara keeps playing.
    these paths lead me back to you. I don't know how, but hope springs eternal.

    Chapter 28: Obligations

    Chapter Summary

    Distance, a trip, keeping contact.

    Chapter Notes

    The next day, she sends Kara flowers as a thank you. On the Friday, the Sonic’s win their first game. 

    —

    Everything moves quickly once she’s in Metropolis and those weeks are perhaps the most intense of Lena’s life. She’s busy from the second she kicks off her covers in the morning, until she collapses back into bed at night. Lex has her rushing between meetings and Lillian is keeping a close eye on everything she does. It’s exhausting at times. It’s also, exactly as Lena hoped it would be. Making a difference was never going to be easy and if she’s to have a chance to change things at LuthorCorp, this is how she needs to do it. 

    This way of life and her responsibilities are not all brand new to her. Lionel always insisted she stayed in the loop and she’s had one foot dipped in the water for the past few years, which definitely helps. She knows all the board and has more than a few allies it turns out.

    It doesn’t take a genius to see that not everyone is happy with how Lex has been directing operations and Lillian may like to think she’s the all-powerful overseer, but she has more enemies than Lena had factored in.

    It’ll take time, she’s young, has to work for it and as predicted, Lex is tying her up in administrative duties, not hiding his intention to get a read on her and her ambitions. 

    -

    Whilst Lena is trying to make a start in her attempts to reenergise LuthorCorp, Kara’s on the court, playing game after game in quick succession. She’s as amazing as she always has been. Not looking like a rookie at all and as per usual, everyone is talking about her. 

    Her team, however, is still struggling and injuries are rapidly becoming an issue. The length of time for rehab has Lena wondering if there isn’t a better way to support recovery and during the few hours where she’s not pushing paper, she hunkers down in the lab, working on adapting one of her previous inventions.

    Lex pokes his head in a few times, seemingly satisfied to see her putting her talent to good use and having a focus, makes the separation a little more bearable.

    —

    A month into her tenure and she’s flying to Japan for a three week trip. Lex is joining her for the first week and it occurs to Lena on the flight, that it’s going to be the most time the siblings have spent together, since Lena left for college. Losing their father, has undoubtedly changed them both and with Lex taking on the responsibility of running LuthorCorp, he’s apparently also indulged in a lust for power, which has made him somewhat unrecognisable at times. She tries to frame the trip as an opportunity for them to reconnect, although her hopes are quickly dashed, when the experience only highlights the negative impact it’s had on Lex. From the way he now drinks more than Jack, to his ability to shout down any opposing ideas.

    It’s hard to reconcile, that the boy who once shared his dreams with Lena, has been replaced by a man, twisted and corrupted by the Luthor corporate machine, A man she’s struggling to see the good in.

    It’s on his last night and over a stupidly expensive bottle of whiskey that he tells her, “this is why we do it.”

    Lena looks at him, his tie is askew, his bald head is extra shiny in the low light of the bar and he looks as tired as she feels. “To drink from the top shelf?”

    “To have people quiver when we walk in the room,” he says, putting his glass back on the table with a clunk. “You see them?” He waves towards the bar. “They want to be us. I walked in and they all looked, because they know who I am, the power I wield. The one on the left is working up the courage to come over here and pitch his latest invention, the woman who keeps glancing at me over the rim of her glass, wants me to take her back to my room.”

    “Can you hear yourself,” Lena scoffs. “What happened to you?”

    Lex picks his drink back up and drains the glass, “excellence.”

    “Entitlement,” Lena corrects him and he shrugs.

    “Call it what you want. This is all going to be yours. We’re Luthor’s. We get to have whatever we want, whenever we want it.”

    “Not everything,” she says quietly, not that Lex is listening. He’s talking about projects and potential. Lena’s targets for the coming fortnight and then he mentions basketball.

    “…big numbers, I want to push her to the front at All-stars. The media’s obsession with pitching her against Danvers is perfect. The rivalry is breeding a whole other world of fanatics, they’re rabid for it.”

    “She’s not half the player.”

    “Doesn’t matter,” Lex pours himself another glass. “Half the people watching, have no interest in the game, they want to see those two sparing. It’s what draws a crowd and if they’re in a room, where we can showcase the Luthor brand, then we win.”

    “And somehow, you still get to use Kara for your own ends?”

    “There’s more than one way to get what you want in life,” Lex chuckles and perhaps Lena should have seen this one coming. Her brother would always find a way. 

    —

    She may not agree with Lex’s view on things, but she accepts there are many advantages that come with having a name like Luthor. It’s what’s given her the opportunity and means to make a difference in this world, but on a rare day off, she’s also reminded of how isolated it can make her feel.

    She’s looking out of a window at the very top of the Tokyo tower, when a crushing sense of loneliness hits. There are millions of people down below, all getting on with their lives, taking kids to school, dashing towards work, tourists desperately seeking the perfect spot for their Instagram shot. It’s busy, hectic, it’s life, and Lena feels so removed from it all.

    For everything she hopes to do in her life, she’s quickly realising, it’s not as much fun without a certain basketball player to do it all with.

    —

    Back home, the injury crisis at the Sonic’s continues, leaving them little choice but to bring in new players to fill the roster and the arrival of Gayle Marsh has people talking. Lena’s seen a few posts, but gets stuck on a think piece for longer than she probably should.

    “The pair have chemistry on the court,” it says. “And off. Having been rivals during High School, the Sonic’s have brought together two of the hottest talents of their class. Marsh has always been on our radar and has unfairly been overlooked, possibly because Danvers is a generational talent. Her big break came with a draft to the Met’s, but heartbreak followed and Marsh was side-lined for the majority of last season with an ongoing shoulder issue and was ultimately waived, giving her the chance to link up with the Sonic’s. Danvers speaks highly of her and her on-court presence is undeniable. Could this matchup be the answer we were all seeking? Will Kara Danvers finally have a player who knows how to shoot, whenever she passes her the ball? We certainly hope so. Some fans are extra excited about the roster changes, especially since Danvers and Marsh were spotted getting dinner together at the….” 

    Lena stops reading and tunes into highlights of last nights game instead. It’s true, Kara and Gayle work well together on the court. It’s still not enough to get them a second win.

    They fight to the very end and the fans are loving it. Lena’s gripped, even though she knows the result. It’s at a certain point in the game, when the camera cuts to the bench, who are on all up on their feet, cheering a bucket, that it strikes Lena what is happening. She’s a fan too, she’s enjoying every second of it. This is her team. This is what Kara has done to her.

    —

    Two days later, the Sonic’s finally do get the W and another comes in quick succession, but the rebuild is short-lived.

    Kara takes a big hit and when the news comes in that she’s ruled out with concussion for the next fixture, Lena calls her, “how’s the head?”

    “Bruised,” Kara grumbles, then the call annoyingly disconnects. Lena’s about to hit redial when Kara’s name flashes up on the screen, reconnecting as a video call, “hey,” she says, her toothy grin filling the screen.

    “Hi,” Lena smiles back at her. “And ouch,” her smile fades when she takes in the purple bruise around Kara’s cheekbone.

    “Very ouch,” Kara touches the spot. “Be okay if I hadn’t smacked my head on the hardwood after.”

    “It had me wincing,” Lena confesses and Kara smiles.

    “Good to know you’re still watching.”

    “What can I say, I really like the way Gayle Marsh owns the court.”

    “Oh hey, no!” Kara says with a fierce shake of her head. “You’ve seen the chatter?”

    “Hard to miss,” Lena looks down at her hands, then back at the screen. Kara’s squinting just a little, leaning closer.

    “I’ve known her for like forever from balling, but we went out to get a better bond and—”

    “You don’t need to explain yourself to me.”

    “I know, I…” Kara looks away for a second, then her blue eyes lock back onto Lena’s. “I know we agreed…and things with us were always…” she shrugs, not filling the blanks of what they were, in the way she always did. “but I guess its, well, uh, she’s a friend. That’s all.”

    Lena takes a breath, relief washing over her, “okay.”

    “If I was seeing someone, I’d tell you,” Kara says and the relief is gone. 

    “Me too,” she replies and if Kara’s smile looks sad, Lena’s probably mirroring it.

    “Soooo,” Kara says, awkwardly trying to move towards safer ground. “Looks like I’m out for a bit. Doc says I was all delusional on the court and it’s mandatory rest.”

    “Concussion protocol is no joke Kara,” Lena says.

    “I knew you’d say that,” Kara beams back at her. “How’s the trip?”

    “It’s beautiful. I’ve managed some sightseeing since Lex left, it’s…” she aches to tell Kara how she misses her, wishes she was here too. Instead she says, “you’d love it.”

    They talk about not very much at all, their days, plans for the coming weeks and it’s like it always was. An ease which came so naturally and makes Lena yearn for those nights when Kara would be sitting cross-legged on her bedroom floor, sharing pizza and telling stories. The call ends when Kara’s needed somewhere else and Lena checks through her schedule, wondering if there’s a chance they may actually get to see one another face to face, once she’s back on home soil. There is one date in the calendar, which she draws three rings around.

    —

    By the time Lena’s back in the country, Kara’s returned to playing and the team scrap away to another win, much to Jack’s delight which fills the group chat. Lena’s taken aback when she sees the photo he puts up. Standing beside Kara, thumbs up and a huge smile on his face. He’s wearing Kara’s NCU jersey and Lena misses those days more than she cares to admit and of course Jack’s followed Kara and traveled to her game. An unfamiliar sense of jealously creeps up on Lena and she quickly stuffs her phone away.

    She’s almost at her apartment, desperate for a few hours sleep in her own bed, for the first time in weeks, when she gets a call from Lex, who has also just returned from his own trip, summoning her to a must-attend appointment and the next thing, she’s telling her driver to set a course towards the Metropolis Arena.

    “We have to watch, it’s part of what we do,” Lex says, handing Lena a Mojito. It’s not as good as the one’s she’d grown fond of in Japan, but it does help her put up with her brother for the next two hours.

    Watching the WNBA live, is a lot different to watching it through a small screen in another country and it’s also very different to the college game she’s used to. These women are powerful, strong athletes and the game is fast. 

    Selina Kyle has been accumulating minutes throughout the first half of the season, a regular starter and a key member of the setup, a fact which Lex has been stating loudly, at every break in play.

    Lena’s seen plenty of Selina, when she’s been on the opposing side to Kara at college and can’t say she sees what Lex does. She’s good, but not a facilitator. She can’t control the court in the way Kara always does and it’s apparent in the amount of steals the opposition rack up. The game plays out, her mojito has her relaxing, the clock is counting down to zero and then Lex says, “winners get dinners.”

    It’s a phrase she’s never heard come out of her brothers mouth before and has her barking out a laugh, which draws some attention from those seated around them, “who even are you?”

    “I’m your moderately drunk and jet-lagged brother, come on, we need food and Selina’s paying.”

    They end up waiting, for over half an hour, at a restaurant, a mile away from the arena. Selina walks in, wearing dark glasses and her tunnel fit, which has heads turning. It appears to be exactly what she was after, as she takes a few minutes to pose for photos.

    “Lena!” She walks towards the table, greeting Lena warmly. “Thank god, much as I love your bro, it’s always a delight to see your beautiful face.”

    “You can chat up my sister later,” Lex says, his eyes flitting from Selina to Lena and back again. “First we need to talk about the idea I had…”

    Lex sits down, detailing his plans and it takes Lena a minute to realise she’s the one who is going to be involved at every step. She’s not averse to working with Selina, but will definitely need to shut down whatever this woman is playing at, because she keeps looking at Lena like she’d happily devour her. 

    Their conversation is interrupted by a server and then they get back to business.

    An hour or so later, the three of them are exiting the restaurant when a series of flashes has Lena raising a hand to shield her eyes.

    “S.K, give us a wave,” someone calls out.

    “We love you,” another shouts.

    Lena takes a step back, only for Lex’s hand to land at the small of her back, “now don’t be shy, Lena. Luthor’s stand in the spotlight.”

    She gives him an elbow to his ribs, but smiles for the photos. It’s a relief to finally get back to her apartment. After a long shower, she sinks under the covers, falling asleep within minutes.

    She wakes to a message from Sam, “something I should know?”

    “I’m home?” She types back.

    A second message arrives containing a link, which takes Lena to an article about Selina and at the bottom, is a photo of Lena at the game the night before, laughing at some story Lex made up. In another image, the two women stand side by side, just having left the restaurant. Lena’s smiling for the cameras. Selina is looking at Lena.

    “She’s predatory,” Lena types back. “And my brother has a twisted agenda.”

    “What’s new???” Sam replies. “You can tell me everything, at dinner, next week. I’ve missed u.”

    “I can’t get to National City, but the end of the month?”

    “I’m going to ignore the fact u’ve forgotten, it’s All-star week 🏀,” Sam’s reply is instant. The three dots showing she’s still typing. “All coming 2 town, told u last month.”

    Lena’s sure they probably did, but with her schedule and the Japan trip, time’s caught up on her. She’d not forgotten, how could she? All-Star week, means Kara will be in the same city as Lena and for the first time, in far too long, she will get to see her face in person.

    “Tell me when you land.”

    “👍”

    -

    She checks the time, hopes it’s not too early to call and waits as the phone rings.

    “Morning,” Kara’s voice is such a beautiful sound at any time of the day, but Lena’s always had a fondness for the way she tries not to sound sleepy. 

    “Did I wake you?” She asks, already sure of it.

    “Nope, well, maybe. My alarm was going to go off in like one minute.”

    “Maybe put it on do not disturb?”

    “It is,” Kara says through a stretch, her sheets rustling through the phone line. “You can set it up to bypass if someone important calls. Suppose you never turn it off?”

    “I only give out my personal number to important people,” she replies.

    “Now we’ve established that we’re both each others VIP’s, you okay?”

    “I am, I flew in yesterday and Lex insisted I went to a game,” she says, unsure how Kara might feel about it.

    “I watched, you looked good. You always do.”

    “Think you’re meant to be watching the ones who bounce the ball,” Lena jokes.

    “You’ve always held my focus,” Kara says and for a second, it’s quiet. “Besides, game wasn’t too hot.”

    “There’s some pictures,” she says, knowing how it looks in the article Sam sent her.

    “I’ve seen,” Kara’s ability to sound unaffected, leaves Lena questioning if she’s overthinking it all, but then Kara adds, “she likes you.”

    “The feeling isn’t mutual,” her reply is quick.

    “Noted,” Lena can hear the smile in Kara’s voice and for some reason, it’s all she needed to put her mind at ease.

    “So, All-star?” The circled date in her diary. The first time they’re going to be in the same city since they said goodbye.

    “You can still make it?”

    “I can. I’m excited to see you, it’s been too long.”

    “Yeah,” Kara says, wistfully. “Way too long.”

    Chapter End Notes

    Next up: All-Star reunion.

    Chapter 29: reunion

    Chapter Summary

    Both in the same city, time to be brave.

    Chapter Notes

    Two more games, two losses and the midway point of the season, has the Sonic’s stuck at the bottom of the table. 

    Kara’s dominating in points per game, showing her mettle and versatility and it’s beyond Lena, how a player can be doing so well and yet the team can’t buy a win. It appears there’s a score to settle when some of the opposing sides come to play and Lena’s not alone in suspecting Kara’s popularity has something to do with it. The narrative being sold, works. It has people watching and talking and a need to get one over the player everyone is talking about, feeds into it. 

    With everything basketball filling her thoughts, Lena has to do a double-take when she walks towards her office on the morning before All_stars, because right there, in the middle of Metropolis, looking like she is absolutely disgusted to be stuck in the city, is the one and only, Cat Grant.

    For a second she considers turning around and hoping Cat doesn’t notice, but those sharp eyes have already landed on her, “Lena,” she says walking over. 

    “Coach,” she says, feeling instinctively frosty.

    “I heard what you did for Leslie,” Cat says, catching Lena off guard, “thank you. She’s really settled into rehab and your investment will go a long way in helping all our future players.”

    Chapter 30: reconnecting

    Chapter Summary

    Moving into a new way of existing.

    Chapter Notes

    With Kara needing to be at an early morning event, Lena makes her way back to her apartment wearing a borrowed/stolen forever grey sweatshirt and a pair of Kara’s training pants. The clothes are comfy, they also smell delightfully of Kara and with a baseball cap, pulled low, she escapes the bustling hotel unnoticed.

Spending the entire morning, smiling to herself, because she has a girlfriend. A very hot, beautiful, amazing girlfriend.

    “It’s functional,” she surmises.

    “You’re a funny woman, Lena,” Kara laughs, turning to look at her, bright eyes shining.
