    Lena laughs, but she hops off the bed, chasing after Kara into the bathroom.

    —

    They return to Metropolis the day before the gala, Lena opening up the door and Kara struggling to heave all their bags inside. The hallway is already full, thanks to Kara’s various kitbags, because she’s about to leave again, this time to play in Unrivalled.

    “Sorry,” Kara says, when she catches the way Lena’s looking at the state of things. “I’ll get some of these shipped to my place next week.”

    “It’s fine,” Lena assures, stepping over one of the bags. She’s gotten used to them, but Kara’s still trying to reorganise things, when Lena returns from the bathroom. It’s something Lena’s seen her do, too many times. Pulling things from one bag, stuffing them into another, all set for yet another trip, to someplace which is not with Lena.

    “Be five more minutes,” Kara says, looking satisfied as she retrieves a jersey from the USA bag and fits it into her carry-on.

    Lena watches her a minute longer, then simply says, “move in?”

    “Huh?” Kara looks up from her task, but it’s obvious she heard what Lena said.

    “I mean it, if you want to? I don’t know why I haven’t asked before, but I suppose it always felt like every time you got here, you were always leaving…” she thinks about it as Kara rises to her feet.

    “I am always leaving,” Kara says, a half smile tugging at the corner of her mouth.

    “But if you moved in, then you’d always be coming home,” Lena says softly, hoping Kara wants this too. It makes sense. They’re in love, she wants to see Kara every chance she gets.

    “Always feels like I’m coming home, when I’m with you,” Kara takes a step closer, undoing Lena with her words, as always. “You’re sure?”

    “Very,” Lena nods, slowly. “This place is empty without you and I know, you’ll be gone half the year, but when you’re not…If you want to, not that I want to put pressure on you. I’m not—” she rolls her eyes at how difficult these things can get sometimes and of course Kara’s just smiling at her. “Do you want to live together?”

    “Sounds cool,” Kara says in her annoyingly nonchalant way. Lena scowls at her and Kara laughs, holding her hands up in defeat. “I’d love to.”

    “Then it’s settled,” Lena beams back at her. “I’ll make space in the closet and you can store all your kit in the spare room and we’ll go shopping for units next weekend or when you’re next home and…one second…” she rushes to the kitchen, rummaging through a drawer until she finds the final thing she needs to make this real. Returning, she dangles the spare key in front of her, slipping it into Kara’s outstretched hand. “Welcome home.”

    She’s being kissed in the next breath, arms wrapped around her waist, lifting her off the ground and carrying her towards the bedroom. Their bedroom, their home.

    Chapter End Notes

    oh and I know I said two, but we all know I've no idea by now and then I wrote this scene and it's an extra I suppose. (so three...?)

    Up next: the gala and things.

    Happy St Patricks and welcome to March Madness...Brackets? all set?

    Chapter 34: Together

    Chapter Summary

    Family events, public outings. Keeping things private.

    Chapter Notes

    Moving in together will inevitably draw attention to their relationship, although Lena finds it hard to see how it could be that much different to the way things have been the last few months. Kara’s been living out of bags, switching up her various training outfits and flying in and out of Metropolis at every opportunity and mostly she’s gone unnoticed. The assumption, that in a city full of celebrity, her presence isn’t all too interesting, is accompanied by the fact that the circles Lena moves in, offer a degree of privacy and discretion when needed. It’s given them the chance to grow as a couple, which has been welcome in a world where it sometimes feels like Kara’s face pops up during every commercial break and Lena’s rise to the head of LuthorCorp has meant her own image is now splashed across several media publications. 

    A combination which has led to their names being linked a few times since the return trip to NCU, with a handful of online articles questioning if Kara may be the next basketball player to benefit from LuthorCorp funding. Alongside them, comes the social media speculation about Kara and whomever she might be dating, speculation which Kara brushes off with ease, for the most part. Laughing about it with Lena whenever they’re together, although at times she looks tired and Lena’s become increasingly aware, of how the life of a professional WNBA player is scrutinised beyond belief. 

    This isn’t college anymore and they can’t live in the bubble which Cat Grant had helped maintain for all her players. Kara’s been a number one draft pick, is the current rookie of the year and has fans on every corner, at every airport and apparently even at the coffee shop next to the gym she’s been training at in Metropolis.

    The second the woman approaches, Lena takes a step back, leaving Kara to kindly take a photo and answer a few questions.

    “Just getting coffee with a friend,” Kara says, eyes darting towards where Lena is trying to look inconspicuous.

    The woman takes note of Lena for a second, there’s a hint of recognition, but then she returns her attention to Kara, “could you sign this one, too?” She pulls something out of her purse and Kara obliges, before taking a selfie. “I love you and Gayle together. I know it’ll be better next season. Late surge from the Sonic’s to the top.”

    “Hundred percent,” Kara smiles through it all, then she’s all Lena’s again, taking her coffee and steering them out of the coffee shop. She pulls her cap a little lower, but there are eyes on them now and Lena can feel the quiet ease of their morning quickly fading.

    “You okay?” she asks, taking double-steps to keep up with Kara’s long strides. “You seem in a hurry to get away.”

    “Hmm,” Kara glances back over her shoulder, Lena looks too. There’s no-one there. “She was filming when we were in the line.”

    “Really?” Lena’s surprised she missed it and remembers she’d been distracted with making plans to accommodate more of Kara’s things in the apartment.

    “They hold their phones in a certain way,” Kara says, the frustration obvious. “Like I’m cool with taking a photo if they ask, but sometimes, I don’t know, makes me uncomfortable, it’s pretty intrusive and it’s not fair on you.”

    “It’s not fair on you,” Lena says pointedly. “I’ll be more aware in future.”

    “Maybe we should just get door-dash next time?”

    “Is that what you want?” Lena’s all for a private life, but being forced in this way, feels wrong.

    “No, I…” Kara stops walking, turning to face Lena, a sad smile on her face. “I just wanted a morning with you.”

    “Well, you have it,” Lena hooks her arm in Kara’s tucking in closely to her side and carries on their walk back to the apartment. “I might not want to be seen, but I also don’t want to hide, Kara. I know it sounds crazy at times but—”

    “No, I get it,” Kara cuts her off. “I didn’t before, at college and everything, but I see it now and also your life is,” she opens out her hands, wide and at the same time, puffs out an incredulous breath, “it’s huge. Nia sends me an article almost every week about how cool you are, not that I need the article to know it, but you know what I mean? What you’re doing with LuthorCorp really matters and I get it. Someone is always trying to write you off or pull you down and yeah, at least we can both relate on how it’s not easy being yourself, when the whole world is looking.”

    “Of all the things we could have in common,” Lena jokes and Kara’s laugh is a welcome sound after everything. Her smile softens as she leans into Kara’s side, “how about we forget about prying eyes and enjoy the last fragments of peace before tonight?”

    “You got it,” Kara says, tucking Lena’s arm back inside hers and sipping her coffee. The rest of the walk home is slower, relaxed. They talk about the coming days and the need to organise some space in the spare room, before Kara joins up with her team in Miami. It’s exciting to plan a future for themselves and Lena can’t wait for it all to begin.

    —

    Despite a day of doing very little, they’re somehow still running late for the Gala. Lena can already hear her mother’s scathing remark about tardiness before they’ve turned the corner. 

    Kara fidgets in the seat beside her and it draws Lena’s attention, because Kara really looks a sight tonight. The pantsuit is a perfect fit, hugging all of Kara’s best assets in a way Lena truly appreciates. She’s commented several times about the way the jacket, clings around those yummy biceps and, oh, yes, that’s why they’re running late. Lena’s finding it incredibly hard to keep her mind on track when Kara is simply so distracting.

    “Ready?” Kara asks, snapping Lena back to reality.

    “As I’ll ever be,” she hesitates before opening the door, turns back to Kara and kisses her swiftly on the lips. “you look beautiful and I can’t tell you enough, how happy I am that you’re my date tonight.”

    The comment has Kara grinning widely, before she pulls Lena in for an all too brief, second kiss, both conscious of their makeup and of what awaits them outside the car.

    They step out and Lena’s dazzled by the rapid flashes of cameras, whilst excited onlookers gasp at the sight of Kara Danvers. Lillian Luthor’s big night has always drawn a crowd and this year is no exception. 

    She looks over her shoulder to find Kara fidgeting with the cuffs of her jacket, seemingly nervous, but when she catches Lena watching her, her face instantly lights up. Lena holds out her hand for Kara to take, but before her girlfriend gets the chance, Lex’s car pulls up and his arrival, appears to be the true reason so many people are gathered outside. 

    “Who knew they were all so keen to witness my return to society,” Lex says through gritted teeth, just as Lena gets to his side. “I did warn mother.”

    “And where is mother?” Lena says, smiling stiffly for the cameras. “Shouldn’t she be here with us, set for the family portrait.”

    “I suspect she’d prefer us all on the stage, she prefers the lighting,” he chuckles and it’s a sound Lena’s missed. He still looks uneasy, adjusting the lapels of his jacket, whilst the flashes continue. “How are you, Kara?”

    “Good, you?”

    “Ah, you know, never really prepared for these kinds of functions and if I’m being honest, this is the first time I’ve showed up sober since I was 15.”

    “14,” Lena corrects him. She remembers the first time and how she helped him evade an enraged Lionel.

    “What would I do without you?” He smiles at Lena. “I hope you’ve warned Kara about the perils of being associated with a Luthor. These reporters will dig through your trash for a by-line.”

     “It’s not that bad,” Lena elbows her brother in the ribs, because she knows the invasive nature of their world, but can’t see that Kara’s is much different. “Kara’s the one who has every teenage girl on the internet thirsting after her.”

    “Thirsting!” Kara snorts out a laugh, her hand coming to rest on Lex’s shoulder. “You know some of them are older women, you should see the DM’s,” she winks at Lena, who crinkles her nose in reply. “And I block them in a heartbeat,” she adds unnecessarily.

    “That’s the difference, you can’t block Janey O’Connell and her bloodlust for a story about me slipping up and losing my sobriety,” he’s glaring at the reporter in question and Lena instinctively moves their bodies, so that the woman can’t get a good look at Lex. 

    Kara catches on and moves to his other side, “how about we go inside?”

    Lena smiles at Kara over the back of Lex’s shoulders and the three of them walk the rest of the carpet together.

    Inside they find Lillian busy fussing over every minute detail, although she drops what she’s doing and rushes towards them, making a show of the greeting as if she’d not seen them for years. Lena assumes it’s performative, except there are no camera’s here and she can’t help but hope that maybe everything that happened with Lex, really has changed their mother, for the better.

    —

    With Lena as the head of LuthorCorp, she’s kept busier than she’d like as the night progresses and although Kara assures her, she’s having fun, she wishes they could escape early. Even more so, when she spots Andrea Rojas making her way over.

    “Lena, darling,” she’s all fake smiles and heady perfume, kissing Lena on both cheeks, before her attention turns to Kara. “And the one and only Kara Danvers? You destroyed my team at Nationals.”

    “Kara, this is Andrea,” Lena makes the introduction and she knows the second Kara works out she’s now talking to Lena’s ex and it’s then she adds, in a whisper, “she’s a Bullhorn.”

    “Sorry to hear it, not sorry we won,” Kara says through a smug smile. Lena gives her a playful shove and Andrea watches along.

    “I did spot you on the far side of the court,” Andrea turns back to Lena. “Had hoped we could talk, move on from the past?”

    Lena’s a hundred percent sure that Andrea has an ulterior motive, but despite the way she’d felt all those years ago and the sting of her mother’s rebuttal which had followed, holding onto animosity feels pointless. “Darling, I think we all moved on years ago,” she says.

    “Good, good,” Andrea says, with a relieved exhale. “I’d been hoping to build a bridge and ever since Lex had his misfortunate accident…” she says, leaving a pause for Lena to fill, which she steadfastly refuses to do. “…perhaps now it’s time to renew our friendship?”

    Kara’s hand has found its way to the small of Lena’s back, it’s splayed wide, firm. A reassuring comfort? Or Kara’s need to anchor herself. Lena turns to her briefly and smiles, hoping it tells Kara all she needs to know, before she says to Andrea, “except we were never friends.”

    “No, I suppose we weren’t,” Andrea replies and for a second she seems to be calculating her next response, it’s then that something else happens, her jaw drops slightly, before she recomposes herself and the usual smirk returns, “oh, of course. I never…a basketball player?” She says with an incredulousness which irks Lena more than any similar derisive comment from Lillian ever could. “I’m surprised Lillian has allowed it.”

    “Contrary to what you might think, my mother does not get a say in who I date.”

    “No?” Andrea’s reply is dripping with the knowledge of a past Lena no longer recognises. 

    “You’re out of line,” Lena says calmly. Time has moved on and she’s no desire to get caught up in any of Andrea’s petty games. “A lot has changed since we last spoke.”

    “So it would seem, have to admit, I never expected you to shove Lex off his throne.”

    “Lena was always going to run the company one day,” Kara says in a low tone, her irritation palpable. Lena wants to tell her it’s not worth it. Nothing with Andrea ever is.

    “Perhaps we’ll do business one day,” Andrea ignores Kara, air kissing Lena before waltzing to the other side of the room and loudly greeting the Senator and her husband.

    “She’s quite something,” Kara says through an exhale of breath, her blue eyes tracking Andrea as she animatedly talks to the group around her.

    “Not my favourite mistake,” Lena turns away, tired of witnessing Andrea’s fake act all over again.

    “You’re okay with her knowing about us?” Kara asks.

    “Andrea?” Lena’s surprised at the question. “Of course, why wouldn’t I be?”

    “I don’t know, it’s just…” she shrugs. “Privacy?”

    “Andrea’s many things, but she’d not try and sell our story, we’ll tell it when we’re ready, I promise.”

    “Okay,” Kara says, nodding, although Lena thinks she’s missing something. 

    “Kara, I love you,” it’s really that simple to Lena, nothing else matters.

    —

    A chance to enjoy a moment to themselves is ruined by the approaching Selina Kyle. Lena’s not had many interactions with her since the WNBA season ended and once she took control of the corporation, she had happily diverted all dealings to Eve, but in a room packed with people, she can’t avoid Selina, no matter how much she might want to.

    “Lena, it’s been too long,” Selina says, her overfamiliarity grating and the way she slides a hand around Lena’s waist, before kissing her cheek, has Kara clearing her throat. “And Kara, I can’t seem to get away from you.” She says, releasing her hold on Lena to swipe two flutes of champagne from a passing server. She offers one to Lena, who declines and then proceeds to drain one of the glasses before taking a sip from the other. “Don’t tell me, you’re going to be as dull as that brother of yours now he’s stopped drinking?”

    “Undoubtedly,” Lena gives a saccharine smile, which has Kara stifling her laughter. “I can direct you towards Otis if you want someone to party with?” 

    “I was still hoping you’d take me up on my offer, dinner?”

    “I’m still taken,” Lena says, her hand brushing lightly against Kara’s, but Kara pulls hers away before Selina notices.

    “Damn shame,” Selina runs an eye across the room. “This place isn’t exactly my vibe, what do you say Kara, want to move on to a club? They love basketball stars in this city, although I think you already know that,” she wiggles her eyebrows, but earns nothing but a tightlipped smile out of Kara.

    Lena’s not sure what is happening and is grateful for one of her mother’s interruptions for once.

    “Lena, it’s time. I’ve got Claudia waiting on us and where is Lex?” She says in exasperation, scanning the room until she catches sight of him.

    “Family portrait?” Lex guesses once he’s made his way over and out of nowhere the photographer appears, already snapping photos.

    “No, no, not here,” Lillian scolds, but as she tries to manoeuvre everyone into position, Claudia keeps shooting. “Not one of those is released without my approval,” she says and finally the lens drops.

    Eventually the required photos are taken. Lillian in the middle of Lena and Lex. It’s all for show, the performance required to prove the Luthor’s are as strong as ever. In truth, it’s also the first time they’ve been at the same function in years. Lena knows her role, has practiced poise and a fixed smile, but it slips from her face when Lillian says, “and Kara, come in to Lena’s left?” 

    Kara admittedly looks just as surprised as Lena by the request, but she’s also had plenty of practice at handling unexpected situations and takes the directed spot.

    “You okay with this?” Lena asks, only for Kara’s ears.

    “Yeah, you?”

    “Always,” Lena turns her head to look directly at Kara and smiles. 

    “Always,” Kara says, filling her lungs with a deep breath.

    “Team photo?” Selina says, inserting herself to Lena’s right before anyone can object. Claudia takes more shots, before they’re finally allowed to get on with enjoying the rest of the evening.

    —

    “What was that with Selina?” Lena asks the second they’re alone.

    “It’s weird right?” Kara’s brow crinkles in the middle. “Girl is bold, but joining a photo without being asked—”

    “Not the photo,” Lena cuts in. “The bit where you…you pulled away?” She’s sure she didn’t imagine it.

    “Oh,” Kara says, her sheepishness confirming Lena’s suspicions. “I get how you said Andrea wouldn’t talk, but Selina?” Kara’s shaking her head demonstratively from side to side. “Drama breeds in the league and Selina would happily feed the machine. You once told me, how you came to NCU for anonymity and I know things have changed since then, but the way some people get all into the private lives of players isn’t something I want for you, for us.”

    “That’s all?”

    “Yes.”

    “We can’t stay a secret forever, Kara.”

    “No, I know,” Kara shrugs and then her shoulders sag. “I don’t trust her and I don’t like her hitting on you, but I also don’t want her being the one who tells everyone about us, which I know she’d do if she got a chance. I want us to choose, to decide who knows and when and…” she opens her mouth to say something, then closes it again.

    “Okay,” Lena nods in understanding, repeating what she said earlier, “we do this our way.”

    “Cool,” Kara kicks at the ground, before her beautiful blue eyes meet Lena’s. “I don’t want to hide either. I want the world to know how much I love you, but I don’t want them to find out because Selina Kyle leaked some story.”

    “Then what do you want?”

    “I don’t know. It’s good like this?” She says, waiting for Lena to agree. “We have privacy and people can talk about us if they want, but they won’t know, not unless we shout about it or you turn up to one of my games with a five on your back.”

    “I could do that,” Lena muses, moving into Kara’s space, her hands slipping underneath her open jacket, until she’s gripping Kara’s waist. “Think I’d look good wearing your number?”

    For a second, Kara appears to think on what Lena’s just said, her eyes roam slowly over Lena’s body as if she’s picturing how Lena would look and then she swallows thickly, “yeah, you’d look good, not sure I’d get much balling done though.”

    Lena throws her head back as she laughs, “oh, how I love you,” she says, eyes shining as Kara laughs along with her. The laughter fades and the look between them becomes more heated. “Want to go home?”

    “Yes,” Kara says without hesitation. “Don’t you have to keep working or mixing or…”

    “I’ve definitely done my duty, lets go,” Lena takes Kara’s hand and they make a swift exit, avoiding any more photographers and escaping any lingering goodbyes.

    —

    She has two more days of enjoying Kara, before packed bags sit in the hallway for yet another trip away. This time it’s for Unrivalled and Kara can’t wait to get back to playing competitively. Lena kisses her goodbye in the early hours and already knows she’ll miss Kara’s presence in the apartment. By mid-morning she’s pining too much and takes a walk, only for Kara to call to say she’s landed and asking about Lena’s thoughts on a space for all her memorabilia. Lena’s wondering just how much stuff Kara might have, but it doesn’t matter, they’ll make room for it. This is going to be Kara’s home now and she wants it be exactly how Kara imagines. They chat for a while and when Lena ends the call, she sees a message from Sam, with a link to an article, titled: Danvers showed up and delivered. Alongside a photo of them both, with Lex and Lillian.

    Lena has to chuckle, because she knows Selina’s been cut from the image and can imagine Lillian had something to do with it. She clicks into the article to read:

    Kara Danvers was spotted at Lillian Luthor’s, star-studded gala, in Metropolis, this week. Known for her charitable work, the Basketball superstar made a return trip to support the annual Luthor fundraiser. Her classmate and one-time tutor, Lena Luthor, was also in attendance and the pair happily posed for photos throughout the night. An eagle-eyed fan might notice Selina Kyle in the background, but this night really only had two standout stars and we’re all here for it.

    It’s the kind of speculation her and Kara were preparing for, the kind which says nothing, yet hints at everything. Lena clicks out of the article and reads Sam’s accompanying message:

    Sam: I’m very here for it! You two got a couple name yet??? 😉❤️

    She’s still laughing when Sam answers the phone, “no,” she says before Sam speaks.

    “Don’t worry, they’ll think up one for you. How was the big do?”

    “Better than usual.”

    “Oh yeah? coz you had Kara with you?”

    “Exactly,” Lena says smugly. She’s happy and can’t disguise it.

    “Jack wants to know if you’re joining us in Miami?”

    “I can’t,” Lena says, she wishes she could, but a quick trip to watch Kara play isn’t possible when she’s in the middle of taking over another company.

    “Boo,” Sam sulks. “Although I don’t think the world is ready for your WAG phase.”

    “My WAG phase?”

    “Don’t pretend you don’t know what I’m talking about. We all know it’s coming.”

    “Maybe,” she says coyly, unable to hide her intentions from her best friend.

    “And that’ll be the day you break the internet,” Sam laughs and their conversation moves onto plans for the coming weeks and months. Promises to meet up, promises Lena plans to keep, because she refuses to let work overtake her whole life like it did with her father and Lex.

    —

    The Unrivalled tournament is unlike anything Lena’s watched before. She tunes in to Kara’s first game from her office and quickly realises she’s not going to get any more work done, while it’s in play, because this 3x3 version of the game is fast. Shorter quarters, less players, a frantic show of how athletic and talented these players are and quite frankly Lena’s left breathless just watching.

    Kara’s a sweaty mess in the first quarter, puffing for air as the game continues at a rapid pace. By the time they reach the end of the third, Lena’s got some idea what is happening. Kara’s team are in the lead. They have to score 11 more points and they win. Sounds easy, but for some reason the final quarter has them missing the first five field-goals and the scores are inexplicably tied. With no commercial breaks, the camera’s close in on Kara’s bench during a timeout and Lena gets to listen in, not that it makes much sense to her, but she sees Kara nodding and then doing all she can to rally her teammates into getting the win.

    Whatever she says works, finally they find the net and keep scoring. A win. A start and right at the side of the court is Jack, raising his hand to high-five Kara. 

    Lena wishes she could find a window to get to one of the games too and figures there has to be some space in her calendar, but before she can check, the head of R&D is on the line and she gets tangled up, working on a new project proposal for the rest of the afternoon.

    —

    The tournament continues and Kara’s team keep fighting for every result. They don’t always come out on top, but they’re on a decent run and are tipped to make the playoffs. Lena’s made an effort to tune in and has been trying to organise her schedule to enable her to make at least one game, but in the end, the chance to witness Kara play comes thanks to a surprise plan in the Unrivalled schedule.

    “We’re coming to Metropolis,” Kara says the moment Lena answers the call. The excitement in her voice, evident.

    “To play?”

    “Yes, to play. It won’t be announced for a few days, but this is huge. No-one can know, so don’t tell Jack!”

    “My lips are sealed,” Lena chuckles, “Jack can find out, along with the rest of the world.” It feels too good to be true, shaking her head, as her smile grows, “you’re really coming home for a few days?”

    “I really am,” Kara says. “You know, it’s crazy how much I’ve missed you.”

    “Same,” she sighs, it’s how things are with them, but it doesn’t mean it’s easy to say goodbye all the time. Kara playing in her city, changes things. Lena may work long days and may not have been able to find a window to fly in and out of Miami, but she can make Metropolis work and she will get to watch Kara play live, “I can’t wait to see you play.”

    “You think you’ll make the game?”

    “I’ll definitely make the game,” she says and she will move the world to make sure she does. Maybe her WAG era is about to begin?

    Chapter End Notes

    Chapter 35: Conclusions

    Chapter Summary

    Unrivalled. court side appearances. Time moves fast.

    Chapter Notes

    been too focussed on trying to wrap up the whole story, which is taking a bit longer than I'd thought, and well, anyway...here's some more.

    Kara flies into Metropolis with her new teammates and a packed itinerary for the coming days. They’ve spoken at every opportunity and Kara’s confident she’ll be home that night, but Lena knows the schedule will limit the time they have, so the second she gets Kara’s message to say she’s going to be stuck at the arena for press commitments, she gets up from her desk and tells Jay, she’ll be taking the rest of the day off. 

    Despite all her best intentions, it’s a first. 

    Like her father and brother, she’s found herself consumed in her efforts to try and reshape LuthorCorp. A short term price, which hasn’t been too much of an issue, because Kara’s also working, hard, to build her own legacy. Their paths may be different, but they are equally driven and it makes it easier to accept there may be times when they can’t always be in the same room. None of that matters today, not when Lena’s spent three weeks looking forward to this very moment and isn’t prepared to go home later to an empty apartment, whilst Kara is stuck at the arena.

    It’s somewhat liberating to walk out of the office doors, before her working day usually ends and it’s also refreshing to know she has a team who can manage without her. 

    She promises herself, she’ll do this more often, but first, she wants to get to Kara.

    Traffic is mercifully light as she crosses the city towards the Met Arena, which has been temporarily revamped with Unrivalled banners and electronic billboards to showcase the big event. It’s truly amazing to see the growth in women’s sports and the new colorway suits the tired arena. There are images of players attached to the flagpoles on the walkway from the parking lot and she smiles up at the enlarged image of Kara in her red and blue jersey.

    Aside from watching Kara play, Lena’s also been keeping an eye on everything to do with the tournament and from a business perspective, she’s impressed. The organisers have a knack of getting the fans exactly what they want and this surprise addition to the calendar, appears to have delivered. Tickets sold out in hours and a pop-up, onsite store is packed with Merch in preparation for the incoming fans. She pauses on her way past and is considering picking up her own replica jersey, with Kara’s name on the back, when she hears her name being called from across the gangway. 

    She turns to find Kara jogging towards her and in a few short strides, she’s being lifted off the ground and spun around in a circle.

    “I didn’t think I’d see you until tonight?” Kara kisses her, before putting her back on her feet.

    “I couldn’t wait and you know? I do own the company. Figured it’s about time I used it to my advantage.”

    “Boss lady Luthor,” Kara’s grinning so much that Lena thinks it might hurt.

    “And, you love me calling the shots,” she says in a sultry tone which has Kara’s grip on her waist tightening.

    “I love it, yes?” Kara says, breathing in slowly through her nose. “You want to maybe sneak into the locker room, for old times sake?”

    “No,” Lena laughs, although she perhaps kind of does and Kara’s smiling back at her, like she knows it. “Don’t you have people you have to be talking to?”

    “They said they’d call when they’re set up,” she pulls her phone out of her pocket. “Said they’d be at least forty minutes, I was going to grab some food, when I saw you.”

    “What? No,” Kara chuckles. “I use it when I need a few minutes to myself before a game. Locker rooms get intense sometimes.”

    “Reaching peak fitness, Lena,” Kara tugs the shirt over her head, flexes and then leans in for a kiss.

    “And then we can go home,” Kara says the word, home, in the softest way as she takes both Lena’s hands, swinging them in the space between them.

    “Go, the sooner you’re done, the sooner you’re all mine,” she gives Kara’s hand a squeeze, before letting it go and finds her own way towards where some of Kara’s teammates are already practising.

Lena likes her and the goals the league have set all seem achievable. The temptation to invest has her swapping details, before Kara reappears and then her focus shifts to the court.

Wanting to look the part, she called in a little help from her trusted stylist and kind of thinks she’s knocked this look out of the park. Lena waves, Kara takes a second to close her mouth, before she returns the gesture and then she’s caught off guard when a ball is tossed right at her, she fumbles, but somehow gets control and the warmup continues.

    “And this is you, if you need anything…” the assistant says, once the camera is turned towards another face in the crowd.

    “You got me,” he says, holding out a hand, which Lena shakes. “Pleasure to meet you at last, Miss Luthor.”

She did this whole rehearsed speech about how you were a light in the dark and I didn’t have the heart to cut her off.”

    “She did?” Lena’s wondering when all this happened.

    “Oh, yes. We knew then how bad she had it,” the woman beside Clark says. “Lois,” she adds, introducing herself.

    Kara moves closer, taking all of Lena’s attention. She tosses the ball back and forth as part of her warm up, but her focus is all on Lena, “you could’ve warned me,” she says, quietly, only for Lena to hear.
