Thankfully her sharp reactions save her from taking a whack to the face. “You look, wow, yeah,” Kara’s got both palms wrapped around the ball, too lost in Lena to remember she’s supposed to toss it back. “I got real lucky.”

    “Yeah, you did!” Lucy says, laughing when Kara suddenly notices her former teammate. 

    “Oh, hey Luce, didn’t see you there.”

    “I know,” Lucy rolls her eyes and then Clark steps forward. The reunion is all too brief, because Kara’s a job to do and coaching staff calling for her to join a team huddle.

    “Time to lock in,” she winks at Lena before jogging away and linking up with the others. 

    Lena takes her seat, chats some more to Lucy, Clark, and Lois whilst they all await for the player introductions and their return to the court. Until finally, the lights dim, the arena quietens and they get set for tip-off.

    —

    The moment the television coverage begins, Lena’s phone starts to blow up.

    Her photo is everywhere it seems.

    Jess is the first to post in the group chat.

    “Courtside baddie!!!!”

    Followed by Jack, “Ahhhhh and when did this happen??? AND wuheeeyyyee was I not told you’d be gracing the court with your presence?????”

    Three dots appear, before Sam’s message comes in, “She’s in love 🥰 and it looks gooooood on her.”

    Lena’s smiling as she types out a response, only for Lucy to peer over her shoulder and laugh, “they’re right, you look hot. I miss those guys and I miss Al’s.”

    “Me too,” Lena says, sending her message, before locking her phone “We had some fun,” her eyes land on Kara and how one look still makes her heart race and her stomach flutter, is beyond her. 

    It’s easy to get lost in the game, when it moves at such a rapid pace. Kara puts on a star performance, bossing the court and hitting a season high. They’re fighting for a place in the playoffs and Lena knows how much Kara wants it, especially after such a lacklustre first season in the W and the way she’s been playing, makes it feel possible.

    The game is close, right up until the final quarter. A true battle, which leaves Lena feeling breathless just watching. The players are putting everything into the game, but despite Kara’s efforts, it’s not enough to get the win. 

    They all knew it was going to be a tough ask to beat a team, stacked with experience. Even so, whilst the opposing team celebrates, it’s hard to witness, how dejected Kara looks. Lena wishes there was something she could do and in that moment, Kara’s eyes meet hers, she nods almost imperceptibly, but it’s enough. Lena knows she’s okay and they’ll all be back to fight next year. A camera zones in on Kara then and just as quickly, her teammates surround her. They stay in that huddle for a while, hands pat backs and they let out those instant feelings as a unit. The game is over and so is their tournament. They grew throughout, Kara’s been championing their progress in every interview she’s done and Lena can imagine, she’s saying something similar to the other players now. This is just the beginning and although the losses hit hard, they’ll be okay. Kara will dust it off and go again, she always does.

    —

    “You looked so good last night,” Kara says at breakfast the next morning, she pushes her iPad across the kitchen counter for Lena to take a look. On the screen is a picture of her, laughing at something Lucy’s said and beside them is Clark, smiling at Lena with a surprising fondness.

    Lena glances over the accompanying article, which is all about LuthorCorp investments strategy and nothing about basketball, “did Nia send you this?”

    Kara drains her coffee, “they kind of mentioned, I was in the room, so it came up on my fyp.”

    “Former classmate?” Lena raises one eyebrow as she finds the part where Kara is indeed mentioned. 

    “Uh huh,” Kara says, coming around the counter, her hands sliding around Lena’s waist. “I love it when a former classmate comes to watch me play.”

    “Oh you do?”

    “Mmm, the best,” Kara kisses her then, slow, until she finally pulls back and takes a satisfied breath. “I hate that I’m leaving my former classmate though.”

    “Me too,” Lena sighs and Kara’s grip tightens. 

    “I’m glad you made it last night, it meant a lot.”

    “I’ll always try, in fact,” she says, moving out of Kara’s hold to sweep up their breakfast things. “I intend to do more than try. Yesterday reminded me of something I promised myself. Work can not dictate every facet of my life. I want to be there, want to watch you in those moments, so I’m going to do my best to make it happen.”

    “Can’t believe you’re the same girl who jumped on me in the gym and told me soooo many times how you hated sports.”

    “I did not jump you.”

    “Memory serves, you stormed in and couldn’t resist kissing my face?”

    “Kara,” she says in a way that usually has Kara raising her hands in defeat, but there’s a defiance in her girlfriend’s eye and it triggers a memory of how tempestuous their beginnings were. Tempestuous, teasing, tempting. She steps closer, nudging Kara’s legs apart with her knee. “Be careful with what you say next.”

    “You can’t deny it, Lena, you always wanted me,” her chin drops and she inhales deeply through her nose. “Couldn’t fight—” Lena stops her with a searing kiss. Whatever Kara’s about to say is very most likely the truth. She was drawn to her from the moment they met and although it may have been a bumpy road in those early days, she’d not change a thing.

    “Less talking Danvers,” she says, lips brushing against the delicate spot on Kara’s neck. Her action is rewarded by hands gripping firmly at her waist. “Show me why I couldn’t get enough of kissing you?”

    —

    They’ve been curled up together on the couch for most of the afternoon, when Kara stands and stretches both hands above her head, “you okay?” she asks, concern in her voice, when she catches Lena watching her.

    Lena gets up, tugging at the bottom of Kara’s shirt, a soft smile on her face, “just going to miss you, is all.”

    She presses a kiss to Kara’s cheek on her way past, but is stopped from going any further by the lightest brush of Kara’s fingers against her wrist, they linger for a second, toying at the watch Lena wears. The one Kara gave her for a non-Valentines present, in a time when they weren’t dating. She could laugh at how she convinced herself it was nothing serious. She was always in love with Kara, “I love you,” she says as the thought rushes through her.

    Lena feels her heart warm, with the affection in Kara’s eyes as she says, “I love you, too.”

    —

    They spend the coming weeks, making the apartment a more homely space. It only really needed the addition of Kara to make that happen, but the photos and the new coffee table, alongside the mismatched set of mugs, which Kara brought with her, makes it feel like it’s theirs.

    Lex visits. He loves talking basketball with Kara and business with Lena. Never quite letting go of the Luthor legacy, but determined not to get lost in the power game of it all, ever again.

    Alex comes to stay for a weekend. Lena discovers they share a similar taste in good scotch, alongside a common bond of how much they both care for Kara. It’s a fun few days and then Kara suggests they invite over a few friends for a game night and Lena gets to witness just how competitive the Danvers sisters can be.

    Jess, Sam, and Jack, fly in to watch a PWHL game, which ends with the five of them drunkenly falling back into the apartment in the early hours of the morning, in a night reminiscent of their college days. Lena curses them all on the Monday morning, when she’s stuck in conference call, trying to out manoeuvre another of Lorde Enterprises underhand deals. She later books tickets for them to do it all again in a few months time.

    Lillian occasionally insists on brunch, it’s sometimes performative, but mostly, thanks to having Kara there, it’s not awful and it all becomes a routine. A normal, which she’d never imagined and yet is all she ever wanted.

    Although, Kara’s not competing, the off-season is still filled with work and training. She keeps similar hours to Lena, heading out early to the gym or for a morning run, then moves to the practice court, working on technique, always trying to improve her game. Lena often comes home to find her watching film of other teams or of players she wishes to emulate and the next day she returns to the court, practicing, hard, alongside a core team of trainers and coaches she’s assembled over her years at NCU, who help her work on all the little things ready for the upcoming season. Basketball is Kara’s constant and the drive to be the best she can be, is something Lena respects and admires, even if it also means Kara occasionally tries to convince her to join her on the court. Lena’s no idea why she agrees, Kara isn’t really going to advance her skills by shooting over her head, but it’s kind of fun and seeing the way Kara’s face lights up, whenever she gets a bucket, counters how ridiculously bad she is at everything else.

    Spring arrives in a heartbeat, signalling a new beginning and Kara’s eagerly keeping track of NCU’s progress going into March. Another year, a different lineup, but people are talking and there’s a genuine chance the Wildcats might be crowned back-to-back champs. Kara’s been invited to attend for several sponsored appearances and is trying to sell it as a chance for them to have a break away, together. Lena’s very aware that the vacation they’d promised themselves is long overdue and readily admits that last March was one of the best times of her life. Following Kara’s journey had been all consuming and traveling across the country with her best friends had been fun and it’s the only reason she’s not said no, yet.

    “The desert?” Lena, however, isn’t as excited about this trip as she was last year.

    “We can chill by the pool, catch a few games…” Kara says, a hopefulness in her eyes as she waits for Lena’s answer and then she catches her bottom lip between her teeth and Lena already knows she’s no power to resist.

    Chapter End Notes

    There will be one more chapter. A few things I would like to add into their story before we're done.

    Chapter 36: always

    Chapter Summary

    Everything happens for a reason.

    Chapter Notes

    Sorry this took longer than I'd hoped. I've been busy doing stupid things and crying over my bumped up car. only it got damaged, not me, but it's been distracting.

    Kara makes the trip to Phoenix, a few days before Lena can join her. She’s always working, even when she’s not playing, and has a host of sponsorship commitments to keep, whilst also planning to link up with her former teammates. She goes under the radar at first, but after she attends a practice session, all the basketball socials are full of chatter about whether Kara is either going to suit up and play or if she’s thinking of giving coaching a try.

    “You look like you enjoyed telling them what to do,” Lena says, not hiding her amusement, when she finally arrives at their rented accommodation.

    “You know it,” Kara laughs, then shakes her head at the very thought. “Glad I’m not playing, Coach was really pushing and I’d forgotten how brutal some of her tirades were,” she takes Lena’s bag and leads her through the house. “Team’s hyped though, working through some plays and they look good.”

    “Better without you?” Lena can’t help herself, she knows Kara’s fielded a hundred or more questions about how she feels about no longer taking part. 

    Kara gives her a look, which says it all really. “Different,” she says coyly, making them both laugh.

    “And coaching?” Lena asks, once the laughter fades, because she can see something in Kara’s eye whenever she talks about helping others to improve or learn the game.

    “Don’t think Cat’s ready to retire, but yeah, it’s maybe an option for the future?” she pulls Lena down onto the couch beside her. “Coach always says I’ve a good eye for the game, or whatever that means and I’m vocal, a leader, know the game, live the game. Still it’s a long way off, first I’m taking the Sonic’s to the top.”

    “This all sounds like I’m committing to a life of basketball,” Lena’s surprised at how the idea actually excites her, although any life with Kara is all she wants and any future she imagines, always has Kara in it.

    “A life?” Kara twists on the couch, so that their knees are touching. “You sticking with me then?”

    “If you want me too?”

    “Yep, definitely,” she edges forward until their lips meet. “Can’t tell you enough, how much I want to do all this and have you here with me.”

    “I’m not going anywhere,” Lena says through a shaky breath as the weight of what they’re saying, registers. 

    Kara takes hold of both her hands, holding her gaze for a long moment, “me either.”

    It’s a promise and it’s a huge leap, Lena lets out a nervous chuckle and Kara kisses her with a tenderness, which says more than any of the words that came before.

    —

    Tiptoeing around promises for the future, is all they have time for, before they’re rushing out of the door and with some erratic driving, make it to the arena just in time to watch NCU take on the Bullhorns in a rematch of last years final. 

    The fans are as hyped for this game, as they were 12 months ago, but to Lena, it feels a world away from last years tournament. There’s no sneaking around and no pressure on Kara to perform and it’s noticeable how relaxed she is, revelling in being on the other side of it all and enjoying every chance she gets to cheer on her former teammates. 

    Now that she’s not quite as invested in the outcome, Lena finds it a lot easier to watch, although as the game progresses and the tension builds, Kara appears to be living every, single, play. 

    Lena winces as the mistakes begin to add up and for the past five minutes, Kara’s been bouncing her leg nervously.

    “It’s when you’re not on the court that I realise how good you are,” she says, with an air of frustration, after another sloppy steal. 

    The comment stops Kara’s nervous fidget and has her turning to Lena with an amused look on her face, “that a compliment?”

    Lena rolls her eyes at her girlfriend, even though it probably is. “they just lack, um, you know?”

    “Me?” Kara isn’t shy in showing how much she’s enjoying hearing these words escape Lena’s mouth.

    “Yes,” she huffs, but can’t hide her smile. This team would look better with Kara on it, every team would in Lena’s opinion.

    Kara leans into her side, their shoulders pressed firmly together, it’s about as affectionate as she gets when they’re in public, both still conscious of the eyes on them and the reality their faces could pop up on the jumbotron at any moment. Lena’s about to say something, when Kara jumps to her feet, raising one hand in the air as a beautiful three-pointer swishes through the net. “That’s what I’m talking about!” She yells at the players on the court.

    From that moment on, the Wildcats come to life. Edging out a tight victory in overtime. Kara’s elated, Lena’s riding a high and after some post win celebrations, they catch a ride home, before staggering towards the bedroom.

    —

    She regrets every drink the following morning, because the desert is not a forgiving place. Thankfully, Kara saves the day, arriving back from her morning run, with a bag of groceries and an ice cold can of some sports drink she swears by.

    “Good?”

    “Better,” Lena says having gulped half of the contents in one go. “How could you run?”

    Kara’s halfway through taking off her vest when she turns around to look at Lena, “sweat it out, rehydrate, go again.”

    “Healthy,” Lena rummages in the bags, grateful to find pain relief was also on Kara’s shopping list.

    “And really sweaty,” Kara says, it’s then that Lena really looks at her and notices the mess she’s in. “Might stick to the treadmill next time, but wanted to get you some supplies.”

    “And this is why I love you,” Lena sinks back into the pillows, hoping her head will stop pounding soon.

    “I’mma take a shower and after, I’ll do that other thing which always cures a headache.”

    Lena opens one eye, just a fraction, catches the way Kara’s looking her over and knows she’ll be true to her promise, “don’t be too long.”

    Two orgasms later and her headache is all but forgotten. Kara’s bouncing around with her usual abundance of energy and with a day to themselves, they move outside towards the pool. Kara soaking up the sun, whilst Lena hides under the cabana, enjoying the view, until she’s distracted by the sight of Kara getting out of the pool, droplets of water rolling off her shoulders, thigh muscles rippling as she steps closer, making Lena’s throat feel as dry as it had when she woke up.

    “Want more?” Kara asks, with a knowing smirk on her face.

    Lena’s never been more grateful that they rented a private house for the weekend. 

    —

    Even without a hangover, the reality of a trip to one of the hottest places on Earth, leaves Lena feeling like she’s melting every time she steps outside. The heat is ridiculous and she’s very glad that this isn’t where Kara ended up being drafted, because there is no escaping the relentless sunshine, which has her constantly reapplying sunblock for the few metres she has to walk each day. Kara, however, is happy to pull on a baseball cap and appears to love the golden sun, which makes her glow and draws out the cutest freckles on her nose.

    “I needed this time,” Kara says, when Lena comments on how rested she looks. “We never had a chance to just be us.”

    It’s true, they spent too long dancing around their feelings, then hiding what was going on with them. She wouldn’t change a thing, but appreciates these quiet moments as a couple even more because of it. “Maybe next vacation, we’ll finally make it out of the country?”

    “I thought you were going to make me promise, we’d avoid all things basketball,” Kara snorts out a laugh.

    “Impossible,” Lena tilts her head to one side. “I’ve accepted my fate, this is you, this is us. We’ll always be looking for a practice court wherever we travel, but perhaps, somewhere with a more temperate climate?”

    “Scandinavia,” Kara says, sitting up straighter. “Lucy couldn’t stop talking about her trip. Good food, beautiful city walks and you can hop around more than one country.”

    “Sounds perfect,” Lena really can’t wait, until then though, she’ll settle for any time with Kara, even in this oppressive desert heat.

    —

    The final is a tough watch, with the Angels outplaying NCU from the tip-off. For once, Cat has no answer and as the second quarter comes to an end, they all look in need of a reset. 

    Holly comes over to grab a few words with Kara during the halftime break, “ready?” She says and Kara nods. They discuss a few points from the opening half and then she smiles warmly at Lena, “and you’re with a fellow NCU alum, I see. Miss Luthor,” Holly adds and the camera pans to Lena. “I’ve been hearing a lot about the good work you’ve been putting in behind the scenes to help promote women in sport, all I can say is, thank you. You’ve made a huge difference from what I’ve been told and there are quite a few college players who want you to know it.”

    Lena nods politely, well practiced in handling media, she easily replies, “thank you, this is definitely the beginning of LuthorCorps investment in the game.”

    “You should see some of the work she’s been doing to help with sports rehab, Lena’s a genius,” Kara says, looking every bit the proud girlfriend and it has Holly glancing from one to the other, with an absolute softness in her eyes.

    “Sounds like you’re her number one fan?”

    “Absolutely,” Kara chuckles.

    Holly’s grinning back at her, she’s clearly fond of Kara and thankfully isn’t the kind of reporter who tries to push for more personal insights, “thoughts on the game?”

    “You know who I’m here for Holly,” Kara winks and Lena almost thinks Holly might be blushing, “Go Wildcats!”

    “Go Wildcats, indeed,” Holly says, turning back to the camera and seamlessly chatting to the studio. She gives Kara a quick nod and returns to some other part of the arena, ready to interview her next target.

    Kara sits back down beside Lena, looking rather pleased with herself, “I think she likes you.”

    “Um, yes,” Lena gives her girlfriend a gentle shove. Across the court she catches Holly looking over at them, a smile on her face before she turns away. “I also think she knows we’re dating.”

    Kara’s jaw drops comically, “are we really that obvious?”

    Lena could laugh at how surprised she is.

    The players return to the court, with the Wildcats working hard to get back in the game, narrowing the margin until only three points separate the two teams and for a few fleeting minutes, there is hope. It’s cruelly ripped away with one loose pass, followed by a string of fouls, which kill all momentum in the game.

    Lena knows the moment it’s lost, from the look Kara gives her and they head into one of the longest five minutes she’s ever had to endure. It’s not a pretty end to the championship and it’s almost a relief when the clock finally hits zero. A sadness follows at the realisation that some of the players on the court have just played their last ever game for NCU and it’s obvious how much the loss hurts.

    They don’t hang about to witness the other teams celebrations, instead Kara’s steers them towards a quiet diner for something to eat, before they meet up with the players at their hotel and despite Lena thinking it’d be a sombre affair, they have a fun evening. Kara makes a point of taking time to chat with everyone, helping to pick up spirits and put the loss into perspective, because Kara will always be a Wildcat, win or lose and she’ll always be there for her teammates and that’s another thing Lena loves about her.

    —

    With only a few days before the WNBA season returns, they take a last minute trip to MidVale, for a chance to enjoy a slower pace of life in the small-town. It’s a world away from their day-to-day lives and everything they both need before commitments force them to spend the next few months in different hotel rooms across the country.

    Kara’s still well-known here, of course, but in her hometown, it’s different. The people are warm and welcoming, treating Kara like she’s part of the family and not a celebrity. Offering her good wishes and occasionally a free drink or two and Kara’s always got time to stop and chat. 

    Away from the bustle at the heart of the small town, they get to indulge in long walks along the beach and lazy afternoons out on the porch, talking until the sun sets. Contentment, mixes with an undeniable sense of happiness and Lena turns to Kara with a soft smile, knowing she’s exactly where she’s meant to be.

    On their last day, Eliza hands over a box of Kara’s things and Lena can’t miss the dusting of pink on her girlfriends cheeks.

    “What’s in here?”

    “My, uh, my school stuff,” Kara reluctantly opens the box, to reveal the contents. Yearbooks, a bunch of trophies, old jerseys, signed posters of Kara’s favourite players. In fact there’s a collection of photos of one player in particular, which Kara tries to take back before Lena can get a proper look.

    “You had a crush on her?”

    “No,” Kara says too quickly. “No, well yes, but no. I mean, she’s the best player I’ve ever seen. I wanted to be her and maybe I didn’t know it then, but,” she turns to look at Lena, catches how amused she is and flings her hands up in the air. “You’re mean.”

    “I’m a Luthor,” Lena says, unable to hide her laughter a second longer. She’s a mind to put one of these posters up in Kara’s future trophy room, when an old jersey catches her interest. It’s blue and red and she’s sure she’s seen it somewhere before, but it’s got the number 1 emblazoned on the front, instead of Kara’s usual five.

    “You know, that one’s special,” Kara says, smiling as Lena takes a closer look. “I’d just committed to NCU and they did a custom fit for my first Slam cover. Guess it’s when everything changed?”

    “I remember seeing your photo everywhere when I came to NCU.”

    “Everywhere?” Kara’s smirking and Lena’s an idea why. “It was like one or two banners, which you had to go into your LuthorDome to even see.”

    “Not my dome,” Lena rolls her eyes, trying to pass by the truth of how she had definitely spent a little too long staring up at one of those banners in the arena in her first week. “And, I was not a fan.”

    “Yeah, yeah, you keep saying that,” Kara steps closer, taking the jersey from Lena’s hands and holding it up against her chest “Think I looked good in blue?”

    “Definitely,” Lena loves how the colour brings out Kara’s eyes. “I’m very happy my brother didn’t get his way over changing the Wildcats colours.”

    “Me too,” Kara laughs, packing the jersey back in the box.

    “They’re changing the name back,” she says, realising she’d not had a chance to tell Kara yet.

    “What name?” Kara asks, not catching Lena’s thread.

    “You can’t call it my LuthorDome, it’ll just be the Den. The way it should be.”

    “Yeah?” The surprise in her voice is actually cute and as she registers what Lena’s telling her, her smile grows and her eyes soften.

    “Yes,” Lena says, definitively, just before Kara kisses her breathless.

    —

    Basketball is back to the forefront of daily life, with Kara’s return to the Sonic’s, and after a rapid two weeks training camp, the league kicks back into gear, meaning Kara’s on the road, more often than not.

    Lena tries to get to as many games as she can, even though it usually involves a last minute dash out of the office and a scramble to the airfield and some days, she just can’t make it, no matter how much she wishes she could. Kara’s understanding, of course. The days she does make it, are always worth it, mostly because she can’t miss the way Kara’s face lights up, when she spots her sitting court-side.

    With Lena’s frequent appearances at games and the occasional picture of them together at various Luthor events, the rumours about their personal relationship persist. Kara’s never troubled by it, deflecting any questions she might get asked, often talking about the work Lena’s putting into supporting the game and no-one pushes for anything more, perhaps assuming she’s expanding on the development and funding which Lex was known for? Either way, it always makes Lena blush to hear Kara talk about her, and her work, with such unbridled affection. Sam always calls her after, teasing her about how in love they both are. She doesn’t argue, it’s very true.

    —

    All-star weekend comes around all too soon and Lena makes certain she’s there for every minute of it. She’s not alone either, Jack’s been filling the group chat up for months, making sure they’re all prepared for the big reunion and before they touchdown, Sam’s complaining about the impending hangovers. 

    Lena avoids the heavy drinking, prioritising her time with Kara, who has spent the last few weeks playing some of the best basketball of her career. Her efforts, alongside the recent acquisitions, have helped the Sonic’s to more wins than they managed to notch up in the entirety of her rookie season. It’s left the team sitting pretty in the middle of the table, with a few rumblings of a playoff run which aren’t only fuelled by Jack’s wishful thinking and it’s what has Kara already focussed on the return to competitive play, although she also insists all-star’s is about the fun and no way is she going to miss out.

    The weekend is as wild as expected and then some. They drink, they party, Kara plays and wins and they party again.  By the end of it, Lena could do with another vacation. 

    Kara leaves early in the morning, back to training and prepping for the next league game, whilst Lena packs up the last of her things and then meets Sam for lunch, before her own flight to Metropolis.

    The food arrives, midway through Sam recalling an awkward moment from the Friday night, when an enthusiastic hanger-on had made a very forward pass at Kara.

    “I’d forgotten how cute a flustered Kara Danvers can be,” Sam says, grinding pepper liberally over her seafood linguine. “The woman had no chill, reminded me of Andrea.”

    “Let’s not,” Lena pours herself a glass of water, the memory of the incident and the thought of Andrea, making her doubt if her stomach can cope with the wine Sam’s just ordered.

    “And I know you two are all for your privacy, but I kind of wanted you to go over and kiss Kara’s face off, just to shut them all up.”

    “Can you imagine what my dear mother would have to say?” Lena jokes, not thinking much about what she’s saying until Sam makes this funny little noise in reply. “What?”

    It takes her friend a considered moment before she replies, “one day, you might want to stop worrying about what Lillian Luthor has to say.”

    Lena’s brow scrunches together, because Sam, of all people, knows she’s not in the business of pleasing Lillian and never has been, “I’m not worrying.”

    “Then what is it?” Sam continues, Lena shrugs, still none the wiser as to what her best friend is talking about. “You keep hiding and I know Kara says she’s cool about it,” Lena sucks in a breath, but Sam’s not done. “And you both have all these reasons and I get it, I do, but I don’t know, wouldn’t it make it easier?” Sam looks as confused as Lena feels right now.

    “Make what easier exactly?” Lena says and at the shake of Sam’s head, she adds, “Sam, what has Kara said to you?”

    “You know, the stuff about keeping it quiet, saving you from all the intrusive press and how you hate the limelight and she won’t push, it’s for you to say when you tell everyone, that kind of thing,” Sam says, like Lena knows exactly what she’s talking about and then her mouth stays open and her eyes are searching Lena’s. “Kara said it’s what you wanted, ever since the whole Draft night thing and how you couldn’t be that kind of couple. She said it was cool, you found a way around it all and things works, so uh? It’s just how it is? Her words, not mine.”

    Lena’s eyes widen and her throat feels suddenly dry. It’s been a year since the draft. Has Kara really assumed they’ve been working away around things ever since?

    “We live together,” Lena says, like it explains all she feels and Sam’s hand is on top of hers now, giving it a gentle squeeze. “I’m not hiding our relationship,” she adds and Sam gives a sympathetic tilt of her head. “I thought Kara wanted to keep things quiet because her fanbase goes rabid over this kind of thing?” It sounds like a question and she’s not sure why. 

    Sam spirals the linguine around her fork, before lifting it to her mouth, “well, yeah, but…” she shrugs. “I don’t think that’d stop her, do you?”

    “I…” Lena shakes her head. “No, I suppose not.”

    —

    The chat with Sam, leaves her with more questions than answers and she spends the next few days thinking about it, until finally, Kara’s home and she gets to ask her face-to-face.

    “How do you feel about the way we’ve been handling our relationship?” She asks. 

    The question sounds ridiculously formal and Kara’s face is a picture as she sits up a little straighter.

    “Am I being interrogated?”

    “No,” Lena scoffs.

    “Lillian’s not about to come in and shake me down?”

    “Kara, please,” Lena rolls her eyes at Kara’s ability to find such an occurrence amusing. She’s a mind to tell her, just how ruthless Lillian can be, but it’s not important right now.

    “I’m cool with it,” Kara says, once she stops laughing. “You’ve always been rule based, it’s one of the things I love about you.”

    “Rule based?”

    Kara gives her a knowing look, which has Lena accepting the truth, she did come into this with a list of requirements, although those were academic based ones, which she’s now finding out, have somehow ended up dictating their relationship.

    “Yeah,” Kara says like it’s obvious. “Haven’t we always been playing by them? Keeping out of the public eye, no hassle, no outside influence. I thought that’s what you wanted?”

    Lena opens her mouth to say something, but she can’t find the words. Kara’s been thinking this is what she’s wanted the whole time and if she’s honest with herself, she’s never tried or shown she wants it any other way.

    Kara must take her silence as confirmation, shifting from the couch, with her usual lazy smile on her face as she suggests they make the most of the short time left until she has to leave, again. “Let’s get some air and then I’m cooking you dinner,” she swipes for her set of keys, before pulling on her jacket.

    Maybe Lena should push the conversation further, but they really only ever have a short time together and she’s more than happy to take a walk in the park, stopping for ice cream and chatting about Kara’s upcoming game. It’s the kind of warm, sunny day, which Lena can tolerate, although Kara still laughs at her when she picks the only bench in the shade. Cherishing the time together has always been her number one priority and no matter what questions might be swirling around her mind, she won’t waste a second. 

    This visit is over too soon, with Kara not really having a chance to unpack her bags and Lena not having enough time to unpack everything she’s learned since she spoke to Sam.

    —

    A few days later, she’s stuck in her office, thinking over their last conversation and still finding no answers.

    She loves Kara and Kara loves her back and it’s possibly why it’s been easy to overlook the obvious. There have been times where she’s dismissed how non-comital Kara can be and others where she’s excused the reluctance to be seen in public, not least because of the baggage which comes from being associated with a Luthor. Lena’s shied away from it her whole life and she’s perhaps been too willing to assume Kara felt the same way.

    She’s flicking through the channels to find highlights from the previous days game, when a few truths slowly dawn on her and they come in the shape of Sam, Jess, Jack, Leslie, Nia and Brainy, all sitting at the edge of the court. Half of them are wearing replica jerseys with Kara’s number emblazoned on the front and there is no doubt, they’re all there to support Kara. Her friends and former teammates and all the fans, cheering on the Sonics, all eager for them to make the playoffs. And Lena? Well, she’s in Metropolis, standing in the middle of her office, one foot crossed in front of the other, the remote control in her hand, tapping it against her shoulder, thinking about how strange it feels to be the only one who wasn’t at such an important game.

    She turns up the volume to hear what the commentators are saying.

    “It’s all about having experience,” the woman says. “Danvers needed a Vet she could rely on last year and they made moves in the off-season which have played out well. Add in the new coach and we’ve seen the difference from week one.”

    “She won the Natty, she’s taken the rookie of the year, can she turn the fortunes around for the Sonics?”

    “They win today and again on Wednesday, they’re headed into the playoffs, where we all know, anything can happen.”

    Lena mutes the conversation, she knows the outcome of the game, even if she’d been stuck in a meeting and unable to watch it live. The Sonic’s got the win and are only hours away from playing again and it’s clear to Lena now, just how much is at stake. She takes a few paces until she’s looking out across the city skyline, when Jay appears in the doorway, “everything okay?”

    It’s not, Lena’s known this for a few days now, perhaps longer. She makes the split second decision to fix it and turns back to Jay with an urgent request, “can you reschedule my appointments? Push as many as you can until next week?”

    “Absolutely,“ her assistant taps a few things into her tablet, whilst Lena makes a quick call to arrange her own transportation. Jay waits for her to finish, before asking, “do you need me to do anything else?”

    “No, I’m all set. Once you’ve fixed my schedule, take the day off. Tomorrow too,” she says, already clearing her desk. She’s somewhere she needs to be and a car ready to rush her back to the apartment to pack a bag, whilst they get the jet ready for her at the airfield. Flying is one of her least favourite things to do, but it’s the only chance she has of getting to Kara’s game in time.

    “Wish Kara good luck from me,” Jay says once Lena finally heads towards the elevator.

    “I will,” she replies, smiling back over her shoulder, pushing down the usual nerves she feels whenever she has to take to the skies.

    —

    She’s late. Luck is not her friend. The flight was bumpy, the car on time, the traffic horrific and it’s raining, hard, but she’s here.

    Thankfully, she’s greeted by Meg the second she steps inside the building and with her help, she’s quickly ushered past security and along quiet corridors, until the hum of the arena gets ever louder, the closer they get to the court. Meg gives her one last smile, “I didn’t tell her, you were coming.”

    “Thank you,” Lena’s takes one last breath and steps forward. 

    With the game well underway, it’s easy to slip past the people already in their seats, with little fuss. Her friends are all seated together on the far side of the court, for the second game in a row, too busy looking at something on Leslie’s phone to even notice that Lena’s just walked in. 

    Kara’s dribbling the ball down the court, her left hand pointing to where she wants another player to go and then in the moment she’s about to shift onto her right foot, she notices Lena. She does a double-take and although the pause is brief enough that most people would miss it, Lena sees it and so does Kara’s opponent, taking advantage of the lapse as she darts out her hand to try and steal the ball. The movement snaps Kara back into play. She shifts the ball, bouncing it forward to Gayle, who uses the space and runs into the paint for an easy lay-up.

    The opposing coach calls a timeout and everyone moves towards the bench, except Kara, who is stuck on the same spot, looking right at Lena, her mouth agape. She’s jolted back into action when Gayle jogs past, tapping Kara on her back and guiding her towards the team huddle. 

    There’s still a game to play and Lena makes her way over to the others, who all look almost as surprised as Kara, all except for Sam, who has a smug look on her face, when she catches sight of Lena’s jersey.

    “Special edition?” She says, once Lena’s in her seat.

    “One of a kind,” Lena winks at her. She knows what she’s doing and hopes Kara gets the message.

    “Everyone will be talking,” Sam teases, increasingly unable to hide her huge smile.

    Lena looks over to Kara and can see from the look on her face, that she’s done exactly what she needed to do, “let them.”

    It’s a statement. Lena knows what it means to show up wearing this jersey. She’s telling Kara she wants to be seen, wants them to be seen.

    The game quickly gets back underway and it’s anything but easy to watch. Too many soft fouls being called and questionable decisions which have Jack on his feet seemingly every other second, but ultimately the Sonic’s come out the victors. After a quick team huddle, Kara’s pulled aside for an interview, one eye on Lena the entire time and then she’s on her way over, a purpose in her stride. Still in her uniform, a white towel slung around her neck and beads of sweat rolling down her shoulders. She looks about as beautiful as Lena’s ever seen her.

    “You’re really here,” Kara’s grin is so wide it must surely hurt.

    “I am,” Lena says, possibly smiling just as hard in return.

    Kara inhales through her nose, hands gripping at the ends of the towel, “you’re wearing my jersey.”

    “I am,” Lena says, again, knowing what it means. She looks down at the blue and red jersey, then back up at Kara.

    “People are going to talk,” Kara steps closer.

    Lena nods, sure of what she’s doing, “I never wanted to hide us. I want the world to know I’m yours and if this is what I need to do…”

    “Yeah?” The hopefulness in Kara’s question, has Lena stepping closer, her hands covering Kara’s, tugging at the towel until Kara’s face is a bare millimetre from her own.

    “Yes,” she says, watching as Kara licks out at her bottom lip. 

    “I’m not going to kiss you,” Kara says, sounding mildly pained by her own words.

    “Oh?” Lena’s wondering if she’s read it all wrong and moves to pull her hands way, but Kara’s quick to stop her.

    “Nope,” she says, with a playful shake of her head. “You’re Lena Luthor, figure I got to at least win a title first?” She’s backing up with a smile on her face and there’s a promise in her words, which Lena’s sure she’ll be keeping.

    “Better get on with it then,” she calls out, before she feels Jack’s hand on her shoulder.

    “And now we get drinks?” He says and Lena laughs.

    Kara joins them in the hotel bar, once she’s finished all her media duties, showered and changed. She wraps her arms around Lena’s waist from behind and places a kiss to the delicate spot on her neck, which has Lena feeling weak in an instant, “you still wearing it?” Her voice is low and her breath warm.

    “Really?” She chuckles, surprised Kara would be so affected.

    “In my jersey, not in my jersey, just seeing you sitting by the court…”

    “Okay, that’s enough,” Sam cuts in between them. “Drink Danvers?”

    “Soda,” she says, with a little lift of her eyebrows.

    Jack’s disappointed pout is too much and Lena bumps into his hip, “she’s gunning for a ring, you know how it is.”

    This lifts Jack’s spirits in an instant. “Unprecedented,” he says, raising his glass in a toast.

    “You know it,” Kara beams back at him. “We’re the disrupters,” she clinks her glass, against his.

    “I like it. To the disrupters,” Jack makes another toast, then pulls out his phone, getting the whole group to slide in for a selfie.

    —

    The Sonic’s fight all the way to the Semi’s, but come up short. Kara’s exhausted, yet happy, “couldn’t ask for more,” she tells Holly in the post game interview. “Everyone on this team has worked hard, we came into this season with a lot of questions and I think we’ve answered them. We’ve a foundation to build from and yeah, we’re gonna keep on disrupting and no-one is going to want to come here and play us next season.”

    “Been quite a second year for you, Kara. Well done and I know I’m not the only one looking forward to what year three brings. Enjoy some rest, any plans?”

    “Yeah, Lena’s taking a much needed vacation and we’ll be spending some of it, doing up the new place,” it’s the first time she’s openly talked about their relationship in an interview and she does it with such ease, that Lena doubts anyone would blink an eye.

    “Domestic bliss?” Holly teases and Kara’s face lights up.

    “Absolutely,” she says, winking at the reporter, before the interview cuts back to the studio.

    —

    The title arrives the following year, not in the W, but at Unrivalled. Kara’s midway through celebrating with her teammates, when she suddenly stops and turns to face Lena, a questioning look in her eyes, which has Lena nodding almost imperceptibly. Kara jogs over, grinning from ear to ear and then her smile fades into something softer, something permanent, she cups Lena’s face with her hands, “I did it, Lena. We got that title.”

    “You really did,” Lena’s not sure how she manages to speak, still struggling from the myriad of emotions after witnessing Kara achieve something so special. There are tears of happiness, threatening to spill from her eyes. She leans into the warm touch of Kara’s palm, smiles when Kara’s lips meet hers in a firm kiss. It might not be the kind of kiss that makes the earth move, but it almost breaks the internet, or so Sam tells her when she bombards them with the many photos which are now circulating. Kara likes one in particular and has it blown up, placing it behind the replica trophy in her room packed with mementoes.

    —

    Time passes, Lena’s focus never wavers. She rebuilds LuthorCorp into a company she’s proud to lead, moving their headquarters to National City and being closer to where Kara plays, is definitely a bonus, enabling them to spend more time together and making it a lot easier for Lena to get to even more games. 

    Kara loves basketball and seems to get better with every season, working hard to improve her game and add to what she offers the team. Even after all this time, Lena’s in awe of just how dedicated she is and it’s why it hits even sweeter, when she eventually wins a championship. 

    They marry the following Spring. On a beautiful day in March, a month which has always been Kara’s favourite of the year and not just because of how it’s always synonymous with everything basketball. Lena agrees, March is full of new beginnings, with white and pink blossom on the trees as nature bursts back to life, after a harsh winter. It brings hope and makes her love grow even stronger.

    There’s still a chill in the air when she walks down the aisle, wearing a white dress with a long trail and her hair flowing around her shoulders. Kara catches sight of her and can’t fight back her tears, quickly wiping at the tracks, before she takes both Lena’s hands in hers, “you look beautiful,” she whispers.

    Lena’s bottom lip wobbles and its a job to hold it together throughout the service.

    Her vows are a promise and she finishes them with the words, “I love you.” 

    “Every day with you, always,” Kara says, just before she kisses her. And, surrounded by their friends and family, they embark on the next chapter in their story.

    —

    A few more years pass, before they make the short trip to NCU, on a cool November night, for Cat’s retirement dinner. They’re joined by players past and present, in celebrating the renowned coach and her many achievements. It’s a time for reminiscing and being back on campus brings back plenty of happy memories. From Kara tearing around campus on stolen golf carts, to the night they got locked in one of the athlete rooms. A time was had and Lena wouldn’t change a minute of it.

    Whilst Kara’s saying her last round of goodbyes, Lena sneaks into the arena and peeks out of the player tunnel. If she closes her eyes, she can picture a younger Kara running out onto the court, almost hear the screams of her adoring fans and is pretty certain that the place still smells the same.

    Even though it’s been several years since the renaming of the LuthorDome, there are still signs of her families influence on the program, even now, but more importantly is the influence Kara’s had on everyone who came to watch and what her legacy will mean to all that come after her. 

    As Lena looks up into the rafters, she feels a well of emotion when she sees Kara’s number hanging there, alongside other former legends of NCU.

    She places one hand across her belly, rubbing it gently, “that’s your mom,” she says barely above a whisper. “She’s never going to stop doing amazing things.”

    A soft hum from behind her, alerts her to Kara’s presence. 

    “And your mommy, is never going to stop making this world a better place for you to live in,” Kara says, crouching down, so that she can kiss the well-formed bump, her eyes catch Lena’s and she smiles. “Together, we could change the world,” she adds, rising to her feet, and Lena has a feeling they probably could. In some ways, perhaps unknown to them, they already have.

    Chapter End Notes

    I want to say a massive thank you to all of you for taking the time to read and follow me through this journey. I did not know how huge this story would become and had no idea I'd commit over a year of my life to it. it feels crazy now and so much of it was born in the middle of the night when I was getting up to watch basketball. hooked from the first game.

    I don't know what's next from me. I will try to finish up the two WIPs I have as I hate to leave things unfinished.
    Until then, goodbye from this one and thank you again.

    And remember, this version of the Luthor-Danvers, are absolutely thriving. Love is always worth the risk.
